# Review task: a REFRESHED ALMA agent skill against its rebuilt evidence pack

The skill below was first written from a ~1,600-paper corpus, reviewed, and fixed (the fix record is the
RECONCILIATION section). The corpus then grew to ~3,500 papers, the evidence pack was rebuilt, and a writer began
updating the skill to the new pack. The writer may have been cut off before finishing, so expect a mix: some
counts and ranges updated to the new pack, others still at the old values, and possibly a half-edited section.

Apply every check from the standard review (attribution, unsupported practice, scientific correctness, omissions,
usability: see below), and also these refresh checks:

A. **Stale or mixed numbers.** Paper counts, "used in N papers", common choices and ranges that do not match the
   NEW pack aggregates (the old corpus was about half the size, so old counts are usually roughly half). The
   description line's "Built from N ALMA papers" must match the pack.
B. **Lost fixes.** Any item the RECONCILIATION marks FIXED that the current skill has undone or reintroduced.
C. **Broken edits.** Duplicated sections, contradictory statements left from an unfinished rewrite, tables whose
   rows disagree with the text, dangling references.
D. **Length.** Over ~300 lines is a low-severity usability finding; say what could move to references/parameters.md.

Standard checks, in this order:
1. Attribution: every number, range, default, software attribution and "papers do X" claim that cites bibcodes is
   supported by the pack for those bibcodes; counts match the pack aggregates.
2. Unsupported practice presented as literature practice.
3. Scientific correctness: formulas, units, CASA task/parameter names and semantics, unsafe advice.
4. Omissions: important pitfalls, assumptions or validation checks clearly present in the pack but missing.
5. Usability: anything an agent would misread or could not act on.

You have no file access: everything you need is in this message.

Output format (Markdown, nothing else):

```
# Codex refresh review: <slug>
VERDICT: CLEAN | FIX
REFRESH: COMPLETE | PARTIAL   (PARTIAL if any section still carries old-pack numbers or an unfinished edit)
## Findings
- RR-01 | high|medium|low | <stale|lost-fix|broken-edit|length|attribution|unsupported|science|omission|usability> | <the claim, quoted briefly> | <problem, citing the pack line or bibcode> | <concrete fix>
- RR-02 | ...
## Notes
<at most 5 lines>
```

Use `VERDICT: CLEAN` only when there are no high or medium findings. Be specific and terse; do not restate the
skill. Do not invent pack content: when you say the pack contradicts a claim, cite the pack line.

=== SKILL.md (continuum-subtraction) ===
---
name: alma-continuum-subtraction
description: "Separate spectral-line emission from continuum in ALMA data: pick line-free channels, fit and subtract the continuum in the uv plane (uvcontsub) or image plane (imcontsub, STATCONT), and build the matching continuum image from the same channels. Use when making line cubes, moment maps, line fluxes or continuum images from ALMA spectral data, when a field is line-rich (hot cores, starbursts, spectral scans), when lines sit in absorption against bright continuum, or when a referee asks how the continuum was removed. Built from 1551 ALMA papers (2012-2026)."
---

# Continuum subtraction and line-free channel selection

Continuum subtraction fits a low-order model of the continuum across channels that carry no line emission and
removes it, leaving a line-only cube; the same line-free channels give the continuum image. Two mistakes matter
most: (1) a wrong line-free channel set (line wings, blends or absorption inside the "continuum" channels) biases
both the line flux and the continuum flux, and (2) the channel set and fit settings are often not reported, so the
result cannot be reproduced. In the pack's "not stated" list, 15 labels about line-free channels occur 323 times
(sum of the labels shown; a card can carry more than one variant, so this is not a unique-card count). Across the
whole corpus, the continuum-subtraction setting is unstated in 64% of 3498 cards (`stats/README.md`; that
denominator includes papers with no line imaging).

**How the counts were made.** "Pack aggregate" means a number printed in the pack's aggregate sections. "My count"
means a regex census over the `processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction` and `processing.imaging.line_free_channels`
fields of all 1551 cards in the pack's bibcode list (1417 cards fill at least one field), read with the pack
builder's own tagging. The pack prints only the first 700 parameter lines (220 papers with these fields), so the
census cannot be checked line by line against the pack text; the same regexes over the printed lines give uv 132,
image 24, uvcontsub 86, first order 41, zeroth order 20. A paper is counted once per category; papers that used two
methods are counted in both. Patterns: uv plane = `uvcontsub`, "uv plane/domain/space", "visibilit", GILDAS uv
tasks, UVLSF; image plane = `imcontsub`, "image plane/domain/space", STATCONT, "per pixel"; first order =
`fitorder=1`, "first-order/degree", "linear fit/model/baseline/polynomial/function"; zeroth order = `fitorder=0`,
"zeroth/zero-order", "constant fit/level/baseline", "flat continuum". Regex counts are approximate (a field that
says "not performed in uv-plane" still matches uv).

## When to use

- Any ALMA line cube, moment map or line flux where the source has detectable continuum (dust, free-free,
  synchrotron, a quasar core, a planet or comet body).
- Building a continuum image from the same spectral windows that carry lines (exclude line channels, then MFS or
  mtmfs image the rest) (2024ApJ...972..111L, 2018MNRAS.475..448T, 2020MNRAS.496..598C, 2020MNRAS.499.2493A).
- Line-rich sources where few channels are line-free: hot cores, complex organics, starburst nuclei, spectral
  scans. Two statistical routes are used here and they are different operations:
  - STATCONT selects the continuum channels, then the subtraction is done in the uv plane: 2018ApJ...866...87B
    (c-SCM plus an aggregate deviation spectrum, threshold adjusted by hand), 2021A&A...647A..87L (corrected
    sigma clipping on preliminary cubes, then uvcontsub), followed by 2019ApJ...882..118X.
  - STATCONT estimates the continuum per pixel and subtracts it in the image plane: 2021A&A...656A..46M,
    2017A&A...604A...6S, 2023A&A...677A..15M, 2024A&A...687A.163B; 2020A&A...636A..67A chose STATCONT "as there
    were very few line free channels"; 2021A&A...653A.129C used it on a Band 3 spectrum too line-rich for the
    classical line-free selection.
- Line searches at high redshift where the line falls in one spw and the other spws give the continuum
  (2013ApJ...770...13W, 2019ApJ...873...50D, 2020A&A...636A..37D).

## When not to use

- **Absorption against a bright continuum.** Keep the continuum for the absorption analysis. 2018A&A...615A.141B
  fitted absorption on non-continuum-subtracted data with XCLASS and a continuum background layer
  (T_B = 132 K); 2021MNRAS.504.2687N processed its line data without continuum subtraction and measured the
  continuum visibilities from 9 channels at each end of the band; 2025ApJ...989...83B computed
  tau = -ln(T_nu / T_cont) and fitted each absorption feature with a Gaussian plus a linear continuum.
  2015ApJ...814...22E subtracted in the image plane because uv-plane subtraction "can cause artifacts in the
  presence of absorption features". Note the beam-filling assumption: 2019MNRAS.485..229R states that a filling
  factor of 1 gives only a lower limit on tau. Subtraction does not recover line emission hidden by optically
  thick line plus continuum: 2016ApJ...820L..25Y subtracted continuum from HCO+ but not from CO.
- **Temperatures from optically thick lines.** 2020ApJ...905...89Y took the disk atmosphere temperature from the
  13CO peak brightness temperature without continuum subtraction (subtracting first would lower the peak where
  the line is optically thick over bright dust; general reasoning).
- **Too few line-free channels to define the continuum.** 2014ApJ...790..160S did not subtract continuum from its
  CO(3-2) cube for that reason. 2020ApJ...905...85S fitted the continuum flux density as a free parameter together
  with the absorption Gaussians, using the other sideband or an SED when line-free bandwidth was short. A
  Gaussian-plus-constant fit is also used, but in the pack it follows a subtraction rather than replacing it:
  2026ApJ..1002L..28Y ran a linear uvcontsub first, then fitted Gaussian plus constant (a residual-baseline term);
  2013ApJ...770...13W subtracted a continuum image and used the fitted constant only as a consistency check.
- **A bright compact source dominates the field.** Remove a model of it first: 2019AJ....157..251T subtracted a
  limb-darkened Neptune model from the data before deconvolution. The pack does not show a fixed follow-up sequence.
- **Continuum fluxes contaminated by a strong line in broadband data.** Correct the continuum flux for the line
  contribution instead: 2015Natur.522..455C subtracted the [C II] contribution from its measured continuum fluxes.

## Steps

Keep two products apart throughout: the calibrated, unflagged visibilities (input for line work) and a
continuum-only copy (input for continuum imaging). Do not flag line channels in the data you will later image as
a line cube, and do not image continuum from continuum-subtracted data (general practice). 2025ApJ...989...30Z is
a clean example: a pseudo-continuum copy flagged +-15 km/s around six lines, self-calibration on it, the tables
applied to the full data, then uvcontsub excluding the same channels.

1. **Calibrate; self-calibrate only if it helps.** Many papers self-calibrated on line-free continuum and applied
   the tables to the line data before subtracting (2018ApJ...852..122H, 2018A&A...615A..28D, 2021A&A...648A..19W,
   2025ApJS..280...33Y, 2026A&A...709A..93D, 2025ApJ...989...30Z). Others transferred the self-cal solutions after
   the subtraction (2020ApJ...898...10T, 2017ApJ...850L..30Y). Self-calibration is conditional: 2024ApJ...961..228S
   got no improvement at S/N < 100, and 2021ApJ...910...53V found its continuum source too faint to improve S/N.
   Adopt it only if the continuum S/N supports it and the image improves. Software: CASA gaincal/applycal.
2. **Check flux scales and frames before combining.** 2021A&A...648A..19W compared the flux calibration of each
   execution; 2021A&A...656A..46M reports line-flux scaling anomalies in some tunings; 2018ApJ...852..122H found
   self-calibrated continuum fluxes within 5% within each band. Doppler-correct moving or multi-epoch data: for a
   comet, phaseshift, uvcontsub, then cvel2 to the comet rest frame (2025NatAs...9.1476C); fixvis/fixplanets for
   proper motion (2018ApJ...852..122H).
3. **Find the line-free channels.** Goal: a channel set with no emission, absorption, or atmospheric features.
   Methods in the pack:
   - inspect amplitude vs channel or visibility plots and excise line peaks (2018ApJ...856..143I,
     2018MNRAS.475..448T, 2020MNRAS.497.3504T), or pick by eye from cubes or spectra (2014Sci...345.1584B,
     2024AJ....167...66Y, 2020MNRAS.496..598C, 2024ApJ...977...37N);
   - exclude a velocity window around each known line, or a multiple of the line FWHM (table below);
   - start from an automatic selection and then iterate: the pipeline findcont ranges as a first guess, then
     exclude channels with line emission above 3 sigma until none remain (2026A&A...709A..93D); a CDMS model
     spectrum shifted to the field V_lsr and widened to 50 km/s, then manual re-identification after the first
     version showed skewed baselines (2025ApJS..280...33Y); the findContinuum algorithm works on dirty image cubes
     and assumes the baseline is the 19% most line-free channels (2023PASP..135g4501H);
   - iterate by hand: 2019Natur.570...83M subtracted, re-imaged, marked weak lines, and required a residual slope
     < 30 mJy across each window, with channels chosen within 1 arcsec of Sgr A*; 2019A&A...627A.114K iterated the
     pure-continuum ranges;
   - statistical selection for line-rich fields (2018ApJ...866...87B, 2021A&A...647A..87L); 2015A&A...576A.129B
     split each spw into 1000-channel sub-windows and kept the best pair of line-free channels in each;
   - drop atmospheric lines and bad edges: 2019A&A...630A..59B excluded channels with atmospheric transmission
     < 0.5; 2017A&A...603A..92V kept far from the 183 GHz water line; 2025A&A...699A.187G excluded five channels at
     each end of each window; 2016A&A...586A..69P excluded end channels.
4. **Make the continuum image from the unsubtracted calibrated data.** Split or select only the line-free channels
   (or flag line channels in a separate copy), average, and image with MFS or mtmfs. The effective bandwidth is
   smaller than the full band: about half (2015ApJ...801...82H), about 7.5 GHz (2018PASJ...70...36I), 4 GHz from two
   line-free spws (2023A&A...673A..46B), 52 MHz (2021A&A...655A..72S), about 5% of the bandwidth
   (2020ApJ...905...25G). 2021A&A...648A..19W and 2024A&A...689A..85G averaged the remaining channels to 125 MHz.
5. **Fit and subtract the continuum from the line data**, writing a new output and keeping the original. Most
   often reported: CASA uvcontsub, 0th or 1st order, over line-free channels of the same spw (416 papers name
   uvcontsub, my count; spread 2013-2026). Alternatives:
   - fit the continuum spws and apply the model to the line spw (2019ApJ...873...50D, 2019A&A...624L...7V,
     2020A&A...636A..37D; 2013ApJ...763..120C with a CLEAN-component model from three off-line bands);
   - subtract a continuum model during deconvolution: ALMA-IMF built a start model from the `.tt0` and `.tt1`
     cleanest-continuum products and subtracted it in the visibilities at the start of tclean
     (2023A&A...678A.194C);
   - mstransform with `fitorder=1` (2020PASJ...72...36T; per observing day in 2017ApJ...850L...5T);
   - image-plane imcontsub (37 papers, my count): 2024ApJ...961..228S, 2025A&A...698A.204H; zeroth order inside a
     20 x 20 arcsec mask (2025A&A...701A.120P); on the smoothed 7m+12m cube before feathering
     (2025A&A...702A.133K); chosen to mitigate the JvM effect (2024AJ....167...66Y), because uvcontsub
     over-subtracted (2024ApJ...977..251H), or because of a phase-center offset (2025PASJ...77..139I);
   - per-pixel STATCONT (2021A&A...656A..46M, 2017A&A...604A...6S), after convolving all cubes to a common beam
     (2021A&A...656A..46M); per-pixel 2-sigma clipped channel averages for a line search (2021ApJ...907..122M); a
     median along the frequency axis before a SoFiA search (2025A&A...702A.183H) or over the full spw
     (2020ApJ...905...25G);
   - GILDAS: UV-BASELINE degree zero (2021A&A...652A..98G), uv-subtract (2020ApJ...890...29F); CLASS baselines on
     extracted spectra (2019A&A...628A..27B, 2019A&A...632A..19T);
   - AIPS UVLSF (2015ApJ...808...97Y); AIPS visibility-domain average of far-off-line channels
     (2017Icar..286..289L); MIRIAD image-plane polynomials (2015ApJ...814...22E).
   After per-spw subtraction, check that overlapping windows agree: 2019Natur.570...83M additionally aligned spws
   by subtracting a line a*nu + b.
6. **Combine data sets: per data set or after combining.** Per data set: 2014A&A...572L...9R; 2024ApJ...972..147C
   (12 m and 7 m separately); 2017A&A...601A...4A and 2017A&A...607L...5Q before merging with IRAM 30m;
   2020ApJ...892L..18I per cycle. Combined first: 2014A&A...570L..14R combined 12 m and ACA, self-calibrated, then
   subtracted; 2019PASJ...71..105T ran uvcontsub on combined ten-day data; 2026ApJ..1005...94N (concat with
   visweightscale) and 2025A&A...703A..15M (concat, then statwt) subtracted from the combined visibilities.
   Combining first is reasonable when flux scales, frames and spectral setups match (step 2); subtract per data
   set when they differ.
7. **Image the line cube and run the checks below.** Measure noise in line-free channels of the subtracted cube
   (2024ApJ...966...59S used 1000 random regions in line-free channels; 2025ApJ...989...30Z imaged > 200 km/s of
   velocity range so enough line-free channels exist to measure the background noise).

### CASA notes (general practice, not from the pack)

- Older `uvcontsub` call, one order chosen explicitly:
  `uvcontsub(vis='x.ms', fitspw='0:20~110;400~480', fitorder=1, want_cont=False)`, which writes `x.ms.contsub`.
  Use `fitorder=0` for a constant. Newer CASA 6 releases replaced this task with one taking `outputvis`,
  `fitspec`, `fitorder` and `fitmethod`, keeping the old one as `uvcontsub_old`. The pack does not establish the
  release where this changed: run `help(uvcontsub)` in the installed version before writing calls.
- `imcontsub(imagename, linefile, contfile, fitorder, chans)` fits each pixel's spectrum; run it on cubes with a
  common beam and spectral frame.
- The ALMA pipeline also subtracts continuum for its delivered cubes (2018A&A...617A..54L, 2025A&A...702A.276P,
  2024A&A...689A.145S). Treat pipeline channel ranges as a starting point and re-check them for line-rich sources,
  as 2026A&A...709A..93D did with findcont.

## Parameter defaults and ranges

These are choices papers reported for their own sources, not transferable defaults. Size masks from the measured
line extent, and choose fit order from bandwidth, continuum curvature and the validation in "Verification checks".

| parameter | most often reported | range seen | papers (count) | examples |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Domain | uv plane (visibilities) | uv, image plane, per-pixel statistical | about 694 uv, 99 image plane, 20 both (my count) | uv: 2016ApJ...829....6W, 2023A&A...673A..46B; image: 2024ApJ...961..228S, 2025A&A...701A.120P; by data set: 2014ApJ...797...90S, 2023MNRAS.521.1045R |
| Fit order | 1 (linear) | 0 to 2 in uv; up to 6 in the image plane for a planet; Savitzky-Golay order 3 | 168 first order, 70 zeroth, 9 both, 4 second order (my count) | 1: 2017ApJ...842...94T, 2025ApJS..280...33Y, 2025A&A...703A..15M; 0: 2019A&A...628A.118B, 2023A&A...673A..46B, 2025PASJ...77..733T; 2: 2025NatAs...9.1476C, 2025PSJ.....6..286M; 3 and 6 (image plane): 2022MNRAS.514.2994G; S-G: 2019ApJ...870L..26C |
| Order per target | lower order where lines crowd | 0 to 1 | examples | zeroth for the line-rich H13CN cube, first for others (2015ApJ...814...22E); first for one quasar, zeroth for the others (2024A&A...689A.220T); zero- or first-order fits (2021ApJ...909...98Y) |
| Excluded half-width, disks and envelopes | +-25 km/s around each line | +-15 to +-60 km/s | 18 state a half-width in this range, 7 of them +-25 (my count) | +-25: 2021A&A...648A..19W, 2024A&A...692A.155K; +-15 from systemic: 2025ApJ...989...30Z, 2025A&A...698A.183D; +-20: 2021AJ....162...99F, 2024A&A...689A..85G; +-30: 2025A&A...703A..15M; +-35: 2021MNRAS.506.1978L; +-50: 2020ApJ...898..146M |
| Excluded half-width, galaxies | no single value; +-300, +-500 and +-1000 each appear twice | +-300 to +-1500 km/s | 10 (my count) | +-300 about the CO(2-1) sky frequency (2020ApJ...890...29F); +-400 to +-450 from [C II] (2024A&A...690A.197M); +-750 (2019A&A...632L...7J); +-800 of z_CO (2023MNRAS.518..691J); also \|v - v_sys\| < 500 km/s, with < 1000 and 100-2200 km/s tested (2025ApJ...989...83B); full mask widths of about 300-600 km/s, not half-widths (2021A&A...652A..98G) |
| Fit channels, galaxies | \|v\| > 500 to 1500 km/s | see examples | examples | \|v\| > 500 (2021A&A...645A..33B); > 560 (2021A&A...654L...8C); > 1000 from the CO(3-2) peak (2019A&A...628A.118B); > 1500 from the [C II] centroid (2019A&A...630A..59B); -2650 to -690 and 820 to 2280 km/s (2023A&A...673A..46B); -2500 to -500 and +500 to +2500 (2019MNRAS.489.1927S) |
| Exclusion relative to line width | 1.25 to 2.5 x FWHM, or 3 sigma_v | 8 x FWHM as a check | examples | 2.5 x FWHM total width, about 900 km/s on average, checked with 8 x FWHM (2020ApJ...904..131N); 2 x FWHM (2019ApJ...887..107F); 1.25 x FWHM from the peak (2020ApJ...904..130V); 3 sigma_v (2020A&A...643A...2B) |
| Undetected line in the subtraction | assume 300 km/s width at the known redshift | n/a | 1 | 2020A&A...637A..84P (400 km/s is used for upper limits, a separate step, in 2020A&A...641A.155V) |
| Edge channels dropped | a few channels per spw edge | 5 per edge | examples | five at each end of each window (2025A&A...699A.187G); end channels (2016A&A...586A..69P) |
| STATCONT clipping | sigma clipping, alpha = 2 | alpha 2 to 3; default | examples | alpha = 2: 2017A&A...604A...6S, 2017A&A...602A..59C; alpha = 3 with -0.5 sigma correction: 2018ApJ...866...87B; default: 2021A&A...656A..46M |
| Line-free fraction | n/a | about 5% to 20% of the bandwidth | 4 | 10-20% (2018ApJ...866...87B); >= 20% in most spws, 10% in two (2021A&A...647A..87L); about 5% (2020ApJ...905...25G); c-SCM can fail below 10% for complex spectra (2018A&A...609A.101S) |
| Line-free channels by position, line-rich core | n/a | 144 channels (280 MHz) at line-poor to 10 (17 MHz) at line-rich positions | 1 | 2018A&A...615A.141B |

## Pitfalls

- **Line emission inside the continuum channels.** Leads to over-subtraction (negative bowls at line positions)
  and a biased continuum (general reasoning). In NGC 253, lines made up 5.3% to 36.6% of the total flux per
  50 GHz bin (2021A&A...656A..46M), so a line-rich "continuum" image can be badly contaminated. An automatic first
  selection left skewed baselines in some ALMA-QUARKS fields until the channels were re-picked by hand
  (2025ApJS..280...33Y).
- **Statistical methods in hot cores.** STATCONT's c-SCM assumes a flat continuum and can fail with < 10% line-free
  channels in complex spectra (2018A&A...609A.101S, tested on 750 synthetic spectra). ALMA-IMF reports that its
  continuum start model was overestimated at bright hot cores with line forests, leaving localised negative
  residuals, and that STATCONT subtraction can be imperfect towards hot cores or bright outflows
  (2023A&A...678A.194C). Per-pixel STATCONT also overestimated the continuum in noisy regions and was "not
  optimal" for lines near the noise level (2021A&A...656A..46M).
- **Absorption plus uv-plane subtraction.** Default visibility-domain subtraction left negative residuals near CS
  in 2014ApJ...792L..25V; 2015ApJ...814...22E moved to the image plane for this reason; 2024ApJ...977..251H moved to
  imcontsub because uvcontsub over-subtracted; 2020MNRAS.498.4109J rejected UVCONTSUB after visual inspection
  showed continuum under-subtraction and adopted a first-order IMCONTSUB on one sideband.
- **Image-plane fitting needs consistent beams and flux scales.** 2021A&A...656A..46M convolved cubes to a common
  beam before per-pixel subtraction; 2024AJ....167...66Y chose imcontsub to mitigate the JvM effect, so check the
  residual/restored flux scaling before comparing image-plane and uv-plane results.
- **High fit order.** A linear or higher fit extrapolated across a broad line, or from one side of the spectrum, is
  poorly constrained (general practice); prefer order 0 unless the slope is measured on both sides. High orders
  also remove broad wings: in Venus PH3 work the polynomial baseline heavily suppressed the pressure-broadened
  wings, so cloud-level abundance profiles could not be derived (2022MNRAS.514.2994G, 6th-order image-plane fits).
- **Few line-free channels.** Papers used the other sideband or an SED (2020ApJ...905...85S), extrapolated the
  broad spw to narrow spws (2019A&A...624L...7V), or switched to STATCONT (2020A&A...636A..67A).
- **Uncertainties.** Formal line errors ignore continuum-subtraction systematics (2019ApJ...882..118X says its
  uncertainties "might be underestimated" for this reason). Reported terms are specific to their data: a 10%
  subtraction error added in quadrature (2013ApJ...773L..34K); a continuum-level uncertainty of about 11%
  (2023A&A...677A..15M; 2021A&A...653A.129C, which added it to the fit errors). Better: estimate the term from
  the spread over alternative masks and fit orders (as 2019Natur.570...83M did, <= 7%) or propagate the fit
  uncertainty, state which quantity it applies to (line flux, peak, ratio), remember a fitted baseline correlates
  errors across channels, and keep it separate from the flux-calibration uncertainty.
- **Missing flux is separate.** Interferometric filtering is a different error that subtraction does not fix
  (2018A&A...615A.141B, 2014A&A...570L..14R, 2020ApJ...890...29F report resolved-out emission; 2023A&A...678A.194C
  links negative bowls and absorption features to missing short spacings); do not attribute negative bowls to
  subtraction without checking short-spacing coverage.
- **Reporting.** Channel ranges and fit settings are routinely missing: 323 not-stated label occurrences about
  line-free channels (pack aggregate, labels overlap); 94 of the 358 `line_free_channels` fields say the ranges
  were not given (my count). Good examples list the channels per spw (2018MNRAS.481.4931E: SPW0 25-118;
  SPW1 50-75, 90-118; SPW2 all; SPW3 9-35, 95-119; 2024A&A...682A..54T), or frequency ranges (2018A&A...612A..29B),
  ideally with the frame (2017MNRAS.472.1239B, barycentric; 2023A&A...673A..46B, sky frequency).

## Verification checks

Checks papers ran:

- Image with and without subtraction and compare (2018A&A...609A..47P, no significant difference;
  2024ApJ...972..111L, the redshifted [C II] substructure persists without uvcontsub; 2018ApJ...852..122H;
  2024ApJ...966L..13D and 2020ApJ...889L..24P made both cubes).
- Vary the fit order, channel ranges or exclusion width (2012ApJ...757..129R, line wings persist;
  2019A&A...628A.118B, first order did not significantly change results; 2019Natur.570...83M, different line-free
  sets changed spectra by <= 7%; 2020ApJ...904..131N, an 8 x FWHM exclusion did not bias the continuum;
  2025ApJ...989...83B, two alternative exclusion cases gave faint, likely spurious deficits or excesses near CO).
- Try another continuum estimate: alternate sideband (2020ApJ...905...85S, "no appreciable differences"); the
  constant from a Gaussian-plus-constant fit agreed with the 120 uJy continuum from line-free basebands
  (2013ApJ...770...13W); two continuum estimates for stacked spectra (2020ApJ...904..131N); LTE modelling
  repeated on STATCONT-subtracted spectra gave consistent N and T_ex (2026A&A...709A..93D).
- Residual flatness and alignment: residual slope < 30 mJy across each spw, with overlapping spws aligned
  (2019Natur.570...83M); coherence of continuum maps from adjacent sub-windows (2015A&A...576A.129B); line-free
  channels checked for residuals (2024A&A...689A.317G).
- Flux-scale consistency between executions or settings (2021A&A...648A..19W; 2018ApJ...856..143I, continuum
  fluxes between settings within about 20%).
- Noise and flux errors from line-free channels: errors scaled so Var(S/sigma) = 1 in line-free channels
  (2019MNRAS.485.2092C); flux uncertainty from the spread of integrals over equally wide line-free ranges
  (2025ApJ...989...30Z) or over 500 random Keplerian masks in line-free channels (2025ApJ...985...84L); line-cube
  noise checked against the expected thermal noise (2026A&A...709A..93D).
- Compare total line flux against single-dish data where available (2014ApJ...797...90S, 76-97% recovered;
  2024MNRAS.527.9343L against JCMT).

What a careful referee adds:

- Ask for a reproducibility record: CASA and pipeline versions, the input data set and spw mapping, channel
  indexing convention, spectral frame and rest frequency, the exact line-free ranges per spw, fit order and
  fitting task (or STATCONT settings). Rest frequency and frame are among the items cards flag as missing
  (2021A&A...648A..19W, 2024ApJ...972..111L, 2016ApJ...829....6W).
- Look for negative bowls in moment-0 maps at line positions and for line residue in the continuum image at line
  peaks.
- For line-rich sources, compare the continuum image against one made from a strict, small channel set.
- For absorption studies, confirm the continuum level used for optical depth came from data that were not
  continuum-subtracted, and state the covering factor assumed.

## Source cards

The full list of papers is in `references/sources.md`. The most informative for this technique:

- 2019Natur.570...83M: iterative uv subtraction per spw, residual-slope criterion, a*nu + b spw alignment, <= 7% test
- 2018A&A...609A.101S: STATCONT method paper; flat-continuum assumption, fails below about 10% line-free channels
- 2018ApJ...866...87B: STATCONT c-SCM plus aggregate deviation spectrum to select channels for uv subtraction
- 2021A&A...656A..46M: per-pixel STATCONT after common-beam convolution; line contribution 5.3-36.6% of flux
- 2023A&A...678A.194C: ALMA-IMF continuum start model subtracted in tclean; failure modes at hot cores
- 2026A&A...709A..93D: findcont start, 3 sigma iteration, uvcontsub first order, STATCONT cross-check of LTE fit
- 2025ApJS..280...33Y: model-spectrum line masking, skewed-baseline check, manual re-selection
- 2020ApJ...904..131N: exclusion of 2.5 x FWHM, tested against 8 x FWHM; two continuum estimates
- 2025ApJ...989...83B: three exclusion cases; absorption optical depth against the unsubtracted continuum
- 2015ApJ...814...22E: image-plane subtraction to avoid uv artifacts with absorption; order by line richness
- 2018A&A...615A.141B: STATCONT channel counts by position; absorption modelled on non-subtracted data
- 2025ApJ...989...30Z: separate pseudo-continuum copy, self-cal, then uvcontsub on the same channel mask
- 2019A&A...628A.118B: zeroth-order uv fit with |v| > 1000 km/s; first order tested
- 2024ApJ...972..111L: uvcontsub fitorder 1; checked the cube without subtraction
- 2024ApJ...977..251H: imcontsub chosen because uvcontsub over-subtracted
- 2020MNRAS.498.4109J: uvcontsub and imcontsub compared; first-order imcontsub on one sideband adopted
- 2022MNRAS.514.2994G: high-order image-plane baselines suppress broad wings
- 2025NatAs...9.1476C: comet workflow with phaseshift, 2nd-order uvcontsub, cvel2
- 2021A&A...648A..19W: +-25 km/s flagging, 125 MHz averaging, self-cal before subtraction, execution flux check
- 2018MNRAS.481.4931E: explicit per-spw channel lists
- 2014ApJ...790..160S: skipped subtraction where too few line-free channels
- 2020ApJ...905...85S: continuum as a free fit parameter; alternate-sideband test
- 2013ApJ...770...13W: continuum from line-free spws checked against a Gaussian-plus-constant fit
- 2012ApJ...757..129R: polynomial order and channel ranges varied

=== EVIDENCE PACK (continuum-subtraction, rebuilt) ===
# Evidence pack: Continuum subtraction and line-free channel selection (`continuum-subtraction`)

Scope: separating line and continuum in the uv or image plane

Built from 1551 non-held-out ALMA papers whose methods cards passed mechanical evidence verification (every quote below is verbatim from the paper). Years 2012-2026.
Matcher (regex over analysis steps): `uvcontsub|imcontsub|statcont|continuum[- ]subtract|subtract\w* (?:the )?continuum|line[- ]free`; processing fields: imaging.continuum_subtraction, imaging.line_free_channels
Tagging is by regex, so a few papers may be off-topic: ignore those.

### Assumptions (bibcode: value)
- 2018A&A...615A.141B: LTE; extended foreground filling beam
- 2018ApJ...856..143I: Gaussian fits within beam recover bulk of flux for compact sources; NGC 1614 uses spatially integrated spectra
- 2025ApJS..280...33Y: Candidate starless cores lack outflows (CO) and ionized gas (H30alpha)
- 2020ApJ...904..131N: Gaussian line profile when correcting for the tails beyond 1.2xFWHM (factor 1.19)
- 2020ApJ...904..131N: Continuum flat within the bandwidth of interest
- 2020ApJ...904..131N: Recomputed weights comparable across datasets so no single source dominates the average
- 2025A&A...701A.120P: Razor-thin intrinsically round disk; projected geometry determines inclination.
- 2025A&A...701A.120P: Negligible radial velocity, Vrad=0; azimuthally averaged ring surface density.
- 2018PASJ...70...36I: H0=70, OmegaM=0.3, OmegaL=0.7
- 2023A&A...678A.194C: Dirty beam volume is integrated up to its first null to compute JvM epsilon factor
- 2023A&A...678A.194C: Noise estimated from a smaller clean beam should be scaled up by the dirty-to-clean beam volume ratio (1/epsilon)

- 2022A&A...665A...3J: For ID9316 CO(5-4) on the edge of the spectrum, line flux is estimated assuming an identical line profile to CO(4-3)
- 2026ApJ..1003L..30W: Keplerian rotation with the adopted TW Hya geometry for the HC3N mask
- 2020ApJ...888...72K: 12CO and 13CO optically thick so T_B = T_gas
- 2021A&A...646A..26B: Target unresolved with native beams; no continuum subtraction
- 2025ApJ...987...77J: Mean molecular weight mu = 2.8; for distant clouds: specific dust opacity at 1.3 mm kappa_1.3mm = 0.01 cm^2 g^-1 (beta = 1.8), uniform dust temperature T_dust …
- 2025NatAs...9.1326S: [C/H] = 1.6e-4, epsilon_8.6 = 4.5e-18 MJy/sr/cm^-2/G0, G0 = 8e4, N_H = 5.5e21 cm^-2
- 2024A&A...686A.235P: Sgr A* position inferred from the S2 orbit.
- 2020ApJ...892..119M: Cosmology Om=0.3, OL=0.7, H0=70
- 2025MNRAS.537.3009R: OH and O2 lines dominate telluric emission; all other telluric line fluxes set to zero
- 2025ApJ...990...53T: Emission treated as optically thin for continuum subtraction, with over-subtraction artifacts acknowledged
- 2018Natur.553..178S: grey body, beta_IR 1-2, T_dust 20-60 K
- 2026A&A...705L..16C: Absolute flux calibration uncertainty about 10%
- 2018ApJ...866L..12G: Planck 2015 cosmology
- 2018ApJ...869L..49I: CO peak optically thick and in LTE


### Limitations the authors admit
- 2018A&A...615A.141B: Dust optically thick so N_H2 and mass are lower limits
- 2018A&A...615A.141B: Interferometer filters out extended emission
- 2018ApJ...856..143I: Only two J-transitions, so RADEX needs an assumed column density
- 2018ApJ...856..143I: Outflow N(H2) is a lower limit, and starburst galaxies lack isotopologue data
- 2025ApJS..280...33Y: A few weak continuum peaks are missed by getsf
- 2025ApJS..280...33Y: Core separations are lower limits owing to projection
- 2025ApJS..280...33Y: N_line can be underestimated for line-rich cores
- 2025ApJS..280...33Y: Free-free emission may inflate the 1.3 mm flux and masses of 27 cores above 100 Msun
- 2025ApJS..280...33Y: 1.3 mm high-resolution data trace core kernels and filter out envelope mass, so high-mass candidates are a lower limit
- 2020ApJ...904..131N: A very faint outflow below the stack detection limit cannot be ruled out
- 2020ApJ...904..131N: Stack is meaningful only if the mean property is well defined; sub-kpc morphology not recoverable
- 2020ApJ...904..131N: Simulated galaxies and observed quasar hosts are not the same population (core differs)
- 2018ApJ...852..122H: Models are not optimal; dust modeling and multi-line fitting deferred; perturbed temperature model not physically self-consistent
- 2018ApJ...852..122H: Absolute alpha uncertain by ~0.4 from flux calibration
- 2025A&A...701A.120P: Gas-mass uncertainties omit conversion-factor systematics.
- 2025A&A...701A.120P: Stellar mass has quasar-subtraction and limited-filter systematics; quoted aperture-flux errors exclude potential systematic errors.
- 2025A&A...701A.120P: Ring parameters are correlated; kinematic uncertainties omit geometry uncertainty and are underestimated.
- 2025A&A...701A.120P: Current S/N and resolution cannot investigate potential radial motions.
- 2025A&A...701A.120P: Spherical mass distribution oversimplifies the companion; quasar virial estimates have large resolution-related uncertainties.
- 2025A&A...701A.120P: Tidal analysis uses difficult-to-verify simplifying assumptions; projection and unknown outer mass budgets limit interpretation.
- 2025A&A...701A.120P: Available information cannot distinguish merger and outflow origins.
- 2018PASJ...70...36I: Mdyn has large unconstrained systematic uncertainty; sources only marginally resolved
- 2023A&A...678A.194C: DR1 includes only 12m-array data without 7m or TP short spacings, causing negative bowls and absorption features; Band 3 spw0 (N2H+) is excluded from DR1 due to extended emission artifacts.
- 2023A&A...678A.194C: Divergent channels around the V_LSR of 12CO (2-1) and C18O (2-1) were masked and left uncleaned in the final cubes, and G351.77 was cleaned only to 10 sigma.
- 2023A&A...678A.194C: Overestimation of the continuum startmodel at bright hot cores with line forests causes localised negative residuals, and STATCONT continuum subtraction can be imperfect towards hot cores or bright o…
- 2023A&A...678A.194C: 91 detected cores with Complex-type DCN spectra (typically higher mass ~17 M_sun) and 238 non-detected cores (including lowest-mass cores < 1 M_sun) are excluded from the single-component kinematic a…
- 2025ApJ...989...30Z: Lower-amplitude velocity perturbations cannot be excluded at the available spectral resolution
- 2025ApJ...989...30Z: Sample too small and ages uncertain for a definitive correlation conclusion
- 2025ApJ...989...30Z: Hypothesis based on SO plus perturbations in a small sample dominated by bright early-type stars
- 2020ApJ...905...89Y: Absolute gas-to-dust ratios are not interpreted because of uncertain C18O abundance and dust absorption coefficient
- 2020ApJ...905...89Y: Dust scattering not modelled in the SED fit
- 2020ApJ...905...89Y: Velocity features within the 0.45 arcsec beam are uncertain
- 2020ApJ...905...89Y: Axisymmetric Keplerian models leave significant residuals in the asymmetric disk
- 2020ApJ...905...89Y: Resolution too limited to separate vortex from spiral origin or to resolve the bump profile
- 2023A&A...673A..46B: The CO-to-H2 conversion factor alpha_CO in molecular outflows is poorly constrained.
- 2023A&A...673A..46B: NED02 is located close to the edge of the ALMA primary beam FWHM, resulting in lower CO(1-0) sensitivity, and its compact size means its morphology and kinematics are more affected by beam smearing.
- 2023A&A...673A..46B: Negative residuals around the systemic velocity in all 3D-BAROLO fits indicate overfitting of the rotational structure, and 3D-BAROLO may force an average rotation curve that produces artificial resi…
- 2023A&A...673A..46B: Spatial resolution (~1 kpc) is insufficient to definitively distinguish between a compact outflow and interaction-induced disk asymmetries; higher resolution data are needed.
- 2023A&A...673A..46B: Hard X-ray emission from NED02 cannot be assessed because it falls within the PSF wings of the brighter companion NED01 in XMM-Newton.
- 2023A&A...673A..46B: X-ray wind energetics are affected by large uncertainties (~70% on absorption amount, plus uncertainties on wind opening angle, launching radius, and black hole mass).
- 2026ApJ..1005...94N: The wide range of published distances to NGC 4061 (68–112 Mpc in NED) introduces a ~37% systematic uncertainty in M_BH, dominating the total error budget.
- 2026ApJ..1005...94N: Parameter degeneracies exist between M_BH and M/L_F814W, between y_cen and v_off (due to the beam size relative to the continuum peak), between inclination i and M/L_F814W, and between flux normaliza…
- 2026ApJ..1005...94N: Adopting the HST F555W band instead of F814W for the stellar mass model introduces an additional ~20% uncertainty in M_BH due to stronger dust attenuation in F555W.
- 2026ApJ..1005...94N: Direct event-horizon-scale imaging of the NGC 4061 SMBH (~0.4 uas) is impossible even with ngEHT (~10 uas resolution).
- 2020MNRAS.496..598C: Continuum source size uncertain because of simple source geometry, low S/N and lens modeling synthesized ALMA data
- 2020MNRAS.496..598C: Higher-S/N images required to confirm mm–UV source offset
- 2020MNRAS.496..598C: Differential magnification can distort CO line profiles
- 2020MNRAS.496..598C: Only projected clump distances are observed
- 2020MNRAS.496..598C: Several clump detections have low significance and need deeper confirmation
- 2020MNRAS.496..598C: Single-epoch UFO and molecular energetics may sample different driving epochs
- 2020MNRAS.496..598C: Energy channeled into ionized gas is omitted
- 2020MNRAS.496..598C: Outflow dynamical depletion time excludes star formation depletion
- 2018A&A...615A..28D: Dust masses and dust-to-gas ratios uncertain by about a factor of 5
- 2018A&A...615A..28D: Extended emission may be resolved out
- 2025ApJ...989...83B: Bandpass calibration effects on tau_0 and tau_CO are not quantified
- 2025ApJ...989...83B: CO(3-2) and CO(2-1) opacities are less reliable than CO(1-0), so LTE reconciliation is not attempted
- 2025ApJ...989...83B: Xa1 maximum recoverable scale cannot entirely rule out diffuse emission over larger regions
- 2018A&A...612A..29B: Outflow mass estimates are order of magnitude given chain of assumptions (alpha_CO, excitation).
- 2015ApJ...814...22E: Simple spherical models do not perfectly match; abundance profiles are ad hoc
- 2012ApJ...757..129R: Model parameters are not unique nor optimized; limited resolution
- 2023A&A...677A..15M: The 1.2'' angular resolution is insufficient to resolve the four embedded compact cores (A, B, C, D) or conclusively establish spatial segregation between molecular species whose peak separations are…
- 2023A&A...677A..15M: A single-temperature LTE model cannot fully capture the temperature gradient in G31, causing a small number of transitions (e.g. in CH3COCH3 and CH3OH v=0) to be poorly reproduced.
- 2023A&A...677A..15M: For species whose unblended transitions only span low upper-state energies (E_U < 100 K, such as CH3CHO, aGg'-(CH2OH)2, 13CH3CN, CH313CN, C2H3CN, C2H5CN), the fitted excitation temperatures may be un…
- 2014ApJ...797...90S: Mass estimates depend on uncertain X_CO; may be an order of magnitude lower if optically thin
- 2018A&A...609A.101S: c-SCM assumes a flat continuum and can fail with <10% line-free channels for complex spectra
- 2013ApJ...770...13W: Source only marginally resolved; intrinsic size poorly constrained and inclination unknown
- 2013ApJ...770...13W: L_FIR uncertainties exclude dust temperature uncertainty
- 2019Natur.570...83M: Line could be unlucky combination of molecular lines or foreground absorption
- 2026A&A...709A..93D: Limited bandwidth (~8 GHz), modest number of securely detected transitions (7 features), and high line density mean coincidental line blending cannot be excluded, making the GA detection tentative.
- 2026A&A...709A..93D: Uncertainties are affected by line blending, baseline effects, and simplifying LTE assumptions.
- 2026A&A...709A..93D: With only two GA detections to date, conclusions on formation mechanisms remain preliminary.
- 2016ApJ...829....6W: CO mass assumes optically thin LTE and could be larger by a factor of a few or more
- 2016ApJ...829....6W: Debris mass depends strongly on assumed size distribution, density, albedo
- 2021MNRAS.504.2687N: LTE assumption and morpho-kinematic models are crude and oversimplified (e.g., modelling shock/pulsation broadening as Gaussian fluctuations and assuming simple power-law radial profiles).
- 2021MNRAS.504.2687N: Poor uv coverage at short baselines for the 2013.1.00166.S (28SiO, CO, HCN) data limits reliable analysis to r <= 60 au (1 arcsec) and prevents quantitative statements at larger radii.
- 2021MNRAS.504.2687N: The 3D inclination angle of the blue stream with the line of sight and the inclination of the rotation axis with the plane of the sky are unknown.
- 2024ApJ...966L..13D: Model ignores non-LTE level populations, so computed line fluxes are not over-interpreted
- 2024ApJ...966L..13D: Model is a mean 1D lukewarm chromosphere, without hot small-filling-factor plasma or cold molecular pockets
- 2024ApJ...966L..13D: H30alpha emission in the model requires an arbitrarily added hydrogen ionization fraction
- 2019MNRAS.485..229R: Mass may differ by up to an order of magnitude due to X-factor and F_ul uncertainty
- 2019MNRAS.485..229R: Filling factor of 1 gives lower limit on tau; velocities are only apparent line-of-sight motions
- 2021MNRAS.506.1978L: The modified blackbody parameters (lambda_0 and beta_0) for the inner warm component are poorly constrained because the sub-mm slope relies only on low-S/N Band 6 and Band 7 inner excesses.
- 2021MNRAS.506.1978L: Lower sensitivity in the Band 6 image prevents an independent detection of the ~20% SW/NE flux asymmetry seen in Band 7.
- 2021MNRAS.506.1978L: The HST coronagraph occults emission within ~2.25'', limiting the ability to quantify the significance of the minor-axis scattered light asymmetry.
- 2021MNRAS.506.1978L: Visibility-space BIC penalises additional model parameters disproportionately because a large fraction of the 19.4 million visibility points are dominated by noise and unaffected by the disc asymmetr…
- 2021MNRAS.506.1978L: The parametric models assume a simple 2D Gaussian clump or a radially constant eccentricity and pericentre orientation, which may be too simplistic for a broad belt with a radially decaying forced ec…
- 2022MNRAS.514.2994G: Excluding short ALMA baselines (<33m) filters out spatial scales larger than 4.3-7.1 arcsec, heavily downweighting the centre of Venus (15.2 arcsec diameter) so that only ~22% of continuum survives a…
- 2022MNRAS.514.2994G: Cloud-level PH3 abundance and altitude profiles cannot be quantified because broad pressure-broadened line wings are heavily suppressed by the polynomial baseline subtraction and lack laboratory PH3-…
- 2022MNRAS.514.2994G: The 1D radiative transfer model neglects scattering, beam convolution, and limb darkening, generating SO2 lines 15-25% less deep than detailed 3D/scattering radiative transfer codes.
- 2022MNRAS.514.2994G: JCMT SO2 and PH3 observations in June 2017 were not strictly simultaneous, being separated by 3 days between the last SO2 spectrum and the start of PH3 observing.
- 2025PSJ.....6..286M: ALMA rubidium-maser cable-routing error likely introduced an unknown frequency shift and slight spectral broadening
- 2025PSJ.....6..286M: ALMA winds are not vertically resolved; equatorial-jet contributions cannot be isolated
- 2025PSJ.....6..286M: GCM and ALMA forward-model inputs differ in vertical treatment
- 2025PSJ.....6..286M: Best combined fit is driven by the poorer-fitting egress lightspot; untested wind shapes may fit better
- 2025PSJ.....6..286M: Longitudinal symmetry excludes possible localized atmospheric events
- 2025PSJ.....6..286M: HASI lacks temporal and latitudinal resolution
- 2025PSJ.....6..286M: Omitting methane overestimates bending angles by roughly 0.5% under the stated 1.5% methane assumption
- 2025PSJ.....6..286M: Mid-event arcs are not reproduced by any tested forward model
- 2017AJ....154..206L: C3H8 lines blended so abundance uncertain; south pole partly hidden
- 2013ApJ...777...92S: 1.3 mm data S/N modest with sparse uv coverage; large fraction of CO(1-0) absorption resolved out
- 2025A&A...703A..15M: Dust mass uncertainties are formal only and do not include systematic uncertainties in dust opacity (which can reach a factor of several) or non-single dust temperatures.
- 2025A&A...703A..15M: CO mass estimates assume LTE at 20 K and ISM isotopic ratios; non-LTE/subthermal excitation, different kinetic temperatures, isotope-selective photodissociation, or fractionation can alter true CO ma…
- 2025A&A...703A..15M: Predicted collisional mass loss rates (M_dot_Dmin) have large uncertainties (40-60% formal, potentially much larger systematically) because smallest grain sizes may deviate from the blowout size in h…
- 2025A&A...703A..15M: Secular planet stirring calculations assume a massless disk and neglect disk self-gravity, which would require a more massive planet if the disk mass exceeds the planet mass.
- 2021ApJ...910...53V: ALMA misses large-scale flux (about half of earlier CO flux, scales around 5 arcsec)
- 2021ApJ...910...53V: True gas morphology cannot be determined without higher resolution data
- 2021ApJ...910...53V: A molecular disk cannot be ruled out
- 2021ApJ...910...53V: Cavity D proximity to the cluster center gives a strong systematic effect on its age and power
- 2021ApJ...910...53V: X_CO not calibrated in BCGs; Galactic value adopted
- 2024ApJ...977...37N: NH3 and H2CO temperatures cannot be compared quantitatively because of a factor of four beam-size difference and peak offsets
- 2024ApJ...977...37N: 24 micron SFRs are lower limits because masked pixels around bright sources are interpolated
- 2024ApJ...977...37N: Errors on SFR likely underestimated because of IMF stochasticity for small clusters
- 2024ApJ...977...37N: H2CO temperature errors underestimated above 80 K
- 2024ApJ...977...37N: Distances for non-bar clouds may be highly uncertain
- 2024ApJ...977...37N: Single-phase gas assumption is an oversimplification
- 2015A&A...576A.129B: Hypotheses for gGg' upper limit admittedly questionable; lines too few and weak to dispel confusion
- 2020MNRAS.499.2493A: Lower flux than in 2012 may be due to emission outside the maximum recoverable scale
- 2020MNRAS.499.2493A: Unclear whether the narrow line in the compact-source spectrum belongs to the compact source
- 2020MNRAS.499.2493A: Blob proper motions depend on identification of Weigelt blobs with ALMA sources
- 2018MNRAS.481.4931E: Velocity resolution is modest and dynamical modelling is deferred.

### 2018A&A...615A.141B: Accelerating infall and rotational spin-up in the hot molecular core G31.41+0.31 (2018; star_formation, astrochemistry; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Is the compact, unfragmented appearance of the hot molecular core G31.41+0.31 real, and what do its rotation and infall kinematics reveal about its collapse?
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: STATCONT on the 218 GHz window; details in Cesaroni et al. 2017 [Observations: "The continuum was determined from the broadest spectral window, centered at 218 GHz, using the STATCONT[^4] algorithm"]
- processing.imaging.line_free_channels: STATCONT continuum channels: 144 (280 MHz) at line-poor to 10 (17 MHz) at line-rich positions [Observations: "ranges from 144, which corresponds to a bandwidth of 280 MHz, at line-poor positions, to 10, which corresponds to a bandwidth of 17 MHz"]
- step 1: Image cubes and continuum
  - method: CLEAN with Briggs robust 0.5 in CASA; continuum from broadest SPW with STATCONT; further analysis in GILDAS [Observations: "Further imaging and analysis were done with the GILDAS[^3] software package."]
  - software: CASA, GILDAS, STATCONT
  - parameter: robust = 0.5 [Observations: "the ROBUST parameter of Briggs ((cite: briggs95)) set equal to 0.5"]
  - validation: High continuum rms attributed to dynamic range, not STATCONT [Observations: "the high rms noise of such map is due to dynamic range problems and not to the STATCONT method used to subtract the continuum"]
- step 8: Model absorption and emission at the core center
  - method: XCLASS fit with background continuum layer, emission layer and foreground absorption layer to CH3CN, 13CH3CN, CH3CN v8=1 on non-continuum-subtracted data [Discussion: "we modeled the spectrum at the central pixel with three different components"]
  - software: XCLASS
  - parameter: background T_B=132 K; line width and velocity offset fixed; source fills beam [Discussion: "the temperature of the continuum background layer was assumed to be constant and equal to the brightness temperature $T_{\rm B}=132$ K"]
  - assumption: LTE; extended foreground filling beam [Discussion: "We assumed that the foreground or absorption component is extended and that it fills the beam"]
  - validation: Same fit for ring at 1.32" (background 1.8 K) to check 12C/13C [Discussion: "we fitted both the CH$_3$CN and $^{13}$CH$_3$CN emission in a ring located at a distance of $1.32\ensuremath{''}$ from the center of the core"]
- result: CH3CN emission-layer excitation temperature at center = 151 ± -8/+83 K [Table 4: "$T_e$ = $151^{-8}_{+83}$ K"]
- result: 12C/13C in the ring away from center and in CH3OCHO = ~10 ± not stated dimensionless [Discussion: "we estimated the column densities of CH$_3$OCHO and CH$_3$O$^{13}$CHO in the rings not affected by absorption and obtained \[$^{12}$C/$^{13}$C\] $\simeq$10"]
- limitation (authors): Dust optically thick so N_H2 and mass are lower limits [Analysis: "Therefore, the H$_2$ column density values should be taken as lower limits."]
- limitation (authors): Interferometer filters out extended emission [Discussion: "our ALMA observations can miss extended emission"]
- not stated (card): Line-free channel selection and STATCONT settings in detail; Cell size, image size, masks, clean threshold, deconvolver; Fixed line widths and source sizes used in each MADCUBA annulus fit; Isotopic ratios used in XCLASS fits and initial guesses; Rest frequencies and spectroscopy database used; Flux calibrator and calibration uncertainty (in Cesaroni et al. 2017); Selection of which CH3OCHO lines…

  - method: uvcontsub on line-free channels, then clean, with spectral binning to ~20 km/s [3. OBSERVATIONS AND DATA ANALYSIS: "After subtracting the continuum emission component using the CASA task “uvcontsub”, we applied the “clean” procedure"]

  - fitting: Gaussian line fits (single or double) [4. RESULTS: "To estimate the line fluxes, we fitted Gaussian curves to the continuum-subtracted spectra, within the beam size, at the individual nuclear positions."]
  - validation: Fluxes in 1 arcsec aperture agree within <30% with beam-sized spectra [5.3. Relation between Molecular Line an…: "the estimated fluxes in the 1 ′′ aperture spectra agree within <30% to those in the beam sized spectra."]
- result: HCN v2=1f to v=0 flux ratio at J=4-3, IRAS 12112+0305 NE = ~0.17 ± not stated dimensionless [5.2.1. Observed HCN-to-HCO [+] Flux Rat…: "the HCN v 2 =1f to v=0 flux ratio at J=4–3 of ∼0.17 is even higher than that of IRAS 20551−4250 (∼0.04)"]
- limitation (authors): Only two J-transitions, so RADEX needs an assumed column density [5.2.4. RADEX Calculations and Molecular…: "At least three independent observational data are necessary to meaningfully constrain these three parameters, while we have only two J-transitions."]
- limitation (authors): Outflow N(H2) is a lower limit, and starburst galaxies lack isotopologue data [5.2.3. Intrinsic HCN-to-HCO [+] and HCN…: "we have no isotopologue data for these starburst galaxies."]
