### 2025ApJS..280...33Y: The ALMA-QUARKS Survey. III. Clump-to-core Fragmentation and Searches for High-mass Starless Cores (2025; star_formation, milky_way_ism; large_program_or_survey)
- question: How do IR-bright massive protocluster clumps fragment into cores (observed core separation vs thermal Jeans length), and are there candidate high-mass starless cores among them?
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: uvcontsub in the uv plane, linear fit (fitorder=1), line-free channels [2.2.1 Data Flag and Basic Setups of Dat…: "employing the CASA task *uvcontsub* with a linear fitting (fitorder=1)"]
- processing.imaging.line_free_channels: automatic selection from CDMS model spectrum shifted to field Vlsr and widened to 50 km/s, then manually re-identified per field with Linemarker; the channel lists are not given [2.2.1 Data Flag and Basic Setups of Dat…: "we shifted the model spectrum and expanded it to a width of 50 $\rm km\,s^{-1}$"] [2.2.1 Data Flag and Basic Setups of Dat…: "we manually reidentified the line-free channels for each field based on the TM2+ACA first version data using *Linemarker*"]
- step 1: Concatenate ACA+TM2 and set line-free channels
  - method: Concatenate visibilities; auto-flag line channels from CDMS-matched model spectrum (width 50 km/s around Vlsr); uvcontsub; first tclean; then manual re-identification of line-free channels with Linemarker and iterate [2.2.1 Data Flag and Basic Setups of Dat…: "the raw visibility data for the QUARKS ACA and TM2 observations were first concatenated using the CASA task *concat*"] [2.2.1 Data Flag and Basic Setups of Dat…: "we manually reidentified the line-free channels for each field based on the TM2+ACA first version data using *Linemarker*"]
  - software: CASA, Linemarker, CDMS
  - parameter: model spectrum width 50 km/s, shifted by field Vlsr [2.2.1 Data Flag and Basic Setups of Dat…: "we shifted the model spectrum and expanded it to a width of 50 $\rm km\,s^{-1}$"]
  - parameter: uvcontsub linear fit, fitorder=1 [2.2.1 Data Flag and Basic Setups of Dat…: "employing the CASA task *uvcontsub* with a linear fitting (fitorder=1)"]
  - validation: First-version data showed skewed baselines for some fields, motivating manual line-free re-selection [2.2.1 Data Flag and Basic Setups of Dat…: "the TM2+ACA first version data reveal a skewed baseline for some fields instead of a constant level"]
- step 3: Make line cubes
  - method: Apply continuum self-cal tables, uvcontsub, then tclean multiscale with a uniform threshold [2.4 Line Imaging: "we applied to four SPWs all the calibration tables from three rounds of continuum self-calibration"] [2.4 Line Imaging: "with the *mutilscale* deconvolution approach and a uniform cleaning threshold of 25$\,\rm mJy\,beam^{-1}$"]
  - software: CASA
  - parameter: cleaning threshold 25 mJy/beam (~0.4 K) for all fields [2.4 Line Imaging: "with the *mutilscale* deconvolution approach and a uniform cleaning threshold of 25$\,\rm mJy\,beam^{-1}$"]
- step 5: Classify cores by evolutionary stage
  - method: Count line peaks above 3 sigma in core-averaged spectra (N_line), combine with CO outflows and H30alpha: starless (N_line<=6, no outflow/H30alpha), warm (6<N_line<=15 or N_line<=6 with outflow, no H30alpha), evolved (HMC, UCHii-h, UCHii-l) [3.2 Classification of cores: "we identified the number of line emission peaks from the core-averaged spectra exceeding a $3\sigma$ noise level across the four SPWs"] [3.2.1 Candidate starless and warm cores: "candidate starless cores are required to only have such cold gas emission of lines with $N_{\rm line} \leq 6$"]
  - software: CASA
  - parameter: 1 sigma = std of line-free channel amplitudes per core (~20 mJy/beam on average) [3.2 Classification of cores: "The $1\sigma$ noise level was calculated as the standard deviation of the amplitudes in the line-free emission channels for each core"]
  - assumption: Candidate starless cores lack outflows (CO) and ionized gas (H30alpha) [3.2.1 Candidate starless and warm cores: "such candidates should lack any star formation signatures, such as outflows traced by CO emission or ionized gas by H30$\alpha$ emission"]
  - validation: Checked starless candidates for broad line wings in H2CO and DCN spectra; none seen [3.2.1 Candidate starless and warm cores: "no broad line-wings were observed in the spectral line profiles (not shown here)"]
- result: Candidate starless cores = 127 (8.1%) ± not stated cores [3.2.1 Candidate starless and warm cores: "127 (8.1%) out of 1562 cores were finally classified as candidate starless cores"]
- result: Candidate warm cores = 971 (62.2%) ± not stated cores [3.2.1 Candidate starless and warm cores: "971 cores (62.2%) were identified as candidate warm cores"]
- result: Candidate evolved cores = 464 (~30%) ± not stated cores [3.2.2 Candidate hot molecular and UCHii…: "the remaining 464 cores ($\sim$30%) were classified as evolved cores"]
- result: Candidate hot molecular cores (HMCs) = 314 ± not stated cores [3.2.2 Candidate hot molecular and UCHii…: "314 candidate HMCs without associated"]
- result: UCHii-h candidate cores = 63 ± not stated cores [3.2.2 Candidate hot molecular and UCHii…: "63 UCHii-*h* candidates with associated"]
- result: UCHii-l candidate cores = 87 ± not stated cores [3.2.2 Candidate hot molecular and UCHii…: "87 UCHii-*l* candidates with associated"]
- result: Average line rms per channel = ~10 ± not stated mJy/beam [2.4 Line Imaging: "the average rms level of the reduced line data is $\sim \rm 10\,mJy\,beam^{-1}$ ($\sim \rm 0.2\,K$) per channel"]
- limitation (authors): A few weak continuum peaks are missed by getsf [3.1 Core extraction: "a few potential sources could be still missing"]
- limitation (authors): Core separations are lower limits owing to projection [3.4 Core Separation: "the measured core separations could represent lower limits due to projection effects"]
- limitation (authors): N_line can be underestimated for line-rich cores [3.2 Classification of cores: "could be underestimated for cores having a large number of complex molecular transitions"]
- not stated (card): Per-field line-free channel lists (the manual Linemarker step cannot be repeated without them); Self-calibration solution intervals, per-round cleaning thresholds, masks and number of iterations per field for the continuum; tclean multiscale scales, continuum cleaning thresholds, niter and spectral-cube channel/velocity gridding for each field; getsf version and configuration beyond min/max size …

### 2020ApJ...904..131N: No Evidence for [C II] Halos or High-velocity Outflows in z ≳ 6 Quasar Host Galaxies (2020; high_z_galaxies, agn_and_quasars; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Do z>~6 quasar host galaxies show extended [C II] / dust halos or high-velocity [C II] outflows, tested with uv-plane stacking of ~1 kpc resolution ALMA data of 27 hosts?
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: uvcontsub on the two spectral windows around [C II], first-order fit (fitorder = 1) allowing a continuum slope [2.2.2 Data products per galaxy: "performed the continuum subtraction using the uvcontsub task on the two spectral windows around the"] [2.2.2 Data products per galaxy: "while allowing for a continuum slope (fitorder = 1)"]
- processing.imaging.line_free_channels: Channels within a total width of 2.5xFWHM around [C II] excluded (on average ~900 km/s); 8xFWHM used as a check for continuum [2.2.2 Data products per galaxy: "the total excluded bandwidth is on average 900"] [2.2.3 Stacking the data in the uv-plane: "this time excluding a much broader bandwidth of"]
- step 2: image each source and cycle, subtract continuum, make [C II] maps
  - method: tclean per source/cycle (Högbom, robust 0.5, 30 MHz channels), uvcontsub continuum subtraction, [C II] intensity map from channels over 1.2xFWHM [2.2.2 Data products per galaxy: "We imaged the calibrated visibilities, using the CASA task tclean, with the Högbom deconvolver, and a channel spacing of 30 MHz"] [2.2.2 Data products per galaxy: "chosen to optimize the signal-to-noise ratio (S/N)"]
  - software: CASA, tclean, uvcontsub
  - parameter: robust = 0.5; clean to 2 sigma within 2 arcsec radius mask [2.2.2 Data products per galaxy: "(robust = 0.5) and cleaned (deconvolved) the maps down to"]
  - parameter: uvcontsub with fitorder = 1, excluding 2.5xFWHM around the line [2.2.2 Data products per galaxy: "while allowing for a continuum slope (fitorder = 1)"]
  - assumption: Gaussian line profile when correcting for the tails beyond 1.2xFWHM (factor 1.19) [3.1.1 Analysis in the image plane: "assuming a Gaussian profile, by multiplying by 1.19"]
  - validation: Integration width of 1.2xFWHM derived analytically to maximise S/N of a Gaussian line [Appendix: Optimizing Gaussian line dete…: "Therefore, the total integration width that optimizes the S/N of a Gaussian line is equal to"]
- step 4: extract aperture spectra and stack them in velocity/sigma_line units
  - method: Residual-scaled aperture flux per channel, Gaussian plus constant fit, spectra rescaled by line width, constant continuum subtracted, stacked without weights [3.2.1 Extracting aperture spectra: "we apply residual scaling on the aperture flux densities obtained from individual channels before a spectrum is extracted"] [3.2.2 Stacking spectra: "it is necessary to rescale the spectra by their respective widths before stacking"]
  - software: CASA
  - parameter: Average sigma_line of 150 km/s [3.2.2 Stacking spectra: "which has an average value of 150"]
  - assumption: Continuum flat within the bandwidth of interest [3.2.2 Stacking spectra: "where we consider the underlying continuum to be flat, within the bandwidth of interest"]
  - fitting: Single Gaussian plus constant [3.2.2 Stacking spectra: "estimated from the fitting of a Gaussian plus constant profile"]
  - validation: Residual after Gaussian subtraction consistent with noise; two different continuum estimation methods mitigate bias [3.2.1 Extracting aperture spectra: "is consistent with pure noise. There is no indication of an additional broad spectral component."] [3.2.2 Stacking spectra: "Having two different methods of continuum estimation largely mitigates any potential bias of subtracting a broad component as continuum."]
- step 5: prepare and image the uv-stacked data
  - method: Split channels from continuum-subtracted visibilities, time-average to 30 s, set ICRS, phase-shift to the [C II] centroid with fixvis, recompute weights with statwt, then tclean (mfs, multi-scale) all sources together; each velocity bin im… [2.2.3 Stacking the data in the uv-plane: "The data was further time-averaged inside 30 s bins to reduce the data volume"] [2.2.3 Stacking the data in the uv-plane: "emission using the task fixvis"]
  - software: CASA, tclean, fixvis, statwt, im.advisechansel, ia.imageconcat
  - parameter: specmode = mfs; multi-scale scales 0, 1, 3, 5, 10 beams; 2 arcsec radius mask to 2 sigma [2.2.3 Stacking the data in the uv-plane: "We imaged the data in the continuum mode (specmode = mfs) and applied the multi-scale deconvolver"]
  - parameter: Velocity bins of fixed 30 km/s or 0.2 sigma_line; channels chosen with im.advisechansel in LSRK, nearest neighbour [2.2.3 Stacking the data in the uv-plane: "The velocity bin was either of fixed size, i.e. 30"] [2.2.3 Stacking the data in the uv-plane: "We used the function im.advisechansel from the CASA toolkit to select the channels of interest in the kinematic local standard of rest frame (LSRK)."]
  - assumption: Recomputed weights comparable across datasets so no single source dominates the average [2.2.3 Stacking the data in the uv-plane: "We manually checked that the ranges of recomputed weights are similar between all datasets"]
  - validation: Anomalous-weight datasets removed; continuum exclusion width tested with a much broader 8xFWHM exclusion [2.2.3 Stacking the data in the uv-plane: "Several anomalous datasets were identified as having (at least) an order of magnitude larger data weights"] [2.2.3 Stacking the data in the uv-plane: "We validated that our choice of excluded frequencies/velocities did not bias the dust continuum results by re-imaging the data, this time excluding a much broader bandwidth of"]
- result: Peak S/N of the stacked aperture [C II] spectrum in 0.2 sigma_line bins = 32 ± not stated dimensionless [4.3 High-velocity outflows: "is detected at a S/N of 32 in velocity bins of"]
- limitation (authors): A very faint outflow below the stack detection limit cannot be ruled out [4.3 High-velocity outflows: "However, we cannot rule out the possibility of a very faint outflow, below our stack detection limit."]
- limitation (authors): Stack is meaningful only if the mean property is well defined; sub-kpc morphology not recoverable [2.2.3 Stacking the data in the uv-plane: "which we cannot know a priori, and deeper observations are necessary to confirm any potential structure in individual objects"]
- limitation (authors): Simulated galaxies and observed quasar hosts are not the same population (core differs) [5.2 Dust emission from the MassiveFIRE …: "Although the simulations and observations do not pertain to the same population of galaxies"]
- not stated (card): Robust/weighting for the uv-stack tclean runs (only given for per-source imaging; figure file names hint at robust weighting); Cell size, image size and primary-beam correction settings for tclean; Per-source aperture radii (chosen manually after visual inspection) and the line-free channels per source; Exact rms used to define the 2 sigma clean threshold and number of tclean iterations; statwt p…

### 2018ApJ...852..122H: CO and Dust Properties in the TW Hya Disk from High-resolution ALMA Observations (2018; protoplanetary_disks, astrochemistry; archival_reuse)
- question: What are the CO and dust properties (CO column density structure, spectral index/grain sizes) in the TW Hya disk at high angular resolution?
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: uvcontsub in visibility plane [$^{12}$CO $J=3-2$ Data Reduction: "The continuum was then subtracted from the line emission in the visibility plane using the `uvcontsub` task."]
- step 1: Reduce and combine archival continuum data
  - method: Calibrate, flag line channels, average line-free channels, phase self-calibrate, align phase centers with fixvis/fixplanets, image Band 6 with MS-MFS [Continuum Reduction: "Because of the relatively large proper motion of TW Hya, the `fixvis` and `fixplanets` tasks in `CASA` were used to shift the datasets to a common phasecenter."]
  - software: CASA
  - parameter: robust=0.5; scales 0, 0.06, 0.15, 0.3, 0.6 arcsec [Continuum Reduction: "Briggs weighting (robust $=0.5$) and scales of $0''$, 0$\ensuremath{''}$06, 0$\ensuremath{''}$15, 0$\ensuremath{''}$3, and 0$\ensuremath{''}$6 were used."]
  - validation: Continuum fluxes of self-calibrated images agree within 5% within each band; compared with published images [Continuum Reduction: "The continuum fluxes from the self-calibrated images were within 5% of one another within each band"]
- step 3: Image 12CO J=3-2 cube and derive moment/peak Tb maps
  - method: Self-calibrate, uvcontsub, mstransform to 0.25 km/s, multi-scale tclean, impbcor; integrated intensity above 3 sigma; peak Tb via Planck law, median of three offset cubes [12CO J=3-2 Peak Brightness Temperatures: "A peak brightness map is computed by taking the maximum value along the frequency axis for each pixel in the image cube and then converting to a brightness temperature using Planck’s law"]
  - software: CASA
  - parameter: integration -1.91 to 7.59 km/s; 3 sigma clip [$^{12}$CO $J=3-2$ Data Reduction: "An integrated intensity map was produced by summing emission above the $3\sigma$ level in the velocity range from $-1.91$ to 7.59 km s$^{-1}$."]
  - validation: Imaged without continuum subtraction; integration limits changed flux <0.1% [$^{12}$CO $J=3-2$ Data Reduction: "truncating or extending the integration range by a few channels changed the integrated flux by less than $0.1\%$"]
- result: 12CO integrated flux (r<4 arcsec) = 42.7 ± 0.2 Jy km/s [12CO J=3-2 Emission Morphology: "The integrated flux, measured inside a circular mask with a radius of $4''$, is 42.7$\pm0.2$ Jy km s$^{-1}$."]
- result: 12CO emission extent (3 sigma) = 3.6 arcsec (215 AU) ± not stated arcsec [12CO J=3-2 Emission Morphology: "$^{12}$CO emission exceeding the $3\sigma$ level extends to a radius of $\approx 3.6\ensuremath{''}$ (215 AU)"]
- result: 12CO/13CO flux ratio in outer annulus (101-214 AU) = ~20 ± not stated dimensionless [Comparison to other CO isotopologues: "Inside this annulus, the $^{12}$CO/$^{13}$CO flux ratio is $\approx20$"]
- result: Peak 12CO brightness temperature 25-90 AU = ~70 to 35 ± not stated K [12CO J= 3-2 Peak Brightness Temperatures: "The peak brightness temperatures between radii of 25 and 90 AU range from $\sim$70 to 35 K"]
- limitation (authors): Models are not optimal; dust modeling and multi-line fitting deferred; perturbed temperature model not physically self-consistent [A “Perturbed Temperature" Model: "this model is not physically self-consistent"]
- limitation (authors): Absolute alpha uncertain by ~0.4 from flux calibration [The Spectral Index Between 1.3 mm and 8…: "A $\sim10\%$ flux uncertainty in each band would correspond to an uncertainty in the overall offset of $\Delta \alpha\sim0.4$"]
- not stated (card): Self-calibration solution intervals and number of iterations; Line-free channel ranges for continuum and uvcontsub; Image size and cell size for clean/tclean; clean thresholds and number of iterations; Exact spectral windows used per program; Procedure for how model parameters were chosen beyond 'some experimentation' (no fit/goodness metric); Radial bin and deprojection details for profiles

### 2025A&A...701A.120P: ALMA survey of a massive node of the Cosmic Web at z ∼ 3: II. A dynamically cold and massive disk galaxy in the proximity of a hyper-luminous quasar (2025; high_z_galaxies, agn_and_quasars; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Characterize the massive rotating disk MQN01-QC near QSO CTS G18.01 and test the origin of the quasar host's blueshifted CO(4–3) emission.
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: imcontsub; zeroth-order polynomial in image domain; 20 arcsec × 20 arcsec rectangular mask. [Reduction and imaging of ALMA data: "Finally, we obtained a continuum-subtracted datacube by employing the CASA task `imcontsub`."] [Reduction and imaging of ALMA data: "we fit with a zeroth-order polynomial and subtract the line-free channels in the image domain within $20\ensuremath{''}\times20\ensuremath{''}$ rectangular mask around the sources."]
- step 1: Image calibrated visibilities and subtract continuum
  - method: tclean with Högbom and natural weighting; inspect dirty cube and continuum, clean detected sources, then image-domain continuum subtraction. [Reduction and imaging of ALMA data: "the CASA task `tclean` employing the Högbom deconvolution algorithm (cite: Hogbom1974), and adopting a “natural” weighting scheme"] [Reduction and imaging of ALMA data: "cleaning down to a threshold of $1.5\sigma$ within two circular masks of $0\rlap{.}{\ensuremath{''}}6$ radius."]
  - software: CASA, tclean, imcontsub
  - parameter: Dirty cube: niter=0, specmode=cube, 25 km s−1 channels; continuum: four SPWs, niter=0, specmode=mfs. [Reduction and imaging of ALMA data: "`niter=0`, `specmode=‘‘cube’’` as a spectral definition mode, and a channel width of $25\,{\rm km\,s^{-1}}$."] [Reduction and imaging of ALMA data: "aggregating all the four SPWs by running `tclean` with `niter=0` in multifrequency synthesis mode (`specmode=‘‘mfs’’`)."]
  - parameter: 0.04 arcsec pixels; zeroth-order continuum fit within a 20 × 20 arcsec mask. [Reduction and imaging of ALMA data: "a pixel size of $0\rlap{.}{\ensuremath{''}}04$"] [Reduction and imaging of ALMA data: "we fit with a zeroth-order polynomial and subtract the line-free channels in the image domain within $20\ensuremath{''}\times20\ensuremath{''}$ rectangular mask around the sources."]
  - fitting: Zeroth-order polynomial for image-domain continuum subtraction. [Reduction and imaging of ALMA data: "we fit with a zeroth-order polynomial"]
  - validation: Dirty-image inspection detected only the two sources; masks encompass observable emission; beam minor axis sampled by ~7 pixels. [Reduction and imaging of ALMA data: "No additional galaxy emission was detected in the field-of-view (FoV)."] [Reduction and imaging of ALMA data: "These were sufficient to encompass the observable emission of both the sources."]
- step 10: Determine companion disk geometry
  - method: CANNUBI forward-models the continuum-subtracted cube with 3DBarolo tilted rings and Bayesian MCMC. [Geometrical parameter derivation: "We run CANNUBI on the continuum-subtracted datacube of MQN01-QC"] [Rotating disk modeling of the MQN01-QC: "The code makes use of $^{\rm 3D}$Barolo to generate mock datacube and total flux maps"]
  - software: CANNUBI, 3DBarolo
  - parameter: NRADII=3; MASK=SMOOTH&SEARCH; FACTOR=1.5; SNRCUT=3; GROWTHCUT=2. [Geometrical parameter derivation: "using three annuli (`NRADII=3`)."] [Geometrical parameter derivation: "`MASK=“SMOOTH&SEARCH”` task adopting `FACTOR=1.5`, and `SNRCUT=3`, `GROWTHCUT=2` as primary and secondary S/N threshold, respectively."]
  - assumption: Razor-thin intrinsically round disk; projected geometry determines inclination. [Rotating disk modeling of the MQN01-QC: "we assume a razor-thin disk model, and therefore that the observed galaxy geometry is entirely due to the projection of an intrinsically round disk on the sky plane."]
  - fitting: Minimize Σ|data−model|; errorType=marasco likelihood; posterior estimates of geometry. [Geometrical parameter derivation: "minimizing the sum of $\left | {\rm data}-{\rm model} \right |$"] [Geometrical parameter derivation: "We set `errorType=“marasco”`, which calculates the likelihood taking into account the asymmetries in the total intensity map (cite: Marasco+2019)."]
  - validation: Three annuli cover the emitting region; posterior distributions and model/data overlays shown. [Geometrical parameter derivation: "These are sufficient to cover the entire emitting line region"] [Geometrical parameter derivation: "we show the best fit model overlaid with the data."]
- step 11: Fit companion rotation and dispersion
  - method: Fit continuum-subtracted cube with PSF-convolved 3DBarolo tilted-ring models; fix CANNUBI geometry and free VROT and VDISP; calculate rotation-to-dispersion ratio. [Kinematical parameter derivation: "We run $^{\rm 3D}$Barolo on the continuum-subtracted datacube of MQN01-QC."] [Kinematical parameter derivation: "we left the rotational velocity (`VROT`), and the velocity dispersion (`VDISP`) as free parameters."]
  - software: 3DBarolo
  - parameter: Same mask; three annuli; fixed radial separation, inclination and PA from CANNUBI; systemic z=3.2475. [Kinematical parameter derivation: "the same mask parameters and three annuli with fixed radial separation, inclination, and position angle corresponding to the best-fit values as derived from the morphological fit with CANNUBI"] [Kinematical parameter derivation: "We set the systemic velocity at the CO(4–3) line redshift of $z=3.2475$"]
  - parameter: NORM=AZIM; WFUNC=2 (cos²φ weighting). [Kinematical parameter derivation: "we employed an axisymmetric disk normalization by setting `NORM=“AZIM”`."] [Kinematical parameter derivation: "employing $\cos^{2}\phi$ as weighting function `WFUNC=2`."]
  - assumption: Negligible radial velocity, Vrad=0; azimuthally averaged ring surface density. [Kinematical parameter derivation: "We also assumed negligible radial velocity ($V_{\rm rad}=0$)"] [Kinematical parameter derivation: "the surface density of each ring is computed directly from the observed flux map using an azimuthal average."]
  - fitting: Minimize absolute data–model differences with cos²φ weighting toward the major axis. [Kinematical parameter derivation: "we minimized the absolute difference between the data and the model employing $\cos^{2}\phi$ as weighting function `WFUNC=2`."]
  - validation: Compare integrated line profile, major/minor-axis PVDs and channel maps; major-axis PVD well reproduced; dispersion supported by minor-axis extent. [Kinematical parameter derivation: "The PVD along the major axis is well reproduced by our rotating disk model"] [Kinematical parameter derivation: "The vertical extend of the emission is captured by our model, which indicates that the velocity dispersion is robustly determined."]
- result: MQN01-QC disk radial extent = 4.1 ± ±0.4 kpc [Table 2: "$4.1\pm0.4\,{\rm kpc}$"]
- result: MQN01-QC ring width = 0.179 ± +0.018/-0.016 arcsec [Table 2: "| Ring Width$\,^{(2)}$ (arcsec) | $0.179^{+0.018}_{-0.016}$ |"]
- result: MQN01-QC disk inclination = 40 ± +10/-14 deg [Table 2: "| $i$ (deg) | $40^{+10}_{-14}$ |"]
- result: MQN01-QC disk position angle = 193 ± +19/-16 deg [Table 2: "| PA (deg) | $193^{+19}_{-16}$ |"]
- result: MQN01-QC 3DBarolo maximum rotation velocity = 513 ± +58/-64 km s−1 [Table 2: "$V_{\rm rot, max}$$\,^{(3)}$ (${\rm km\,s^{-1}}$) | $513^{+58}_{-64}$"]
- result: MQN01-QC 3DBarolo radially averaged velocity dispersion = 46 ± +12/-12 km s−1 [Table 2: "| $\bar{\sigma}$$\,^{(4)}$ (${\rm km\,s^{-1}}$) | $46^{+12}_{-12}$ |"]
- result: MQN01-QC 3DBarolo rotation-to-dispersion ratio = 11 ± +4/-3 dimensionless [Kinematical parameter derivation: "$V_{\rm rot, max}/\bar{\sigma}=11^{+4}_{-3}$."]
- limitation (authors): Gas-mass uncertainties omit conversion-factor systematics. [Molecular gas mass and dust characteris…: "the reported uncertainties on molecular gas masses do not take into account systematic uncertainties on the assumed conversion factors."]
- limitation (authors): Stellar mass has quasar-subtraction and limited-filter systematics; quoted aperture-flux errors exclude potential systematic errors. [Stellar mass estimation of MQN01-QC fro…: "considering the photometric systematic uncertainties originating from the quasar light subtraction and the lack of flux measurements from other filters."]
- not stated (card): Exact line-free channel ranges for continuum imaging and imcontsub.; Complete tclean calls, including image dimensions, spectral frame and velocity convention, clean iteration limit, and mask centers.; Explicit final cleaned-cube spectral grid; 25 km s−1 is specified for the initial dirty cube.; Detailed band 6 mosaic acquisition, calibration and imaging recipe deferred to Paper I.; Noise-estimat…

### 2018PASJ...70...36I: Subaru High-z Exploration of Low-Luminosity Quasars (SHELLQs). III. Star formation properties of the host galaxies at z ≳ 6 studied with ALMA (2018; high_z_galaxies, agn_and_quasars; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What are the star formation properties, sizes, dynamical masses and black hole-host mass relation of low-luminosity (M1450 > -25) quasar hosts at z >~ 6, compared with optically luminous quasars?
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: subtracted in the uv plane; continuum from all line-free channels (~7.5 GHz) [2 Observations and data reduction: "the continuum emission was subtracted in the ($u$, $v$) plane before making the line cube"]
- step 1: Image cubes and continuum
  - method: Calibrate and clean with CASA; uv continuum subtraction; bin to 50 km/s [2 Observations and data reduction: "All images were reconstructed with the CASA task `clean` (gain = 0.1, weighting = briggs, robust = 0.5)."]
  - software: CASA
  - parameter: briggs robust 0.5; 0.1 arcsec pixels [2 Observations and data reduction: "The pixel scale of all maps in this paper is set to 0$\ensuremath{''}$.1."]
- step 2: Extract [CII] line properties
  - method: Single Gaussian fit of continuum-subtracted spectrum in 1.5 arcsec aperture; L_[CII] via standard equation [3.1: "We fit each continuum-subtracted spectrum with a single Gaussian profile to extract the redshift"]
  - software: CASA
  - parameter: 1.5 arcsec diameter circular aperture [3 Results: "we decided to measure the rest-frame FIR properties with a common 1$\ensuremath{''}$.5 diameter circular aperture."]
  - assumption: H0=70, OmegaM=0.3, OmegaL=0.7 [1 Introduction: "$H_0$ = 70 km s$^{-1}$ Mpc$^{-1}$, $\Omega_{\rm M}$ = 0.3, and $\Omega_{\rm \Lambda}$ = 0.7"]
  - fitting: single Gaussian; double Gaussian tested for J2216-0016 (reduced chi2 1.05 vs 1.24) [3.5: "the resultant reduced $\chi^2$ is 1.05 (degree of freedom [^6], d.o.f. = 14), which is improved over the single-Gaussian fit"]
- step 6: Search for companion emitters
  - method: CLUMPFIND on continuum-subtracted cubes, peak S/N>=5; continuum emitters at S/N>=5 [3.4.1: "We used the `CLUMPFIND` software (cite: 1994ApJ...428..693W) to search for line emitters other than the HSC quasars themselves with a peak $S/N \geq 5$."]
  - software: CLUMPFIND
  - parameter: Delta S = 1 sigma, S_start = 3 sigma; 43 MHz binning [3.4.1: "The relevant parameters were $\Delta~S = 1\sigma$ and $S_{\rm start} = 3\sigma$"]
  - validation: Rejected candidates without S/N>=3 in adjacent channels [3.4.1: "we rejected candidates that do not show $S/N \geq 3$ emission in any of the channels adjacent to the peak ones"]
- result: L_[CII] range = (3.8-10.2)e8 ± not stated Lsun [abstract: "$L_{\rm [C\,\emissiontype{II}]} = (3.8-10.2) \times 10^8~L_\odot$"]
- result: J2216-0016 [CII] flux = 1.05 ± 0.08 Jy km/s [Table 2: "1.05 $\pm$ 0.08"]
- result: [CII] FWHM range = 192-356 ± not stated km/s [6 Summary: "The line FWHM ranges from 192 to 356 km s$^{-1}$"]
- result: 1.2 mm continuum flux density = 136-246 ± not stated uJy [Table 2: "$f_{\rm 1.2mm}$ ($\mu$Jy) | 157 $\pm$ 23 | 189 $\pm$ 32 | 136 $\pm$ 27 | 246 $\pm$ 12"]
- limitation (authors): Mdyn has large unconstrained systematic uncertainty; sources only marginally resolved [4.3: "Keeping the existence of such large *unconstrained* systematic uncertainty in mind"]
- not stated (card): Line-free channel selection and velocity ranges for moment-0 maps; Clean mask, threshold and iteration settings; image size; CASA task parameters for imfit (regions, initial guesses); Dust-temperature SED details for the gray body integration and L_FIR band definition; Gas-to-dust ratio value used; Details of nu2GC selection and M_BH from Onoue et al.; Which archive products or scripts were used …

### 2023A&A...678A.194C: ALMA-IMF. VII. First release of the full spectral line cubes: Core kinematics traced by DCN J = (3‒2) (2023; star_formation, astrochemistry, milky_way_ism; large_program_or_survey)
- question: How to homogeneously calibrate and image the 12m-array spectral line cubes for the 15 ALMA-IMF protoclusters, and what are the global and core-scale kinematics traced by DCN (3-2) across different evolutionary stages?
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: Continuum startmodel (from .tt0 and .tt1 cleanest continuum products) subtracted in visibilities at start of tclean; optional image-plane continuum subtraction using STATCONT [Continuum start-model: "This model cube is constructed from the .tt0 and .tt1 products of the mostly line-free `cleanest` continuum imaging (see (cite: Ginsburg2022)), `tclean` then stores this as an initial model and subtracts it from the visibilities at the beginning of the deconvolution process."] [ALMA-IMF data pipeline: "The optional continuum subtraction on the line cubes described in Step 8 is performed in the image plane using the `statcont` procedure, as described in (cite: statcont2017)."]
- step 1: Calibrate, image, and post-process the 12m spectral line cubes
  - method: Restore QA3-reprocessed measurement sets with scriptForPI.py, split spectral windows and combine 12m configurations, build continuum startmodel from cleanest continuum (.tt0 and .tt1), deconvolve line cubes with multi-scale tclean using a … [ALMA-IMF data pipeline: "The custom ALMA-IMF data pipeline developed to produce the calibrated and imaged continuum data (as described in (cite: Ginsburg2022)) was subsequently adapted to process the full spectral line windows."] [JvM correction: "The JvM correction rescales the residuals to the same units (Jy per clean beam) by multiplying them by the $\epsilon$ factor before the final image restoration."]
  - software: CASA, tclean, astroquery, radio-beam, STATCONT
  - parameter: pbmask = 10% of primary beam response [Cleaning optimisation and masking: "For these reasons, we opted to use the primary beam mask (pbmask) as our `tclean` mask, setting its limit to 10% of the primary beam response."]
  - parameter: Cleaning threshold = 5 sigma (10 sigma for G351.77) [Cleaning threshold: "We found that using a relatively shallow threshold of 5$\sigma$ resulted in the most stable outcomes, that is, mitigating the effects of divergence and ‘stippling’ over the full dataset."] [Cleaning threshold: "In the end, for this source, we ran `tclean` only down to 10$\sigma$ level for all spectral windows."]
  - parameter: Multi-scale scales = geometric series from 0 increasing by ~x2 minor beam/cell size up to ~5.5'' [Cleaning optimisation and masking: "We defined the scales used in multi-scale `tclean` using a geometric series starting at 0 and increasing by $\sim \times 2$ the ratio of the minor beam axis to the cell size until a scale of $\sim$5.5$''$ is reached."]
  - assumption: Dirty beam volume is integrated up to its first null to compute JvM epsilon factor [JvM correction: "The dirty beam is only considered up to its first null since this is the part approximated to a Gaussian during deconvolution and because the volume of a dirty beam without zero spacing integrated over its full domain is formally zero."]
  - validation: Internal quality assessments of tclean products testing masking methods, deconvolvers, and thresholds to prevent stippling and divergence [Cleaning optimisation and masking: "We performed several iterations of `tclean` using the Hogbom and multi-scale deconvolvers and tested methods to mask the line emission: 1) an internally developed auto-masking, 2) the CASA auto multi-threshold masking, and 3) a primary beam mask."]
- step 2: Estimate cube noise levels homogeneously across all fields and spectral windows
  - method: Calculate MAD (scaled by 1.4826) on the 25% of channels with the lowest intensity within manually defined continuum noise regions on JvM-corrected, non-continuum-subtracted, non-primary-beam-corrected cubes, then scale by 1/epsilon to conv… [Noise estimation: "To estimate noise levels, we use the median-absolute-deviation (MAD) estimator and take a threshold cut in intensity so that the noise is estimated using only the 25% of the channels with the lowest intensity across the full spectral window, and using the manually defined regions which were created for the continuum e…"] [JvM correction: "The most conservative approach to obtaining the noise, which we adopt here, is to estimate the noise from emission and line-free regions in the JvM cubes (in units of Jy per clean beam) and scale by 1/$\epsilon$ factor (see 3.5), thus, taking the higher noise estimates in units of Jy per dirty beam."]
  - software: astropy.stats.median_abs_deviation
  - parameter: 25% lowest intensity channels; MAD scale factor = 1.4826 [Noise estimation: "To estimate noise levels, we use the median-absolute-deviation (MAD) estimator and take a threshold cut in intensity so that the noise is estimated using only the 25% of the channels with the lowest intensity across the full spectral window, and using the manually defined regions which were created for the continuum e…"] [Footnotes: "Implemented in `astropy.stats.median_abs_deviation` and we scale this by 1.4826 such that the reported value is equivalent to the standard deviation if the underlying data are normally distributed."]
  - assumption: Noise estimated from a smaller clean beam should be scaled up by the dirty-to-clean beam volume ratio (1/epsilon) [JvM correction: "In other words, we assume the noise estimated from a smaller clean beam should be larger by the ratio of the dirty and clean beam areas."]
  - validation: Compared achieved noise against theoretical proposal noise scaled to the same spectral resolution, beam size, and central frequency [Noise estimation: "we scaled the requested noise to the same spectral resolution, beam size, and central frequency as those obtained in the observed data cubes for all spectral windows."]
- step 3: Create global DCN (3-2) moment maps
  - method: Shift B6 spw1 STATCONT continuum-subtracted, non-primary-beam-corrected cubes to DCN (3-2) rest frame, extract a 50 km s^-1 subcube around protocluster V_LSR, and compute moment 0, 1, and 2 maps over manually determined velocity ranges usi… [Global scale DCN (3-2) morphology: "We then extract a 50 km s$^{-1}$ wide subset of channels around the $V_{\rm LSR}$ reported in (cite: Motte2022) and create the first three moment maps towards the 15 protoclusters, using the non-primary beam corrected cubes."] [Global scale DCN (3-2) morphology: "The DCN (3-2) emission is integrated over a manually determined velocity range for each protocluster, using a 4$\sigma$ threshold to identify channels where DCN (3-2) emission is present over an area larger than the beam size (the velocity ranges used for each protocluster are provided in the figure captions)."]
  - software: spectral-cube
  - parameter: Subcube width = 50 km s^-1; pixel and channel threshold = 4 sigma (dirty beam noise); rest frequency = 217.23854 GHz [Global scale DCN (3-2) morphology: "DCN (3-2) has a rest frequency of 217.23854 GHz (cite: muller2001; muller2005) and is located in B6, spectral window 1, with a spectral resolution of 0.34 km s$^{-1}$."] [Global scale DCN (3-2) morphology: "Furthermore, for the first and second-moment maps, we take an additional threshold cut per channel and include only pixels that contain emission $>$4$\sigma$, preventing noisy pixels from being added into them."]
- step 4: Extract core-averaged background-subtracted DCN (3-2) spectra and fit hyperfine structure
  - method: Extract average DCN (3-2) spectrum for each of the 595 continuum cores from an elliptical aperture of 2x the continuum FWHM, subtract background spectrum from an elliptical annulus (2x to 3x continuum FWHM, excluding overlapping neighbour … [DCN (3-2) line extraction and fitting: "We use an elliptical aperture with a major (minor) axis length twice that of the continuum source major (minor) FWHM to extract the spectrum, where the continuum core sizes are taken from (cite: louvet22)."] [DCN (3-2) line extraction and fitting: "We extract the background emission from an elliptical annulus with an inner major (minor) axis size equal to the size of the spectral aperture (i.e. twice the continuum source major (minor) FWHM) and outer radius size 1.5x larger (i.e. three times the continuum source major (minor) FWHM)."]
  - software: spectral-cube, PySpecKit
  - parameter: Core aperture = 2x continuum FWHM; background annulus = 2x to 3x continuum FWHM; detection criteria: S/N > 4 sigma (from MAD in 30 line-free channels) and velocity dispersion > 0.2 km s^-1; 7 hyperfine components from CDMS [DCN (3-2) detection of cores: "A core is determined to have a DCN (3-2) detection if a velocity dispersion of $>$0.2 km s$^{-1}$ and a signal-to-noise ratio (S/N) $>$4$\sigma$ are found in the core averaged, background-subtracted spectrum."] [DCN (3-2) detection of cores: "The noise in the spectrum is estimated from the MAD in 30 continuous channels ($\sim$10 km s$^{-1}$) from either the lower or upper part of the spectrum that were identified by eye to be in a line free part of the spectrum."]
  - assumption: DCN (3-2) is an optically thin dense gas tracer with critical density ~10^7 cm^-3; FWHM linewidth = sigma_obs * sqrt(8 ln 2) [Analysis: "We utilise the molecular transition DCN (3-2) as a proxy for the gas associated with the core as it is typically an optically thin tracer with a critical density of $\sim$10$^7$ cm$^{-3}$."] [DCN (3-2) detection of cores: "The linewidths are taken as $\sigma_{obs}\times\sqrt{8ln2}$, where $\sigma_{obs}$ is the observed velocity dispersion from single component HFS fits to the core-averaged, background-subtracted spectra."]
  - fitting: Single-component hyperfine structure (HFS) model fit in PySpecKit; single-component Gaussian fit in PySpecKit for comparison lines (DCO+, OC33S, C18O, OCS, 13CS, N2D+) in G338.93 [DCN (3-2) line extraction and fitting: "The resulting core-average, background-subtracted spectrum for each core is then fitted using a single component hyperfine structure model (HFS) adapted for the DCN (3-2) transition in `PySpecKit` (cite: Ginsburg2011)."] [Comparison with other molecular lines: "They are fitted and classified as Single- or Complex-type spectra in the same way as for DCN (3-2) (described in 4.3), but using only a single component Gaussian fit in pyspeckit."]
  - validation: Visual inspection of all fitted spectra to separate Single- and Complex-type spectra; repeated extraction and fitting without background subtraction to test for background-subtraction bias [DCN (3-2) detection of cores: "This is done manually by visually inspecting all fitted DCN (3-2) spectra."] [DCN (3-2) detection of cores: "To assess if the background subtraction could bias the results, we also perform an extraction on the non-background subtracted DCN (3-2) spectra towards each core in 11."]
- result: Mean JvM epsilon factor across all spectral windows and fields = 0.62 ± 0.17 dimensionless [JvM correction: "The mean, median and standard deviation for $\epsilon$ over all spectral windows and fields are 0.62, 0.60, and 0.17, respectively."]
- result: Median JvM epsilon factor across all spectral windows and fields = 0.60 ± 0.17 dimensionless [JvM correction: "The mean, median and standard deviation for $\epsilon$ over all spectral windows and fields are 0.62, 0.60, and 0.17, respectively."]
- result: Average ratio of line beam to continuum beam (excluding B3 spw0) = 1.2 ± not stated dimensionless [Beam sizes: "The average ratio of the line beams and continuum beam is $\sim$1.2, except for B3, spectral window 0 (which includes the N$_2$H$^+$ transition), where the ratio is on average $\sim$1.4."]
- result: Total continuum cores with DCN (3-2) detection (Single + Complex) = 357 ± not stated cores (60% of 595) [DCN (3-2) detection of cores: "Of the 595 continuum cores, 357 (60%) have a DCN (3-2) detection."]
- result: Total continuum cores with Single-type DCN (3-2) detection = 266 ± not stated cores (~45% of 595) [DCN (3-2) detection of cores: "After filtering out the cores with a Complex DCN (3-2) spectrum, the sample is reduced to 266 continuum cores, that is, $\sim$45% of the continuum cores have a single component HFS fit."]
- result: Average Single-type DCN (3-2) core detection rate in Young protoclusters = 35 ± not stated % [Global scale DCN (3-2) morphology: "| Average | 36 | 12 (19) | 35 (53)% | 5.2 | | 1.9 | 1.5 | 1.3 | 0.76 | | | |"]
- result: Average Single-type DCN (3-2) core detection rate in Intermediate protoclusters = 30 ± not stated % [Global scale DCN (3-2) morphology: "| Average | 28 | 9 (13) | 30 (45)% | 7.4 | | 2.9 | 1.5 | 1.3 | 0.64 | | | |"]
- result: Average Single-type DCN (3-2) core detection rate in Evolved protoclusters = 62 ± not stated % [Global scale DCN (3-2) morphology: "| Average | 59 | 37 (44) | 62 (75)% | 10.4 | | 2.3 | 1.2 | 1.1 | 0.51 | | | |"]
- limitation (authors): DR1 includes only 12m-array data without 7m or TP short spacings, causing negative bowls and absorption features; Band 3 spw0 (N2H+) is excluded from DR1 due to extended emission artifacts. [Data products: "For spectral window 0 in B3, which includes the N$_2$H$^+$ line, the 12 m only data still contain artefacts due to the extended emission and missing short spacings, we thus exclude this window from this DR1 release."]
- limitation (authors): Divergent channels around the V_LSR of 12CO (2-1) and C18O (2-1) were masked and left uncleaned in the final cubes, and G351.77 was cleaned only to 10 sigma. [Cleaning threshold: "In these cases, we chose to mask the channels that showed divergence and re-run the `tclean`, leaving those channels uncleaned in the final cubes (the masked channel ranges can be obtained in the `imaging_parameters.py` file)."]
- limitation (authors): Overestimation of the continuum startmodel at bright hot cores with line forests causes localised negative residuals, and STATCONT continuum subtraction can be imperfect towards hot cores or bright outflows. [Released line cubes and post-processed …: "The `STATCONT` procedure employed simultaneously over the full spectral window can satisfactorily remove the continuum for most of the bandwidth and fields, however, towards, the positions of hot core candidates or bright outflows, the continuum subtraction could be improved."]
- not stated (card): Exact CASA and pipeline versions used for measurement set restoration and line cube imaging; Cell sizes, image dimensions, weighting scheme/robust parameter for the line cubes, and exact masked channel ranges (deferred to the external imaging_parameters.py file on GitHub); Exact manually determined velocity integration ranges for the DCN (3-2) moment maps of each of the 15 protoclusters (paper st…

### 2025ApJ...989...30Z: SO Emission in the Dynamically Perturbed Protoplanetary Disks around CQ Tau and MWC 758 (2025; protoplanetary_disks, astrochemistry; archival_reuse)
- question: Is SO emission in the spiral-hosting, dynamically perturbed disks of CQ Tau and MWC 758 detected, where does it arise, and is it linked to dynamical perturbations?
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: CASA uvcontsub, solint=1, fitorder=1, excluding the line-flagged channels [Self-calibration: "using the CASA `v6.2.1-7` implementation of the `uvcontsub` task, with `solint=1`, `fitorder=1`, and excluding the same channels used to create the pseudo-continuum datasets"]
- processing.imaging.line_free_channels: Channels within +/-15 km/s of systemic velocity around 12CO, 13CO, C18O, DCN, SO and H2CO lines flagged [Self-calibration: "we created pseudo-continuum datasets flagging channels within $\pm15$ km s$^{-1}$ of the systematic velocity"]
- step 1: Self-calibrate archival EBs and build continuum-subtracted datasets
  - method: Pseudo-continuum from line-flagged data, per-EB phase-only self-cal, EB alignment, iterative phase-only self-cal (short then combined baselines), amplitude rescaling, two amplitude+phase cycles, apply to full data, bin 30 s, uvcontsub [Self-calibration: "Self-calibration was performed iteratively, first on the short-baseline observations and then on the combined short- and long-baseline EBs"] [Self-calibration: "time-binned the self-calibrated measurement sets by 30 s to reduce the data volume"]
  - software: CASA, tclean, statwt, gaincal, uvcontsub
  - parameter: Pseudo-continuum made by flagging +/-15 km/s around 12CO, 13CO, C18O, DCN, SO, H2CO lines [Self-calibration: "we created pseudo-continuum datasets flagging channels within $\pm15$ km s$^{-1}$ of the systematic velocity"]
  - parameter: Imaging of pseudo-continuum: multiscale, briggs robust=0.5, smallscalebias=0.6, cycleniter=300, gain=0.3 [Self-calibration: "(`smallscalebias=0.6`), `cycleniter=300`, `gain=0.3`, and a `briggs` weighting scheme with `robust=0.5`"]
  - parameter: First phase-only round: infinite solint, antennas with <3 baselines and SNR<4 rejected [Self-calibration: "rejecting those gain solutions corresponding to antennas with $<3$ baselines and ${\rm SNR}<4$"]
  - parameter: Amplitude+phase cycles: infinite solint, SNR<5 solutions flagged, amplitude gains >20% flagged [Self-calibration: "Amplitude gains $>20\%$ were also flagged"]
  - validation: Peak SNR improvement after each step and ratio images between EBs [Self-calibration: "For CQ Tau, the peak SNR increased from 112.77 to 151.32 ($\approx34\%$) after self-calibration of the short-baseline data"]
- step 3: Image SO line cubes
  - method: tclean cube imaging of continuum-subtracted data, multiscale, natural weighting with uv-taper, Keplerian or continuum mask [Imaging and summary plots: "For SO, the continuum-subtracted measurement sets were imaged using `tclean`"]
  - software: CASA, tclean, keplerian_mask
  - parameter: CQ Tau: 23 channels of 0.7 km/s; MWC 758: 7 channels of 1.4 km/s [Imaging and summary plots: "23 channels of 0.7 km s$^{-1}$ (corresponding to absolute velocities from $-1.3$ to 13.9 km s$^{-1}$ in the LSRK reference frame) for CQ Tau"] [Imaging and summary plots: "7 channels of 1.4 km s$^{-1}$ (corresponding to absolute velocities from 1.7 to 10.1 km s$^{-1}$ in the LSRK reference frame) for MWC 758"]
  - parameter: gain=0.01, 1 sigma threshold [Imaging and summary plots: "a conservative `gain=0.01`"]
  - parameter: natural weighting with Gaussian uv-taper 0.05 arcsec (CQ Tau) and 0.25 arcsec (MWC 758) [Imaging and summary plots: "a Gaussian $uv$-taper with $0.05\ensuremath{''}$ (CQ Tau) and $0.25\ensuremath{''}$ (MWC 758) full width at half maximum"]
  - validation: Different weighting, robust and uv-taper choices were tested; continuum and Keplerian masks compared [Imaging and summary plots: "Different weighting schemes, `robust` parameters, and Gaussian $uv$-tapers were tested."]
- step 4: Detect SO and measure line fluxes by shift-and-stack
  - method: gofish extraction of spectra from circular apertures, shifted to a common line centre assuming Keplerian rotation, stacked, and integrated [SO emission: "These line intensities were measured by integrating the shifted and stacked spectra extracted from a circular mask with a radius of $0.33\ensuremath{''}$ (CQ Tau) and $0.80\ensuremath{''}$ (MWC 758)."]
  - software: gofish
  - parameter: Aperture radius set where the cumulative SO emission flattens: 0.33 arcsec (CQ Tau), 0.80 arcsec (MWC 758) [SO emission: "These apertures correspond to the disk radius where the cumulative distribution of the SO emission"]
  - parameter: Cubes over >200 km/s velocity range to estimate background noise in line-free channels [Imaging and summary plots: "across a much wider velocity range ($\gtrsim200$ km s$^{-1}$), essential to accurately determine the background noise in line free channels"]
  - assumption: Keplerian velocity field used to shift spectra [Imaging and summary plots: "under the assumption of a Keplerian velocity field, corrects for the known disk rotation by shifting such spectra to a common line center"]
  - validation: Flux uncertainty from standard deviation of integrals in equally wide line-free spectral ranges [SO emission: "The flux uncertainties, instead, were estimated as the standard deviation of the fluxes measured by integrating the shifted and stacked spectra in non-overlapping spectral ranges"]
- result: SO J_N=6_5-5_4 integrated flux, CQ Tau = 72.4 ± +/-14.6 (4.9 sigma) mJy km/s [SO emission: "with fluxes of $72.4\pm14.6$ ($4.9\sigma$ detection) and $31.4\pm9.3$ ($3.4\sigma$ detection) mJy km s$^{-1}$, respectively"]
- result: SO J_N=6_5-5_4 integrated flux, MWC 758 = 31.4 ± +/-9.3 (3.4 sigma) mJy km/s [SO emission: "with fluxes of $72.4\pm14.6$ ($4.9\sigma$ detection) and $31.4\pm9.3$ ($3.4\sigma$ detection) mJy km s$^{-1}$, respectively"]
- limitation (authors): Lower-amplitude velocity perturbations cannot be excluded at the available spectral resolution [SO emission: "However, we cannot exclude lower-amplitude velocity perturbations"]
- limitation (authors): Sample too small and ages uncertain for a definitive correlation conclusion [Comparison with the other SO-bearing Cl…: "and our sample size is too small to draw a definitive conclusion"]
- limitation (authors): Hypothesis based on SO plus perturbations in a small sample dominated by bright early-type stars [Spirals and companions: "However, we stress that our hypothesis is based on the concurrent presence of SO emission and dynamical perturbations in a small sample with a significant fraction of bright early type stars"]
- not stated (card): Exact pixel size and image size (number of pixels) for continuum and cube imaging; Systemic-velocity-centred line-free channel ranges for each EB beyond the +/-15 km/s flagging rule; Exact CASA tclean threshold values and niter per image, and the continuum mask used for each cube; The adopted Keplerian mask geometry beyond Table A3 (e.g. emission surface height, mask width); Which gofish options …

### 2020ApJ...905...89Y: Kinematical Signs of Dust Trapping and Feedback in a Local Pressure Bump in the Protoplanetary Disk around HD 142527 Revealed with ALMA (2020; protoplanetary_disks; archival_reuse)
- question: Does the gas kinematics (deviation from Keplerian rotation) in the HD 142527 disk reveal a local pressure bump, and how does it relate to the dust density, gas-to-dust mass ratio and dust beta (dust trapping and feedback)?
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: Continuum subtracted from the line data in the uv domain; line-free channels not stated [Data: "and the continuum was subtracted from the molecular-line data in the *uv* domain."]
- step 1: Calibrate, self-calibrate, combine, image and align the archival data
  - method: Calibrated raw archive data with ALMA scripts or pipelines, phase self-calibration on continuum, uv-domain continuum subtraction for lines, combined data of the same line or band, Briggs imaging, convolution to a 0.45 arcsec beam, and alig… [Data: "We calibrated the raw visibility data obtained from the archive using the software Common Astronomy Software Applications (cite: McMullin07) with the calibration scripts or pipelines provided by the ALMA observatory."] [Data: "Then we aligned the images obtained in different frequency bands by centering these imaging at the continuum peak positions measured in the corresponding frequency bands."]
  - software: CASA
  - parameter: Briggs weighting with robust values per image in Table 2 (0, -0.5, 2); final circular beam 0.45 arcsec [Data: "All the images were generated with the Briggs weighting."] [Appendix: Continuum and molecular-line …: "All the images were convolved to a circular beam with a size of 0.45$''$."]
  - assumption: Missing flux is not an issue because maximum recoverable scales (3.4 to 18.7 arcsec) exceed the disk diameter [Data: "Thus, we expect that our results and joint analysis are not affected by the effects of missing fluxes."]
- step 4: Estimate gas surface density from the C18O (1-0, 2-1, 3-2) spectra
  - method: At each position, LTE radiative transfer through a vertical temperature and hydrostatic density structure plus a thin midplane dust layer, fitted to the three C18O spectra [3.2 Gas distribution: "we computed the radiative transfer equation assuming the local thermal equilibrium (LTE) condition, integrated the equation along the line of sight,"] [3.2 Gas distribution: "generated model spectra of the C$^{18}$O (3–2), (2–1), and (1–0) lines to fit the observations for every position in the HD 142527 disk."]
  - parameter: Vertical temperature profile exponent n = 2 [3.2 Gas distribution: "Here we adopted $n=2$."]
  - parameter: Midplane temperature Tm = dust temperature from the SED fit [3.2 Gas distribution: "In our calculations, $T_m$ was adopted to the dust temperature."]
  - parameter: Atmosphere temperature Ta = peak brightness temperature of 13CO (3-2) without continuum subtraction [3.2 Gas distribution: "$T_{\rm a}$ was adopted to be the peak brightness temperature of $^{13}$CO (3–2) without continuum subtraction"]
  - parameter: Za = one pressure scale height, with stellar mass 2.48 Msun [3.2 Gas distribution: "computed with the midplane temperature and the stellar mass of 2.48 $M_\odot$"] [footnote 3: "adopting $Z_{\rm a}$ to be one pressure scale height provides better fits to the observed spectra."]
  - parameter: Model centroid velocity and line width set to the mean of Gaussian fits to the three observed C18O lines [3.2 Gas distribution: "the centroid velocity and line width of the model spectra were adopted to be the mean centroid velocity and line width of the three C$^{18}$O lines"]
  - assumption: C18O abundance 2e-7, zero where temperature is below 20 K (CO freeze-out) [3.2 Gas distribution: "The C$^{18}$O abundance was assumed to be a constant of $2\times10^{-7}$ (cite: Wilson94; Jorgensen04), and it was set to be zero in the regions where the temperature is below 20 K"]
  - assumption: Dust settled to the midplane and thermally coupled with gas, so Td represents the midplane temperature [3.2 Gas distribution: "Assuming the dust grains have settled to the midplane and are thermally coupled with gas in the HD 142527 disk"]
  - assumption: Disk treated as face-on (no inclination correction in the radiative transfer), inclination 26 deg [3.2 Gas distribution: "We did not consider that the line of sight is inclined with respect to the disk midplane in our radiative transfer calculation"]
  - validation: Model fluxes typically match observations within 3 sigma or within 20% [3.2 Gas distribution: "The fluxes of our model spectra typically match with the observations within the 3$\sigma$ uncertainties, or the difference is less than 20%."]
- limitation (authors): Absolute gas-to-dust ratios are not interpreted because of uncertain C18O abundance and dust absorption coefficient [3.3 Gas-to-dust mass ratio: "we do not discuss the absolute values of the estimated gas-to-dust mass ratio"]
- limitation (authors): Dust scattering not modelled in the SED fit [3.1 Dust distribution: "The effects of dust scattering were not considered in our simple SED fitting."]
- limitation (authors): Velocity features within the 0.45 arcsec beam are uncertain [5.1 Deviation from Keplerian rotation: "any velocity feature within a radius of 0.45$''$ is uncertain due to the beam convolution"]
- not stated (card): Array type (12 m vs 7 m) and configurations of each project, and which execution blocks or MOUS were used; CASA version and ALMA pipeline version, and which projects used the delivered scripts versus the pipeline; flux, bandpass and phase calibrators; Self-calibration details: solution intervals, number of iterations, reference antenna, which datasets were self-calibrated and the SNR criteria; Li…

### 2023A&A...673A..46B: Another X-ray UFO without a momentum-boosted molecular outflow. ALMA CO(1-0) observations of the galaxy pair IRAS 05054+1718 (2023; nearby_galaxies, agn_and_quasars; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Investigate the presence of a large-scale molecular outflow in the galaxy pair IRAS 05054+1718 (NED01 and NED02) and test the AGN feedback blast-wave scenario by comparing molecular outflow and X-ray ultra-fast outflow energetics.
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: uvcontsub with zeroth-order polynomial fit [ALMA CO(1-0) observations: "we subtracted the continuum from the CO(1-0) spectral windows in the *uv* visibility plane using the task `uvcontsub`."] [ALMA CO(1-0) observations: "We selected a zeroth-order polynomial fit to the continuum channels adjacent to the CO(1-0) line"]
- processing.imaging.line_free_channels: 112.26-113.0 GHz and 113.57-114.12 GHz sky frequency ranges (v in [-2650, -690] km s^-1 and [820, 2280] km s^-1) [ALMA CO(1-0) observations: "in the 112.26-113.0 GHz and 113.57-114.12 GHz sky frequency ranges (corresponding to $v\in[-2650,-690]$ km s$^{-1}$ and $v\in[820, 2280]$ km s$^{-1}$ with respect to the CO(1-0) line centre)."]
- step 1: Image and measure 3 mm continuum emission
  - method: Combine the two low-resolution line-free spectral windows (4 GHz bandwidth) and fit 2D Gaussians on the primary-beam corrected map. [ALMA map of the 3mm continuum: "The map was obtained by combining the data (total bandwidth $\Delta\nu=4$ GHz) from the two low-resolution spectral windows centred at 101.190 GHz and 99.387 GHz, which are not contaminated by line emission."] [ALMA map of the 3mm continuum: "A 2D Gaussian fit performed on the PB-corrected map shown in Fig. 18 delivers best-fit peak flux densities of $S_{cont}^{NED01}=0.71\pm0.03$ mJy beam$^{-1}$ and $S_{cont}^{NED02}=2.26\pm0.05$ mJy beam$^{-1}$"]
  - software: CASA
  - parameter: Spectral windows centred at 101.190 GHz and 99.387 GHz, total bandwidth = 4 GHz [ALMA map of the 3mm continuum: "The map was obtained by combining the data (total bandwidth $\Delta\nu=4$ GHz) from the two low-resolution spectral windows centred at 101.190 GHz and 99.387 GHz, which are not contaminated by line emission."]
  - fitting: 2D Gaussian fit on PB-corrected continuum map [ALMA map of the 3mm continuum: "A 2D Gaussian fit performed on the PB-corrected map shown in Fig. 18 delivers best-fit peak flux densities"]
- step 2: Extract total CO(1-0) spectra, fit Gaussian profiles, refine systemic redshifts, and derive total molecular gas masses
  - method: Fit single and double Gaussian functions to continuum-subtracted CO(1-0) spectra to determine central sky frequencies and systemic redshifts, then compute line luminosities and molecular gas masses from redshift-corrected cubes. [4.1.1 NED01: "To refine the redshift estimate of NED01 we performed two spectral fits, one with a single-Gaussian function and one with two Gaussians"] [4.2 CO(1-0) morphology: "By using the CO(1-0) line fluxes obtained from the spectral fits, we computed the total CO(1-0) line luminosities of the two galaxies, and from these estimated their molecular line masses"]
  - software: CASA
  - parameter: PV diagram slit sizes ~30'' and ~9'' aligned with major and minor axes of NED01 [4.1.1 NED01: "The CO(1-0) position-velocity (PV) diagrams displayed in Fig. 3 were computed in `CASA` from slit-like apertures with sizes of $\sim30''$ and $\sim9''$, aligned with the major and minor axes of the CO(1-0) disk of NED01 respectively"]
  - assumption: alpha_CO = 4.3 ± 1.3 M_sun (K km s^-1 pc^2)^-1 for galaxy disks [4.2 CO(1-0) morphology: "by adopting a standard CO-to-H$_2$ conversion factor of $\alpha_{CO}$ = $4.3\pm1.3$ M$_{\odot}$ K km s$^{-1}$ pc$^2$."]
  - assumption: Systematic flux calibration error of 5% included in integrated flux uncertainties [4.2 CO(1-0) morphology: "Errors on the CO(1-0) integrated fluxes include a systematic calibration error of 5%, typical for ALMA Band 3 observations."]
  - fitting: 1-Gaussian and 2-Gaussian spectral line fits [4.1.1 NED01: "To refine the redshift estimate of NED01 we performed two spectral fits, one with a single-Gaussian function and one with two Gaussians"] [NED01: "To refine the redshift estimate of NED01 we performed two spectral fits, one with a single-Gaussian function and one with two Gaussians"]
  - validation: Verified that the new redshift estimate matches the rotational centre in major and minor axis PV diagrams [4.1.1 NED01: "We further checked that the new redshift estimate matches with the kinematic centre of NED01’s host galaxy disk."]
- step 4: Model rotating CO(1-0) disk kinematics and extract non-rotational residuals
  - method: Crop continuum-subtracted datacubes with imsubimage, export with exportfits, fit concentric tilted-ring rotating disk models with 3D-BAROLO, subtract best-fit model cubes from data, and fit double-Gaussian functions to residual spectra. [5.1 Modelling of the CO(1-0) disk in NE…: "We produced the CO(1-0) datacubes that need to be given as an input to the software by cropping the continuum-subtracted ALMA CO(1-0) (clean) datacube with the CASA task `imsubimage` and then exporting it into a FITS file using the `exportfits` task."] [5.1 Modelling of the CO(1-0) disk in NE…: "For each region, we also produced a residual map by subtracting the BBarolo disk model from the input datacube."]
  - software: CASA, 3D-BAROLO
  - parameter: NED01: v in [-600, +600] km s^-1, PA = 95° ± 10° (fixed), inclination = 45° ± 15° (fixed), S/N > 2.3; Big region = 35''x35'' (7 rings of 2'' width), Medium = 16''x15'' (7 rings of 2''), Small = 10''x8'' (3 rings of 2''); residual box 8.4''… [5.1 Modelling of the CO(1-0) disk in NE…: "Using the ALMA moment 1 map shown in Fig. 20, we estimated $PA=95^\circ\pm10^\circ$ and $\iota=45^\circ\pm15^\circ$."] [5.1 Modelling of the CO(1-0) disk in NE…: "The fitting was performed considering only pixels with a $S/N>2.3$ to enable inclusion of the low-S/N extended features."]
  - parameter: NED02: non-PB-corrected cube, box 27.4''x25.4'', v in [-1000, +1000] km s^-1, PA = 120° ± 10°, inclination = 70° ± 15°, S/N > 3, 5 rings of 2'' width; residual box 8''x8'' [5.2 Modelling of the CO(1-0) disk in NE…: "obtained by cropping the cleaned (non-PB-corrected) continuum-subtracted ALMA datacube around NED02 using a box size of $27.4''\times25.4''$ (i.e $9.4\times8.7$ kpc), and selecting a velocity range of $v\in[-1000,+1000]$ km s$^{-1}$"] [5.2 Modelling of the CO(1-0) disk in NE…: "From the ALMA observations, we estimated a position angle of $PA=120^\circ\pm10^\circ$ and an inclination of $\iota=70^\circ\pm15^\circ$. We applied a S/N threshold of $S/N>3$ for each pixel included in the modelling. We used five rings, each of $2\ensuremath{''}$ width (0.7 kpc)."]
  - assumption: Line emission is distributed in a geometrically thin disk dominated by pure rotational motion [5.1 Modelling of the CO(1-0) disk in NE…: "An important assumption of the BBarolo model, to consider when interpreting the results, is the hypothesis that the line emission is distributed in a geometrically thin disk whose kinematics is dominated by pure rotational motion."]
  - assumption: alpha_CO = 2.1 ± 1.2 M_sun (K km s^-1 pc^2)^-1 for residual/outflow gas mass [Table 3: "Computed assuming $\alpha_{\rm CO} = 2.1 \pm 1.2$ M$_{\odot}$ (K km s$^{-1}$ pc$^2$)$^{-1}$, which is the value with associated uncertainty estimated by (cite: 2018Cicone2) for the molecular outflow in NGC 6240."]
  - fitting: Ring-by-ring 3D tilted-ring fitting in 3D-BAROLO, followed by double-Gaussian fitting to extracted residual spectra [5.1 Modelling of the CO(1-0) disk in NE…: "The disk model is built up by combining concentric rings with a user-defined width, and the comparison between models and data is performed ring by ring."] [5.1 Modelling of the CO(1-0) disk in NE…: "shows that the blueshifted CO(1-0) residual emission is spectrally resolved into two emission peaks, which we modelled with double-Gaussian functions"]
  - validation: Compared three region sizes (Small, Medium, Big) for NED01 to verify that blueshifted residuals persist across all fits and selected the Big region fit as the most conservative (lowest residuals) [5.1 Modelling of the CO(1-0) disk in NE…: "In order to study the effect of adding the extended CO(1-0) line components in the BBarolo modelling, we run BBarolo on three regions with different sizes, and compare the results."] [5.1 Modelling of the CO(1-0) disk in NE…: "We find that a similar residual, compact, and blueshifted structure is common to all three fits performed with BBarolo, and it is actually minimised in this one performed on the *Big region* compared to those using the smaller regions."]
- result: Systemic redshift of NED01 = 0.0178 ± 0.0004 dimensionless [4.1.1 NED01: "We obtained $z=0.0178\pm0.0004$, which is offset by $v\sim-210 \ kms^{-1}$ with respect to the previously known redshift."]
- result: Systemic redshift of NED02 = 0.016812 ± 0.000003 dimensionless [4.1.2 NED02: "We used this value and its associated uncertainty to compute a new systemic redshift of NED02 equal to $z=0.016812\pm0.000003$, which is offset by $\sim50$ km s$^{-1}$ with respect to the previously known redshift."]
- result: 3 mm continuum peak flux density of NED01 = 0.71 ± 0.03 mJy beam^-1 [ALMA CO(1-0) observations: "with respective continuum peak flux densities equal to $S_{cont}^{NED01} = 0.71\pm0.03$ mJy beam$^{-1}$ and $S_{cont}^{NED02} = 2.26\pm0.05$ mJy beam$^{-1}$."]
- result: 3 mm continuum integrated flux of NED01 = 0.76 ± not stated mJy [ALMA map of the 3mm continuum: "The source-integrated continuum flux obtained through the fit is $S_{3mm}=0.76$ mJy for NED01 and $S_{3mm}=2.4$ mJy for NED02."]
- result: 3 mm continuum peak flux density of NED02 = 2.26 ± 0.05 mJy beam^-1 [ALMA CO(1-0) observations: "with respective continuum peak flux densities equal to $S_{cont}^{NED01} = 0.71\pm0.03$ mJy beam$^{-1}$ and $S_{cont}^{NED02} = 2.26\pm0.05$ mJy beam$^{-1}$."]
- result: 3 mm continuum integrated flux of NED02 = 2.4 ± not stated mJy [ALMA map of the 3mm continuum: "The source-integrated continuum flux obtained through the fit is $S_{3mm}=0.76$ mJy for NED01 and $S_{3mm}=2.4$ mJy for NED02."]
- result: Total CO(1-0) integrated flux of NED01 = 33.1 ± 1.4 Jy km s^-1 [Table 2: "| NED01 | 33.1 (1.4) | 5.1 (0.2) | 2.1 (0.7) |"]

- result: Total CO(1-0) integrated flux of NED02 = 59 ± 3 Jy km s^-1 [Table 2: "| NED02 | 59 (3) | 8.0 (0.4) | 3.4 (1.1) |"]

- result: NED01 CO residual Component 1 integrated flux (Big region) = 1.63 ± 0.14 Jy km s^-1 [Table 3: "| $S_{\rm CO(1-0)}dv$ \[Jy km s$^{-1}$\] | 1.63 (0.14) | 0.98 (0.11) |"]
- result: NED01 CO residual Component 1 molecular mass (Big region) = 0.5 ± 0.3 10^8 M_sun [Table 3: "| $M_{mol}$$^{\dag}$ \[10$^8$ M$_{\odot}$\] | 0.5 (0.3) | 0.31 (0.18) |"]
- result: NED01 CO residual Component 2 integrated flux (Big region) = 0.98 ± 0.11 Jy km s^-1 [Table 3: "| $S_{\rm CO(1-0)}dv$ \[Jy km s$^{-1}$\] | 1.63 (0.14) | 0.98 (0.11) |"]

- result: NED02 CO residual Component 2 integrated flux = 1.8 ± 0.2 Jy km s^-1 [Table 4: "| $S_{\rm CO(1-0)}dv$ \[Jy km s$^{-1}$\] | 2.4 (0.2) | 1.8 (0.2) |"]
- limitation (authors): The CO-to-H2 conversion factor alpha_CO in molecular outflows is poorly constrained. [Introduction: "For different ISM environments, such as the massive molecular outflows discovered in local starbursts and AGN host galaxies (cite: 2020Veilleux), the $\alpha_{\rm CO}$ parameter is very poorly constrained."]
- limitation (authors): NED02 is located close to the edge of the ALMA primary beam FWHM, resulting in lower CO(1-0) sensitivity, and its compact size means its morphology and kinematics are more affected by beam smearing.
