### 2026ApJ..1005...94N: Dynamical Evidence for a Billion Solar-mass Black Hole in Galaxy NGC 4061 from ALMA <SUP>12</SUP>CO(2─1) Kinematics (2026; nearby_galaxies, agn_and_quasars; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What is the dynamical mass of the supermassive black hole in the massive early-type galaxy NGC 4061, and does it resolve the discrepancy between previous indirect estimates based on conflicting velocity dispersion measurements?
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: CASA uvcontsub with a linear power-law function in the uv plane [2.3 Creating the Combined-$^{12}$CO(2$-…: "The continuum was modeled with a linear power-law function and subtracted from the combined and calibrated visibilities in the *uv* plane to isolate the $^{12}$CO(2$-$1) line emission."] [2.3 Creating the Combined-$^{12}$CO(2$-…: "This subtraction was carried out using the CASA task `uvcontsub` (e.g., (cite: Davis20; Nguyen20))."]
- processing.imaging.line_free_channels: all line-free channels across the 12 targeted SPWs (exact channel ranges not stated) [Creating the Combined-12CO(2-1) Data Cu…: "with all line-free channels across the 12 targeted SPWs"]
- step 1: Calibrate, combine, and image 1.3 mm continuum and 12CO(2-1) line cube
  - method: Calibrate Cycle 6 and Cycle 7 execution blocks with the ALMA Science Pipeline, combine the three measurement sets with CASA concat using visweightscale, image the 1.3 mm continuum with tclean (MFS) and fit source with imfit, subtract conti… [2.1 Data Reduction: "We combined the visibility data from all three MS into a combined and calibrated MS using the `concat` task in CASA."] [2.2 Creating the Combined 1.3 mm Contin…: "We produced the combined continuum image at 1.3mm using the CASA `tclean` task in multifrequency synthesis mode (cite: Rau11), combining the continuum SPWs with line-free channels from the targeted SPWs of all three MS."]
  - software: CASA, ALMA Science Pipeline, concat, tclean, imfit, uvcontsub
  - parameter: visweightscale = 0.6 (2019.1.00036.S) and 0.2 for each of the two 2018.1.00397.S tracks [2.1 Data Reduction: "The optimal visibility–weight scaling factors applied in the `visweightscale` mode were 0.6 for the `uid_A002_Xea90c0_X334e` MS (2019.1.00036.S), and 0.2 for each of the `uid_A002_Xe03886_X7606` and `uid_A002_Xe03886_Xd75e` MSs (2018.1.00397.S)."]
  - parameter: Briggs robust = 0.5, pixel scale = 0.04'', image size = 512 x 512 pixels^2, channel width = 10 km s^-1, clean threshold = 1.5 sigma_RMS [2.3 Creating the Combined-$^{12}$CO(2$-…: "The resulting cube has dimensions of 512 $\times$ 512 pixels$^2$ with a pixel scale of $0.04\ensuremath{''}$, which adequately samples the synthesized beam while maintaining a manageable file size. We used a channel width of 10 km s$^{-1}$—an optimal choice for SMBH dynamical modeling (cite: Davis14; Nguyen21; Ngo2025…"] [2.3 Creating the Combined-$^{12}$CO(2$-…: "The continuum-subtracted dirty cube was cleaned down to a threshold of 1.5 times the root-mean-squared (RMS) noise level ($\sigma_{\rm RMS}$; measured from line-free channels)."]
  - assumption: Rest frequency of 12CO(2-1) = 230.538 GHz [2.3 Creating the Combined-$^{12}$CO(2$-…: "The velocity axis was referenced to the rest frequency of 230.538 GHz."]
  - fitting: 2D Gaussian fit to the continuum source using CASA imfit; linear power-law continuum fit in uvcontsub [2.2 Creating the Combined 1.3 mm Contin…: "A two-dimensional (2D) Gaussian fit using the `imfit` routine in CASA confirms the source is spatially resolved."] [2.3 Creating the Combined-$^{12}$CO(2$-…: "The continuum was modeled with a linear power-law function and subtracted from the combined and calibrated visibilities in the *uv* plane to isolate the $^{12}$CO(2$-$1) line emission."]
  - validation: Continuum flux density checked against the ~10% ALMA flux calibration uncertainty [2.2 Creating the Combined 1.3 mm Contin…: "with an integrated flux density of $3.48 \pm 0.18$ mJy, consistent within the $\approx$10% ALMA flux calibration uncertainty."]
- result: 1.3 mm continuum integrated flux density = 3.48 ± +/-0.18 mJy [Table 1 (continuum): "| Integrated flux (mJy) | $3.48\pm0.18$ |"]
- result: 1.3 mm continuum deconvolved source size = 0.26 x 0.22 ± +/-0.03 x +/-0.03 arcsec^2 [Table 1 (continuum): "| Deconvolved size (arcsec$^2$) | $(0.26\pm0.03)\times(0.22\pm0.03)$ |"]
- limitation (authors): The wide range of published distances to NGC 4061 (68–112 Mpc in NED) introduces a ~37% systematic uncertainty in M_BH, dominating the total error budget. [4.5.1 Distances: "Since the inferred $M_{\rm BH}$ scales linearly with the assumed distance ($M_{\rm BH} \propto D$), the wide range of distance estimates for NGC 4061 (68–112 Mpc; NED)—primarily based on optical and near-infrared light curves of the Type Ia supernova SN 2008bf—constitutes the dominant source of uncertainty in our $M_{…"]
- limitation (authors): Parameter degeneracies exist between M_BH and M/L_F814W, between y_cen and v_off (due to the beam size relative to the continuum peak), between inclination i and M/L_F814W, and between flux normalization f and sigma_gas. [4.4 Results: "While the other model parameters are well constrained, a clear anti-correlation between $M_{\rm BH}$ and $M/L_{\rm F814W}$ is evident (see both Figure 9 and Figure 10)."]
- limitation (authors): Adopting the HST F555W band instead of F814W for the stellar mass model introduces an additional ~20% uncertainty in M_BH due to stronger dust attenuation in F555W. [4.5.7 Using the Alternative F555W MGE S…: "Overall, adopting the F555W-based stellar mass model introduces an additional uncertainty of approximately 20% in the inferred SMBH mass for NGC 4061, likely reflecting the greater sensitivity of the F555W band to dust attenuation compared to the F814W band."]
- not stated (card): Exact frequency/channel ranges identified as line-free for continuum imaging and uvcontsub; Self-calibration details (Section 2.3 refers to the 'final self-calibrated, cleaned cube', but no self-calibration procedure, solution intervals, or calibration modes are described); Resolution of the discrepancy in reported continuum RMS noise (Section 2.2 text states 120 uJy beam^-1, while Table 1 lists …

### 2020MNRAS.496..598C: Linking the small-scale relativistic winds and the large-scale molecular outflows in the z = 1.51 lensed quasar HS 0810+2554 (2020; agn_and_quasars, high_z_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Resolve the host mm continuum, measure molecular gas mass and extent, and search for an outflow linking small scale relativistic winds to large scale molecular gas.
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: uvcontsub; linear fit in visibility plane to visually selected line-free channels [ALMA Observations and Data Reduction: "Prior to creating the CO line cubes, the continuum has been subtracted from the datasets using the CASA task *uvcontsub*."] [ALMA Observations and Data Reduction: "A linear fit of the continuum in the visually selected line-free channels of the spectral windows has been calculated in the visibility plane and subtracted from the full spectral range."]
- processing.imaging.line_free_channels: Visually selected; exact channel selections not stated [ALMA Observations and Data Reduction: "on the visually selected line-free channels of the observed spectral ranges."]
- step 1: Measure continuum fluxes
  - method: Image line-free channels with mfs; align optical and mm images on D; fit low-resolution continuum with CASA 2D Fit. [ALMA Observations and Data Reduction: "The images of continuum emission in both bands have been obtained running the CASA task *tclean* in the multi-frequency synthesis (mfs) mode on the visually selected line-free channels of the observed spectral ranges."] [Analysis of the mm Continuum Emission o…: "The HST and the ALMA band 4 continuum images were aligned relative to the isolated image D."]
  - software: CASA, tclean, 2D Fit
  - parameter: Briggs robust = 2 [ALMA Observations and Data Reduction: "To obtain the maximum sensitivity we used the Briggs weighting scheme with the robust parameter set to 2 (natural weighting)."]
  - parameter: Continuum mean frequencies 143 GHz and 97 GHz [Analysis of the mm Continuum Emission o…: "We find the flux densities of the continuum at mean frequencies of 143 GHz and 97 GHz"]
  - fitting: CASA 2D Fit within a source-centered 2.5″ radius circle [Analysis of the mm Continuum Emission o…: "To estimate the flux density of the continuum we used the CASA tool 2D Fit with the fitting region set to a circle of radius of 2$^{\prime\prime}\!\!.$5 centered on the source."]
  - validation: Compare with CARMA upper limit [Analysis of the mm Continuum Emission o…: "the observed ALMA flux densities are found to be just below the 3$\sigma$ upper limit of $\sim$1 mJy inferred from the non-detection of HS 0810$+$2554 by CARMA (cite: 2011ApJ...730..108R)."]
- step 2: Measure integrated CO spectra
  - method: Subtract a visibility-plane linear continuum, form cubes and moment 0 images, extract circular-aperture spectra, and fit three Gaussians. [ALMA Observations and Data Reduction: "A linear fit of the continuum in the visually selected line-free channels of the spectral windows has been calculated in the visibility plane and subtracted from the full spectral range."] [ALMA Observations and Data Reduction: "We finally obtained the integrated intensity images (moment 0 maps) of each datacube, using the task *immoments*."]
  - software: CASA, uvcontsub, immoments, uvmodelfit
  - parameter: Extended CO(3–2): 137.714–137.980 GHz; extraction radius 0.7″ [The Einstein Ring of the CO(J=3→2) Emis…: "The image of the CO(J=3$\rightarrow$2) line emission was produced from the continuum subtracted data, in the frequency range 137.714 GHz to 137.980 GHz."] [The Einstein Ring of the CO(J=3→2) Emis…: "A spatially integrated spectrum was extracted from a circular region centered on HS 0810$+$2554 with a radius of 0$^{\prime\prime}\!\!.$7."]
  - parameter: Compact CO(3–2): 137.7–138.0 GHz; CO(2–1): 91.8–92.0 GHz; extraction radii 3″ [Analysis of the CO(J=3→2) and CO(J=2→1)…: "in the frequency range 137.7 GHz to 138.0 GHz and CO(J=2$\rightarrow$1) line in the frequency range 91.8 GHz to 92.0 GHz."] [Analysis of the CO(J=3→2) and CO(J=2→1)…: "Spatially integrated spectra of the CO(J=3$\rightarrow$2) and CO(J=2$\rightarrow$1) line emission were extracted from circular regions centered on HS 0810$+$2554 with radii of 3 $''$."]
  - assumption: Systemic z = 1.50849 ± 0.00002 [The Einstein Ring of the CO(J=3→2) Emis…: "We have assumed a systemic redshift of $z = 1.50849 \pm 0.00002$"]
  - fitting: Single Gaussian rejected; three-Gaussian χ²/ν: 11.4/10 extended CO(3–2), 18.5/17 compact CO(3–2), 11.1/9 CO(2–1) [The Einstein Ring of the CO(J=3→2) Emis…: "The fit with three Gaussians results in an acceptable fit with ${\chi^2}/\nu$ = 11.4/10."] [Analysis of the CO(J=3→2) and CO(J=2→1)…: "The fit with three Gaussians results in an acceptable fit with ${\chi^2}/\nu = 18.5/17$."]
  - validation: Independent uvmodelfit flux estimates; extended and compact CO(3–2) parameters consistent within errors [Analysis of the CO(J=3→2) and CO(J=2→1)…: "We also independently estimated the flux density of the CO(J=3$\rightarrow$2) emission line using the `uvmodelfit` command that fits a single component source model to the uv data."] [Analysis of the CO(J=3→2) and CO(J=2→1)…: "The best fit-parameters of the properties of the CO(J=3$\rightarrow$2) lines obtained with the extended and compact configurations are consistent within the error bars."]
- result: High-resolution combined continuum flux density at 143 GHz = 401/μcont ± ±50/μcont μJy [Analysis of the mm Continuum Emission o…: "The flux density of the continuum of the combined images at a mean frequency of 143 GHz is estimated to be (401 $\pm$ 50)/$\mu_{\rm cont}$ $\mu$Jy"]

- result: CO(2–1) Gaussian FWHM, components 1–3 = 230; 45; 174 ± ±54; ±16; ±42 km s−1 [Analysis of the CO(J=3→2) and CO(J=2→1)…: "($v_1$, FWHM$_1$) = ($-$127 $\pm$ 22 km s$^{-1}$, 230 $\pm$ 54 km s$^{-1}$)"]

- result: uvmodelfit CO(2–1) mean flux density over 638 km s−1 = 3.4/μ21 ± ±0.1/μ21 mJy [Analysis of the CO(J=3→2) and CO(J=2→1)…: "we independently estimate an average flux density of I = (3.4 $\pm$ 0.1 mJy)/$\mu_{21}$ within a spectral window of width 638 km s$^{-1}$."]
- result: Compact CO(2–1) outer-peak separation, conclusions = 257 ± ±29 km s−1 [Discussion and Conclusions: "$\Delta{v_{21}}$ = 257 $\pm$ 29 km s$^{-1}$ and $\Delta{v_{32}}$ = 228 $\pm$ 27 km s$^{-1}$, respectively."]
- result: Compact CO(3–2) outer-peak separation, conclusions = 228 ± ±27 km s−1 [Discussion and Conclusions: "$\Delta{v_{21}}$ = 257 $\pm$ 29 km s$^{-1}$ and $\Delta{v_{32}}$ = 228 $\pm$ 27 km s$^{-1}$, respectively."]
- result: Extended CO(3–2) outer-peak separation, conclusions = 254 ± ±23 km s−1 [Discussion and Conclusions: "the two outer peaks separated by $\Delta{v_{32}}$ = 254 $\pm$ 23 km s$^{-1}$."]
- result: CO(2–1) outer-peak separation, abstract = 220 ± ±19 km s−1 [abstract: "$\Delta{v_{21}}$ = 220 $\pm$ 19 km s$^{-1}$ and $\Delta{v_{32}}$ = 245 $\pm$ 28 km s$^{-1}$, respectively"]
- result: CO(3–2) outer-peak separation, abstract = 245 ± ±28 km s−1 [abstract: "$\Delta{v_{21}}$ = 220 $\pm$ 19 km s$^{-1}$ and $\Delta{v_{32}}$ = 245 $\pm$ 28 km s$^{-1}$, respectively"]
- limitation (authors): Continuum source size uncertain because of simple source geometry, low S/N and lens modeling synthesized ALMA data [Discussion and Conclusions: "very uncertain due to the assumed simplistic elliptical source model, the low S/N of the continuum ALMA data and the inherent problems with lens modeling the synthesized ALMA data."]
- limitation (authors): Higher-S/N images required to confirm mm–UV source offset [Lensing Analysis: "We caution, however, that higher S/N ALMA images would be required to confirm this offset."]
- limitation (authors): Differential magnification can distort CO line profiles [The Einstein Ring of the CO(J=3→2) Emis…: "The CO line profiles of lensed AGN will in general be distorted by differential magnification across the molecular disk"]
- not stated (card): Exact visually selected line-free channels for continuum imaging and uvcontsub.; tclean cell size, image size, deconvolver, cleaning mask, stopping threshold and iteration limit.; Primary beam correction and treatment of off-axis clump fluxes.; Full fitted SIE and external-shear lens parameters and their uncertainties.; glafic version, optimizer settings, parameter bounds, fitting statistic and i…

### 2018A&A...615A..28D: ALMA spectral line and imaging survey of a low and a high mass-loss rate AGB star between 335 and 362 GHz (2018; evolved_stars, astrochemistry; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What are the dominant physical, dynamical and chemical processes in the inner wind of the low mass-loss rate AGB star R Dor and the high mass-loss rate AGB star IK Tau, as traced by an ALMA band 7 spectral line and imaging survey?
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: Continuum subtracted, then entire cubes imaged [2.2 Data reduction: "The continuum was subtracted and the entire cubes were imaged."]
- step 1: Calibrate and image continuum and line cubes
  - method: Standard CASA calibration scripts, self-calibration on continuum channels, continuum subtraction, imaging of full cubes [2.2 Data reduction: "Line-free continuum channels were identified and used for self-calibration, applied to all data."]
  - software: CASA
  - parameter: robust 0.5 [2.2 Data reduction: "all images discussed used partial uniform weighting (CASA ‘robust 0.5’)."]
  - validation: Astrometric accuracy checked using phase-reference solutions and check sources; 17 mas adopted [2.2 Data reduction: "it seems reasonable to adopt 17 mas astrometric accuracy for both targets."]
- step 2: Derive dust mass and dust mass-loss rate from continuum
  - method: Subtract convolved stellar point source from continuum, convert the residual to dust mass assuming optically thin emission, and compute crossing time from the momentum equation solution [3 Continuum image: "we can estimate the 345 GHz emission from dust by subtracting a (stellar) point source, convolved with the natural synthesised beam, from the continuum"]
  - parameter: Drift velocity forced below 20 km/s [3 Continuum image: "We force the drift velocity to be lower than 20 km/s"]
  - assumption: Optically thin dust, opacity ~7 cm2/g, dust temperature 200-650 K (IK Tau) and 400-1530 K (R Dor) [3 Continuum image: "Assuming that the dust is optically thin and for an opacity of $\sim$7 cm$^2$/g"]
  - validation: Compared with dust-to-gas ratios from SED/SPHERE studies in literature; uncertainty factor ~5 [3 Continuum image: "subject to large uncertainties (being around a factor of 5)"]
- result: Continuum peak IK Tau = 0.069 ± not stated Jy/beam [3 Continuum image: "The continuum peak is 0.069 Jy/beam for IK Tau and 0.596 Jy/beam for R Dor"]
- result: Continuum peak R Dor = 0.596 ± not stated Jy/beam [3 Continuum image: "The continuum peak is 0.069 Jy/beam for IK Tau and 0.596 Jy/beam for R Dor"]
- result: Dust mass IK Tau = 3.7e-7 ± factor ~5 Msun [3 Continuum image: "we obtain a dust mass within the 3$\sigma$ contour of $3.7 \times 10^{-7}$ M$_{\odot}$ and $2 \times 10^{-8}$ M$_{\odot}$"]
- result: Dust mass R Dor = 2e-8 ± factor ~5 Msun [3 Continuum image: "we obtain a dust mass within the 3$\sigma$ contour of $3.7 \times 10^{-7}$ M$_{\odot}$ and $2 \times 10^{-8}$ M$_{\odot}$"]
- result: Dust mass-loss rate IK Tau = ~1e-8 ± factor ~5 Msun/yr [3 Continuum image: "dust mass loss rate of $\sim$1$\times10^{-8}$ M$_{\odot}$/yr for IK Tau and of $\sim$5$\times10^{-9}$ M$_{\odot}$/yr for R Dor"]
- result: Dust mass-loss rate R Dor = ~5e-9 ± factor ~5 Msun/yr [3 Continuum image: "dust mass loss rate of $\sim$1$\times10^{-8}$ M$_{\odot}$/yr for IK Tau and of $\sim$5$\times10^{-9}$ M$_{\odot}$/yr for R Dor"]
- limitation (authors): Dust masses and dust-to-gas ratios uncertain by about a factor of 5 [3 Continuum image: "subject to large uncertainties (being around a factor of 5)"]
- limitation (authors): Extended emission may be resolved out [2.2 Data reduction: "we have resolved-out extended emission"]
- not stated (card): Number of execution blocks and exact calibrator assignment per spectral window; Line-free channel ranges used for continuum and self-calibration; Self-calibration solution intervals and number of iterations; tclean deconvolver, masks, clean thresholds and iteration counts; Per-cube beam and rms for each spectral window; Exact adopted stellar flux model at 345 GHz; Parameters of the RADEX models u…

### 2025ApJ...989...83B: CO Emission and Absorption-line Survey of the M87 Nucleus Using Archival ALMA Imaging (2025; agn_and_quasars, nearby_galaxies; archival_reuse)
- question: Is there molecular gas in the inner kpc of M87 (CO emission from the circumnuclear disk, ionized-gas filaments and dusty clouds, or CO absorption against the nucleus), as probed by archival ALMA imaging?
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: uvcontsub with fitorder = 1; three line-channel exclusion cases (|v-vsys| < 500, < 1000 km/s, and 100 < |v-vsys| < 2200 km/s for CO(1-0) only); main results use the first case [MFS and Spectral Imaging: "Lastly, we subtracted the continuum for MSs containing CO transitions using the `uvcontsub` task (with `fitorder` = 1) with three distinct fitting regions."] [MFS and Spectral Imaging: "we explore relatively narrow emission and absorption-line results derived using the first `uvcontsub` case"]
- processing.imaging.line_free_channels: Channels with |v_LOS - v_sys| < 500 km/s excluded in the first case (main case) [MFS and Spectral Imaging: "we excluded channels corresponding to either $|\ensuremath{v_\mathrm{LOS}}- \ensuremath{v_\mathrm{sys}}| < 500$ km s$^{-1}$ to probe more standard velocity ranges for extragalactic gas"]
- step 4: Subtract continuum in the uv plane and image CO cubes
  - method: uvcontsub with fitorder = 1 with three line-channel exclusion cases, then image continuum-subtracted data at native and coarser (~20-40 km/s) binning [MFS and Spectral Imaging: "Lastly, we subtracted the continuum for MSs containing CO transitions using the `uvcontsub` task (with `fitorder` = 1) with three distinct fitting regions."] [MFS and Spectral Imaging: "We imaged the continuum-subtracted data into spectral cubes at both the native frequency spacing and at more coarse binning ($\sim$20$-$40 km s$^{-1}$) to increase the signal-to-noise ratio (S/N)."]
  - software: CASA, uvcontsub, tclean
  - parameter: Case 1: exclude |v_LOS - v_sys| < 500 km/s (used for the main emission and absorption results) [MFS and Spectral Imaging: "we excluded channels corresponding to either $|\ensuremath{v_\mathrm{LOS}}- \ensuremath{v_\mathrm{sys}}| < 500$ km s$^{-1}$ to probe more standard velocity ranges for extragalactic gas"]
  - parameter: Case 3 (CO(1-0) only): exclude 100 < |v_LOS - v_sys| < 2200 km/s [MFS and Spectral Imaging: "In a third case for CO(1$-$0) only, we excluded the ranges $100 < |\ensuremath{v_\mathrm{LOS}}- \ensuremath{v_\mathrm{sys}}| < 2200$ km s$^{-1}$ to test for possible broad emission-line features"]
  - fitting: Linear (fitorder = 1) continuum fit per visibility [MFS and Spectral Imaging: "Lastly, we subtracted the continuum for MSs containing CO transitions using the `uvcontsub` task (with `fitorder` = 1) with three distinct fitting regions."]
  - validation: Cases 2 and 3 tested; they produce faint, likely spurious spectral deficits or excesses near redshifted CO [MFS and Spectral Imaging: "we explore the second and third `uvcontsub` cases that produce very faint (and likely spurious) spectral deficits or excesses near redshifted CO transitions"]
- step 7: Fit CO absorption lines in peak continuum spectra
  - method: Compute opacity tau_nu = -ln(T_nu/T_cont); fit each CO absorption feature with a Gaussian and linear continuum including Hanning smoothing; MC resampling for errors; weighted averages over data sets; Voronoi-binned refit of Xa1 across the … [CO Absorption-line Properties: "we fit each CO absorption feature with a Gaussian line profile and a linear continuum to recover the line centroid"] [CO Absorption-line Properties: "Here, the peak continuum spectrum $T_\nu \propto S_\nu/\theta_\mathrm{maj}\theta_\mathrm{min}$ is measured in K units and $T_{\nu,\mathrm{cont}}$ is the fitted linear continuum level about the CO line feature."]
  - software: CASA
  - parameter: MC resampling adding noise (std of line-free portions) to the best-fit model and shifting bin centers by a normal fraction (std = 1/4 channel width) [CO Absorption-line Properties: "We estimated parameter uncertainties by an MC resampling technique that adds random noise to the best-fitting model spectrum before refitting."] [CO Absorption-line Properties: "These frequency shifts were also drawn from a normal distribution with standard deviation equal to a quarter of the corresponding $\Delta \nu_\mathrm{obs}$ value in Table"]
  - parameter: For CO(2-1) only the Xb APP-mode fit from 2016.1.01154.V is used among .V data [CO Absorption-line Properties: "but we only utilize spectral fits to the most regular Xb data set from program 2016.1.01154.V when calculating CO absorber properties"]
  - assumption: Hanning smoothing incorporated to recover intrinsic line widths [CO Absorption-line Properties: "These fits incorporated Hanning smoothing[^2] to recover intrinsic $\sigma_\mathrm{LOS}$."]
  - fitting: Gaussian + linear continuum least-squares-type fit with MC uncertainties (no sampler or priors stated) [CO Absorption-line Properties: "we fit each CO absorption feature with a Gaussian line profile and a linear continuum to recover the line centroid"]
  - validation: Centroid-frequency ratios of CO(1-0), (2-1), (3-2) broadly consistent with expectations; line detected across multiple data sets and transitions [CO Absorption-line Properties: "and the observed ratios are broadly consistent with expectations"]
- result: Weighted-average peak opacity tau_0 CO(1-0) = 0.00239 ± 0.00054 dimensionless [CO Absorption-line Properties: "we also constructed final weighted-average peak opacities $\overline{\tau}_\mathrm{0,CO(1-0)} = 0.00239\pm 0.00054$ and $\overline{\tau}_\mathrm{0,CO(2-1)} = 0.00390\pm 0.00063$"]
- result: Weighted-average peak opacity tau_0 CO(2-1) = 0.00390 ± 0.00063 dimensionless [CO Absorption-line Properties: "we also constructed final weighted-average peak opacities $\overline{\tau}_\mathrm{0,CO(1-0)} = 0.00239\pm 0.00054$ and $\overline{\tau}_\mathrm{0,CO(2-1)} = 0.00390\pm 0.00063$"]
- result: Integrated opacity tau_CO(1-0), weighted average = 0.0281 ± 0.0045 km/s [CO Absorption-line Properties: "The integrated opacity $\overline{\tau}_\mathrm{CO(1-0)} = 0.0281 \pm 0.0045$ km s$^{-1}$"]
- result: Integrated opacity tau_CO(2-1), weighted average = 0.0410 ± 0.0041 km/s [CO Absorption-line Properties: "$\overline{\tau}_\mathrm{CO(2-1)} = 0.0410 \pm 0.0041$ km s$^{-1}$"]
- result: Weighted-average absorber velocity offset v_LOS - v_sys (CO(1-0) and CO(2-1)) = -77.21 ± 0.44 km/s [Kinematics: "the weighted-average $\overline{v}_\mathrm{LOS} - \ensuremath{v_\mathrm{sys}}= -77.21 \pm 0.44$ km s$^{-1}$"]
- result: Weighted-average absorber line width sigma_LOS = 4.65 ± 0.47 km/s [Kinematics: "the observed $\overline{\sigma}_\mathrm{LOS} = 4.65\pm0.47$ km s$^{-1}$ is typical of disk-like clouds"]
- result: Velocity gradient of CO(1-0) absorption across the central beam = <= 3 km/s across ~10 pc ± not stated km/s [CO Absorption-line Properties: "shows the velocity gradient to be no more than 3 km s$^{-1}$ across $\sim$10 pc"]
- limitation (authors): Bandpass calibration effects on tau_0 and tau_CO are not quantified [CO Absorption-line Properties: "However, we do not attempt to quantify here the bandpass calibration effects on"]
- limitation (authors): CO(3-2) and CO(2-1) opacities are less reliable than CO(1-0), so LTE reconciliation is not attempted [Temperature and Column Densities: "Unfortunately, neither $\tau_\mathrm{CO(3-2)}$ or $\overline{\tau}_\mathrm{CO(2-1)}$ values are as reliable as is $\overline{\tau}_\mathrm{CO(1-0)}$"]
- limitation (authors): Xa1 maximum recoverable scale cannot entirely rule out diffuse emission over larger regions [Channel Maps: "cannot entirely rule out diffuse emission spread out over a couple of the larger regions with $\sim$5$''$ extent"]
- not stated (card): CASA and pipeline versions used for each data set (only 'the appropriate pipeline version' is given); Briggs robust value for MFS maps, cell size, image size, niter/threshold, deconvolver and masks for tclean MFS and cube imaging; Which flux calibrator and which spws had setjy rescaling, and the exact rescale factors per spw; Self-calibration details: refant, solint sequence, number of loops, whi…

### 2018A&A...612A..29B: Molecular outflow and feedback in the obscured quasar XID2028 revealed by ALMA (2018; agn_and_quasars, high_z_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Is there a molecular (CO) outflow and what are the gas content, depletion time and disc kinematics in the obscured QSO XID2028, which hosts a massive ionised outflow?
- processing.imaging.line_free_channels: continuum from TDM SPWs: 224-225.5 GHz and 236.5-238.2 GHz [2.1 1.3 mm continuum: "we collapsed the line free channels in the two TDM spectral windows (in the ranges 224-225.5 GHz and 236.5-238.2 GHz)"]
- step 1: Measure 1.3 mm continuum
  - method: Clean image with Briggs weighting of line-free TDM channels, then Gaussian fit to visibilities [2.1 1.3 mm continuum: "We then estimated the continuum flux by fitting with a Gaussian function the visibilities of the continuum dataset (via the CASA task `uvmodelfit`)."]
  - software: CASA clean, uvmodelfit
  - parameter: Visibility noise 7.5 uJy; flux 142 uJy [2.1 1.3 mm continuum: "We measured a continuum flux of 142 $\mu$Jy for a detection significance of 19$\sigma$"]
  - fitting: Gaussian fit in uv plane [2.1 1.3 mm continuum: "fitting with a Gaussian function the visibilities of the continuum dataset"]
- step 2: Measure CO(5-4) flux and size
  - method: Continuum-subtracted cube; spectrum from polygonal region; Gaussian uvmodelfit in -800 to 800 km/s [2.2 CO(5-4) emission line: "We then estimated the line flux by fitting with a Gaussian function the visibilities of the continuum-subtracted dataset (using the CASA task `uvmodelfit`), in the same velocity range as above."]
  - software: CASA, uvmodelfit
  - parameter: velocity range -800 to 800 km/s; 20 km/s binning for spectrum [2.2 CO(5-4) emission line: "integrated over the velocity range \[-800$\div$800 km s$^{-1}$\] around the expected line frequency"]
  - assumption: z=1.5930 for velocity conversion [2.2 CO(5-4) emission line: "The redshift of the narrow component of the rest-frame optical emission lines (z=1.5930) was adopted to convert the frequency to velocity space"]
  - fitting: Single Gaussian line fit, FWHM~500 km/s, reduced chi2 2.27 [2.2 CO(5-4) emission line: "the fit with a single Gaussian component returns FWHM$\sim$500 km s$^{-1}$"]
- result: 1.3 mm continuum flux of nucleus = 142 ± 7.5 uJy [Table 1: "S$_{\rm cont, nucleus}$ ($\mu$Jy) | 142$\pm7.5$"]
- result: Continuum deconvolved FWHM = 0.30 ± 0.05 arcsec [Table 1: "FWHM (deconvolved, $''$) | 0.30$''$$\pm$0.05$''$"]
- result: CO(5-4) line intensity = 0.77 ± 0.03 Jy km/s [Table 1: "$I_{\rm CO(5-4)}$ (Jy km s$^{-1}$) | 0.77$\pm$0.03"]
- result: CO(5-4) line luminosity log L' = 9.63 ± 0.05 K km/s pc^2 [2.2 CO(5-4) emission line: "logL’$_{\rm CO(5-4)}$/(K km s$^{-1}$ pc$^2$)=9.63$\pm0.05$"]
- limitation (authors): Outflow mass estimates are order of magnitude given chain of assumptions (alpha_CO, excitation). [6 High velocity molecular gas: "We stress that the two values suffer from large uncertainties, given the chain of assumptions employed"]
- not stated (card): Briggs robust value, cell size, image size, clean threshold and masks; Exact velocity range/channel selection used for continuum subtraction of the CO cube; Details of the uvmodelfit starting parameters; Exact SLED-extrapolation ratios (r51 etc.) used for CO(1-0) flux; Flux correction and noise estimates for the Band 3 and Band 7 snapshot datasets and how they were imaged; 3D-BAROLO ring setup an…

### 2015ApJ...814...22E: Detection of Infall in the Protostar B335 with ALMA (2015; star_formation; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Is there unambiguous infall toward the Class 0 protostar B335, seen as redshifted absorption against the continuum, and can an inside-out collapse model reproduce the HCN and HCO+ profiles?
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: Image plane, MIRIAD; polynomial fit to line-free channels, first-order for HCO+, HCN, CS and zeroth-order for H13CN [Observations: "The polynomials were first-order for the HCO$^{+}$, HCN, and CS cubes, but zeroth-order for the H$^{13}$CN cube due to an abundance of lines in that spectrum."]
- step 1: Self-calibrate and image continuum and line cubes
  - method: Self-calibration on smoothed line-free data, MFS continuum imaging, Clark CLEAN of continuum and each channel [Observations: "A smoothed version of the data was self-calibrated using line-free channels to create a continuum dataset."]
  - software: CASA
  - parameter: Cell 0.1", 512x512, thresholds 0.5 mJy (continuum) and 60 mJy per channel [Observations: "down to a threshold of 60 mJy per channel, again $\sim$2 $\sigma$."]
- step 2: Subtract continuum in image plane
  - method: Polynomial fit to line-free channels, subtracted in image plane to avoid artifacts with absorption [Observations: "Continuum subtraction in the uv plane can cause artifacts in the presence of absorption features, so we removed the continuum in the image plane."]
  - software: MIRIAD
- limitation (authors): Simple spherical models do not perfectly match; abundance profiles are ad hoc [4.2 Simple Models of Line Emission: Pur…: "The simple, spherical models do not perfectly match the observations."]
- not stated (card): Line-free channel ranges for continuum and baseline; CASA version; Whether tclean or clean was used and whether clean masks were applied; uv weighting/robust; Model velocity field and shell structure details are only in Evans 2005; Mass-weighted dust temperature model details; Model beam/spectral convolution details; Exact fit procedure for abundances (done by eye?)

### 2012ApJ...757..129R: Kinematics of the CO Gas in the Inner Regions of the TW Hya Disk (2012; protoplanetary_disks; archival_reuse)
- question: What do the spatially and spectrally resolved 12CO J=2-1 and J=3-2 line wings from the TW Hya disk reveal about the temperature and kinematic structure of the inner disk?
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: performed on visibilities; various polynomial orders/channel ranges tested [2 Data and Calibration: "varying the polynomial order and channel ranges employed to determine the underlying continuum level"]
- step 1: Calibrate and image CO cubes
  - method: Calibrate SV data in CASA, continuum-subtract, invert with natural weighting, CLEAN with polygon mask; native and binned channels [2 Data and Calibration: "We utilized two different channel spacings: (1) “native", meaning 0.20 and 0.12 km s$^{-1}$ for $J$=2$-$1 and $J$=3$-$2"]
  - software: CASA
  - parameter: systemic LSR velocity +2.87 km/s forced onto a channel center [2 Data and Calibration: "we elected to force the reduced spectral datasets to have a channel precisely centered on the known systemic LSR velocity of TW Hya, $+$2.87 km s$^{-1}$."]
  - validation: Tested atmospheric CO, flat Tsys re-calibration, and continuum subtraction variations; wings persist [2 Data and Calibration: "we re-calibrated the data instead with a spectrally flat, band-averaged $T_{\rm sys}$ correction. This had no discernable effect on the results"]
- result: Projected line-wing velocity extent = 2.1 ± not stated km/s [2 Data and Calibration: "significant ($>$5 $\sigma$) emission in the wings of both CO transitions out to projected velocities at least $\pm$2.1 km s$^{-1}$"]
- result: CO 2-1 integrated flux (5"x5" box, 0.20 km/s) = 17.5 ± 1.8 Jy km/s [Table 1: "$7.8\pm0.8$ | $17.5\pm1.8$"]
- result: CO 3-2 integrated flux (0.12 km/s) = 37.2 ± 3.7 Jy km/s [Table 1: "$9.7\pm1.0$ | $37.2\pm3.7$"]
- limitation (authors): Model parameters are not unique nor optimized; limited resolution [3.3.3 A Disk Warp: "the warp model parameters we determine or even the formulation we adopt are not unique, nor optimized"]
- not stated (card): CLEAN threshold, polygon mask geometry, cell and image size; Exact line-free channels and polynomial order for continuum subtraction; LIME settings (number of grid points, photons) and CO abundance X_co value; Gas-to-dust ratio and dust properties details for RADMC-3D model; Exact MPFIT setup, initial values and how manual adjustments were made; Distance to TW Hya adopted

### 2023A&A...677A..15M: The GUAPOS project. III. Characterization of the O- and N-bearing complex organic molecules content and search for chemical differentiation (2023; astrochemistry, star_formation; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Characterize the abundances and emission morphology of nine O-bearing and six N-bearing complex organic molecules toward the hot molecular core G31.41+0.31, compare them with other sources and chemical models, and search for chemical diffe…
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: STATCONT statistical continuum determination on extracted spectrum and cubes [Observations: "The baseline has been analyzed by (cite: colziguapos) with the software STATCONT (cite: alvaro2018), to statistically determine the value of the continuum level and then subtract it from the spectrum."] [Analysis of the maps: "To create the maps of each line, we first removed the continuum emission from the cubes using the software STATCONT (cite: alvaro2018), and then created the integrated intensity (moment 0) map for each line."]
- step 1: Extract and baseline-subtract the central spectrum
  - method: Extract spectra from an area equal to the beam size centered on the continuum peak across all basebands, align them, and subtract continuum baseline using STATCONT. [Observations: "For all the observed basebands, the spectra have been extracted from an area equal to the size of the beam and centered toward the peak of the continuum."] [Observations: "The baseline has been analyzed by (cite: colziguapos) with the software STATCONT (cite: alvaro2018), to statistically determine the value of the continuum level and then subtract it from the spectrum."]
  - software: CASA, STATCONT
  - parameter: Aperture size = 1.2'' x 1.2'' (beam size) [Observations: "the spectra have been extracted from an area equal to the size of the beam and centered toward the peak of the continuum."]
  - parameter: Continuum level uncertainty = ~11% [Observations: "Both from the alignment of the different spectra and from the statistical analysis performed with STATCONT on the final spectrum, the uncertainty on the determination of the continuum level is $\sim11\%$."]
  - validation: Consistency between spectral alignment and STATCONT statistical analysis (~11% continuum uncertainty) [Observations: "Both from the alignment of the different spectra and from the statistical analysis performed with STATCONT on the final spectrum, the uncertainty on the determination of the continuum level is $\sim11\%$."]
- step 3: Create normalized mean emission maps and fit 2D Gaussians to determine peak positions and sizes
  - method: Subtract continuum from cubes with STATCONT, compute moment-0 maps for up to 8 unblended lines per species, normalize each map to its peak, average them with CASA immath, and fit a 2D Gaussian and compute residuals with CASA imfit. [Analysis of the maps: "To create the maps of each line, we first removed the continuum emission from the cubes using the software STATCONT (cite: alvaro2018), and then created the integrated intensity (moment 0) map for each line."] [Analysis of the maps: "To obtain a mean map of each species, we normalized each map to its peak intensity and then averaged all the maps of the same species together, using the task *immath* of CASA."]
  - software: STATCONT, CASA
  - parameter: Up to 8 unblended lines per species covering low and high E_U [Analysis of the maps: "To create the emission maps of the species analyzed in this work we selected for each of them eight of the most unblended lines, or the maximum number possible for those species for which we detect less than eight (mostly) unblended transitions to fit."]
  - fitting: 2D Gaussian fit using CASA task imfit [Analysis of the maps: "have been fitted with a 2D Gaussian using the task *imfit* inside CASA to obtain the position of the peak of emission and the size of the emission."]
  - validation: Inspection of 2D Gaussian residual maps (residuals in core center <= 3% of peak) and comparison with moment-0 maps of individual low-energy transitions in Appendix D [Analysis of the maps: "The task *imfit* produces also the maps of the residuals (i.e. the difference between the maps in input and the best 2D models computed by the task itself)"] [Positions of molecular species emission…: "This is due to the fact that the emission is nearly Gaussian, with the residual values contributing at maximum for a factor of 3% with respect to the peak emission."]
- result: CH3CHO emission peak position (J2000) and size = 18h 47m 34.308s, -01d 12m 46.132s, 1.64'' x 1.60'' (PA 50 deg) ± not stated hms, dms, arcsec, deg [Vinyl cyanide: C$_2$H$_3$CN: "| CH$_3$CHO | $\sim10-70$ | 34.308 | 46.132 | $1.64\times1.60$ | 50 |"]
- result: CH3OCH3 emission peak position (J2000) and size = 18h 47m 34.319s, -01d 12m 46.052s, 1.98'' x 1.83'' (PA 22 deg) ± not stated hms, dms, arcsec, deg [Vinyl cyanide: C$_2$H$_3$CN: "| CH$_3$OCH$_3$ | $\sim10-220$ | 34.319 | 46.052 | $1.98\times1.83$ | 22 |"]
- result: C2H5CN emission peak position (J2000) and size = 18h 47m 34.311s, -01d 12m 46.094s, 1.95'' x 1.59'' (PA 20 deg) ± not stated hms, dms, arcsec, deg [Vinyl cyanide: C$_2$H$_3$CN: "| C$_2$H$_5$CN | $\sim20-120$ | 34.311 | 46.094 | $1.95\times1.59$ | 20 |"]
- limitation (authors): The 1.2'' angular resolution is insufficient to resolve the four embedded compact cores (A, B, C, D) or conclusively establish spatial segregation between molecular species whose peak separations are smaller than the beam. [Positions of molecular species emission…: "The distances between the center of emission of the different species are smaller than the beam (1.2$''$), therefore the spatial resolution of the observations is not sufficient to have conclusive results about the possible presence of spatial segregation for some classes of molecular species, or for selected species."]
- limitation (authors): A single-temperature LTE model cannot fully capture the temperature gradient in G31, causing a small number of transitions (e.g. in CH3COCH3 and CH3OH v=0) to be poorly reproduced. [Acetone: CH$_3$COCH$_3$: "A small number of transitions of CH$_3$COCH$_3$ are overestimated by the best-fit model. A better agreement over the whole spectra would require a more accurate description of the temperature gradient of the source G31"]
- limitation (authors): For species whose unblended transitions only span low upper-state energies (E_U < 100 K, such as CH3CHO, aGg'-(CH2OH)2, 13CH3CN, CH313CN, C2H3CN, C2H5CN), the fitted excitation temperatures may be underestimated. [Spectral analysis: "some of the molecular species presented in this work do not have unblended transitions with $E_{\rm{U}}>100\,\rm{K}$, leading to possibly underestimated excitation temperatures."]
- not stated (card): CASA version used for calibration, imaging, immath, and imfit; MADCUBA version used for SLIM/AUTOFIT analysis; Exact coordinates of the continuum peak from which the 1.2'' beam spectrum was extracted; Velocity integration ranges used to compute the moment-0 maps of each selected transition; Details of calibration and imaging (e.g. self-calibration, cell size, image size, masking) deferred to Mini…

- processing.imaging.line_free_channels: Continuum from line-free channels after inspecting line widths; for high-velocity maps >750 (400) km/s from Vsys for CO (CN) [3.3.1: "Continuum was determined more than 750 (400) km s$^{-1}$ away from $V_{\rm sys}$ for the CO (CN) lines"]

  - validation: Recovered 76-87% of single-dish CO(1-0) flux (Cycle 0), 91-97% (CSV+Cycle 0) [2.1 ALMA: "We recovered 91–97% of the single-dish CO(1–0) flux in the cubes made from the CSV$+$Cycle 0 data."]
- limitation (authors): Mass estimates depend on uncertain X_CO; may be an order of magnitude lower if optically thin [7 Discussion and Conclusions: "Because most outflow parameters in Table 8 depend on $X_{20}$, followup studies on the physical and chemical properties of the high-velocity gas are highly desired."]

### 2018A&A...609A.101S: STATCONT: A statistical continuum level determination method for line-rich sources (2018; astrochemistry, star_formation, instrumentation; technical_calibration_or_instrument)
- question: How can the continuum level of line-rich spectra and datacubes be determined automatically and accurately with a statistical method?
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: Classical approach: line-free channels identified in average spectrum around position A; compared to c-SCM continuum [3 Application to real datasets: "are produced after identifying line-free channels in an average spectrum extracted around position A"]
- step 2: Compare continuum estimators
  - method: Maximum, mean, median, percentiles, Gaussian, KDE maximum and sigma-clipping applied to intensity distributions and scored by discrepancy from 50 K [2.2 Statistical comparison of continuum…: "We determine the continuum level for all the spectra using all the methods described in Section"]
  - software: STATCONT
  - parameter: Histogram bin size set by rms noise; KDE Gaussian kernel with Silverman's rule [2.1 Continuum level in spectral data: s…: "In `STATCONT` the size of the bins is defined by the rms noise level of the observations"]
  - validation: Success rate vs discrepancy from known continuum [Statistical comparison of continuum met…: "we list the success rate determined as the number (in percentage) of synthetic spectra for which the continuum level has been determined with a certain level of accuracy"]
- step 3: Correct sigma-clipping (c-SCM)
  - method: Add or subtract sigma_SCM (+1, +0.5, 0, -0.5, -1) based on fractions of emission/absorption channels relative to oSCL±rms (33% and 25% thresholds) [2.4 Corrected sigma-clipping continuum …: "the continuum emission level is corrected by adding or subtracting the value of the uncertainty to the continuum level"]
  - software: STATCONT
  - validation: Tested on 750 synthetic spectra [Statistical comparison of continuum met…: "The final statistically significant sample consists of 750 different synthetic spectra."]
- step 4: Apply to ALMA cubes
  - method: Pixel-by-pixel c-SCM to produce continuum, noise and line cubes; compare with classical line-free-channel continuum [3 Application to real datasets: "We apply the c-SCM in `STATCONT` to determine the continuum level in real observational data."]
  - software: STATCONT
  - validation: Compared with classical continuum maps [Application to real datasets: "In the top-right panel, we compare with the continuum map that is created after searching for line-free channels following the classical approach."]
- result: c-SCM success rate (discrepancy <5%) = 87.9 ± not stated percent [Table 2: "*(L)* c-SCM | 50.19 | 50.22 | $\phantom{0}$8.2 | 48.8 | 87.9"]
- result: c-SCM success rate (discrepancy <1%) = 48.8 ± not stated percent [Table 2: "*(L)* c-SCM | 50.19 | 50.22 | $\phantom{0}$8.2 | 48.8 | 87.9"]
- result: SCM success rate (discrepancy <5%) = 77.1 ± not stated percent [Table 2: "*(K)* SCM | 50.60 | 50.49 | $\phantom{0}$8.4 | 33.8 | 77.1"]
- limitation (authors): c-SCM assumes a flat continuum and can fail with <10% line-free channels for complex spectra [Summary: "A current limitation of `STATCONT` is that it assumes that the continuum level, or baseline of the spectrum, is flat"]
- not stated (card): Sigma-clipping alpha value, tolerance and iteration limit; Calibration and imaging parameters of the ALMA cubes (beam, rms, CASA version); Line-free channel ranges used for the classical maps; Random seeds and XCLASS parameters for the synthetic spectra; Positions of A and B in the real data

### 2013ApJ...770...13W: Redshift 6.4 Host Galaxies of 10<SUP>8</SUP> Solar Mass Black Holes: Low Star Formation Rate and Dynamical Mass (2013; high_z_galaxies, agn_and_quasars; observational_alma_primary)

### 2026A&A...709A..93D: Tentative detection of the glycine isomer glycolamide in a hot molecular core (2026; astrochemistry, star_formation; archival_reuse)
- question: Whether the glycine isomer glycolamide is present in the hot molecular core G358.93-0.03 MM1 and how its abundance compares to chemically related amides (formamide and acetamide) across environments.

  - software: CASA, findcont, uvcontsub

  - parameter: MT-MFS with two Taylor terms; multiscale scales = 0, 5, 15, 50, 150 pixels (continuum) and 0, 5, 15, 50 pixels (line) [Data Reduction: "Each iteration employed a multi-term multi-frequency synthesize (MT-MFS) deconvolver with two Taylor terms and multiscale cleaning scales of 0, 5, 15, 50, and 150 pixels."] [Data Reduction: "continuum-subtracted visibilities were imaged with the multiscale deconvolver, using the same weighting scheme and scales (0, 5, 15, and 50 pixels)."]
  - parameter: Briggs weighting robust = 0.5 [Data Reduction: "Briggs weighting with a robust parameter of 0.5 was adopted throughout."]
  - fitting: First-order polynomial fit to line-free channels in uvcontsub [Data Reduction: "The continuum emission was then removed in the UV domain using the CASA task uvcontsub, where the line-free channels were fitted with a first-order polynomial."]
  - validation: Line-cube sensitivity (~0.5 K per 0.5 km s^-1 channel) checked against expected thermal noise; continuum subtraction compared with STATCONT [Data Reduction: "The final line-cube sensitivity achieved was $\sim$0.5 K per 0.5 km s$^{-1}$ channel, consistent with the expected thermal noise after self-calibration."] [Molecular line identifications: "We also assessed the robustness of the continuum subtraction by comparing our CASA-based procedure with STATCONT (cite: Sanchez18)"]

  - method: Extract spectra from slightly offset position to reduce Doppler blending; construct a full source LTE model of all detected species in GILDAS CLASS/Weeds; optimize T_ex and N_t for GA, FA, NH2 13CHO, and AA using CLASS/ADJUST with SciPy Po… [Spectroscopic inputs and local thermody…: "The observed spectra were modeled under the local thermodynamical equilibrium (LTE) assumption using CLASS/Weeds (cite: Mar11) sets of Grenoble Image and Line Data Analysis Software (GILDAS)"] [Spectroscopic inputs and local thermody…: "To refine the LTE parameters, we optimized the initial CLASS/Weeds model by CLASS/ADJUST using the Powell algorithm (cite: Powell64) implemented in the SciPy optimization library."]
  - software: GILDAS, CLASS/Weeds, CLASS/ADJUST, SciPy, STATCONT
  - parameter: V_LSR fixed to -16.5 km s^-1; source size theta fixed to deconvolved continuum size [Spectroscopic inputs and local thermody…: "$\theta$ was set to the deconvolved continuum sizes, $\Delta V$ to the average of Gaussian fits for bright unblended lines, and $V_{\rm LSR}$ to -16.5 km s$^{-1}$ for G358.93 MM1 (cite: 2019ApJ...881L..39B)."]
  - parameter: Line widths fixed to 1.4 km s^-1 (GA), 2.8 km s^-1 (FA), 2.0 km s^-1 (NH2 13CHO), and 2.1 km s^-1 (AA) [Molecular line identifications: "Under the LTE assumption, the best-fit model yields a column density of $N_{\rm t}$(GA) = (5.3$\pm$1.1)$\times$10$^{14} \rm cm^{-2}$ and an excitation temperature of $T_{\rm ex}$(GA) = 102$\pm$20 K, with the linewidth of GA set to 1.4 km s$^{-1}$."] [Molecular line identifications: "In the analysis, the line widths were fixed to the average values of 2.8 km s$^{-1}$ for the FA lines and 2.0 km s$^{-1}$ for the NH$_{2}$$^{13}$CHO lines to derive the column densities and excitation temperatures."]
  - assumption: Local thermodynamic equilibrium (LTE) [Spectroscopic inputs and local thermody…: "The observed spectra were modeled under the local thermodynamical equilibrium (LTE) assumption using CLASS/Weeds (cite: Mar11) sets of Grenoble Image and Line Data Analysis Software (GILDAS)"]
  - assumption: 12C/13C = 28 +/- 5 to scale NH2 13CHO to total NH2CHO column density [Molecular line identifications: "the optically thin $^{13}$C isotopologue was used to infer the total FA column density $N_{\rm t}$(FA), adopting a scaling factor of $^{12}$C/$^{13}$C = 28$\pm$5 (cite: 2023A&A...670A..98Y)."]
  - assumption: Conservative 20% uncertainty on T_ex and N_t [Spectroscopic inputs and local thermody…: "we adopted a conservative uncertainty of 20% for the derived $T_{\rm ex}$ and $N_{\rm t}$ values."]
  - fitting: Powell optimization algorithm in CLASS/ADJUST (SciPy) with T_ex and N_t as free parameters; manual adjustment for background species in full source model [Spectroscopic inputs and local thermody…: "To refine the LTE parameters, we optimized the initial CLASS/Weeds model by CLASS/ADJUST using the Powell algorithm (cite: Powell64) implemented in the SciPy optimization library."] [Molecular line identifications: "For species other than GA, AA, and FA, these parameters were adjusted manually to best reproduce the observed spectrum."]
  - validation: Rotational diagram analysis on 7 unblended GA transitions and repeat LTE modeling on STATCONT-subtracted spectra [Molecular line identifications: "As an independent consistency check on the best-fit LTE solution, we performed a rotational-diagram analysis based on seven unblended transitions of GA (Appendix [8](#app:C))."] [STATCONT-based continuum subtraction an…: "Using the STATCONT-processed data, we re-identified the candidate GA transitions and performed LTE modeling following the same methodology described in the main text."]

- result: Column density of syn-glycolamide (GA) = 5.3e14 ± +-1.1e14 cm^-2 [Table 1: "| $syn$-HOCH$_{2}$C(O)NH$_{2}$ | 102$\pm$20 | 5.3$\pm$1.1 | 0.7$\pm$0.2 |"]

- result: Excitation temperature of GA from STATCONT-based analysis = 98 ± +-20 K [STATCONT-based continuum subtraction an…: "the derived column density and excitation temperature—$N = (3.7\pm0.7)\times10^{14}$ cm$^{-2}$ and $T_{\rm ex} = 98\pm20$ K—are consistent with those obtained from the CASA-based analysis within the adopted uncertainties."]

### 2016ApJ...829....6W: ALMA Observations of HD 141569’s Circumstellar Disk (2016; debris_disks, protoplanetary_disks; observational_alma_primary)

  - validation: Consistent with integrating all channels within 10% [3.2 Gas Disk: "This value is consistent with integrating over all channels of the CO map to within $10\%$."]
