### 2021MNRAS.504.2687N: Morpho-kinematics of the circumstellar envelope of the AGB star R Dor: a global view (2021; evolved_stars, astrochemistry; archival_reuse)
- question: Characterise the global morpho-kinematics (rotation, expansion, turbulence, absorption, south-eastern blue-shifted stream, and broad outflows) of the circumstellar envelope of the oxygen-rich AGB star R Dor from the stellar surface up to ~…
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: Processed without continuum subtraction (both 2017.1.00191.S and re-reduced 2013.1.00166.S) [Observations and data reduction: "The line data were processed without continuum subtraction."] [Observations and data reduction: "As the $uv$ coverage for short baselines is poor, we reduced these data anew, using GILDAS with natural weighting and without continuum subtraction."]
- processing.imaging.line_free_channels: Two sets of 9 frequency channels each (7.7 km s^-1) at the beginning and end of the frequency band used to measure continuum visibilities for 29SiO [Absorption of the 29SiO line emission b…: "Turning now to the actual $^{29}$SiO observations, we select the two frequency intervals covering $V_z$=$\pm$4 km s$^{-1}$ and define a third interval, meant to measure continuum emission, made of two sets of 9 frequency channels each (7.7 km s$^{-1}$) at the beginning and end of the frequency band."]
- step 1: Calibrate and image high-resolution 29SiO and SO2 data and re-image archival 28SiO, CO, and HCN data
  - method: Average visibilities over 20 s intervals, select 81 channels (69 km s^-1), calibrate with ALMA scripts, and deconvolve/clean with natural weighting and no continuum subtraction in both CASA and GILDAS (using GILDAS as default). Re-reduce 2… [Observations and data reduction: "Visibilities are averaged over 20 s wide time intervals."] [Observations and data reduction: "Deconvolution and cleaning were done with natural weighting using two different codes: CASA and GILDAS."]
  - software: CASA, GILDAS
  - parameter: time averaging = 20 s; 81 channels = 69 km s^-1 (0.854 km s^-1 bin); pixel size = 5x5 mas^2; natural weighting; no continuum subtraction [Observations and data reduction: "We select a frequency interval of 81 channels corresponding to a Doppler velocity interval of 69 km s$^{-1}$, each Doppler velocity bin being 0.854 km s$^{-1}$ wide."] [Observations and data reduction: "In both cases we use pixels of 5$\times$5 mas$^2$."]
  - assumption: Systemic velocity of R Dor is 7 km s^-1 [Observations and data reduction: "The systemic velocity of R Dor (7 km s$^{-1}$) is taken as origin."]
  - validation: Compared CASA and GILDAS imaging results and checked visibility distributions of 28SiO I vs II to restrict analysis to r <= 60 au. [Observations and data reduction: "In what follows we use the GILDAS results as default but we checked their consistency with the CASA results whenever possibly relevant."] [Observations and data reduction: "Accordingly, we restrict the analysis to distances from the star not exceeding 60 au (1 arcsec)."]
- limitation (authors): LTE assumption and morpho-kinematic models are crude and oversimplified (e.g., modelling shock/pulsation broadening as Gaussian fluctuations and assuming simple power-law radial profiles). [Absorption over the stellar disc: "While local thermal equilibrium (LTE) is not expected to provide a reliable description of the CSE, in particular in the close neighbourhood of the star, its prediction provides a useful reference with which to compare observations."]
- limitation (authors): Poor uv coverage at short baselines for the 2013.1.00166.S (28SiO, CO, HCN) data limits reliable analysis to r <= 60 au (1 arcsec) and prevents quantitative statements at larger radii. [Observations and data reduction: "Accordingly, we restrict the analysis to distances from the star not exceeding 60 au (1 arcsec)."]
- limitation (authors): The 3D inclination angle of the blue stream with the line of sight and the inclination of the rotation axis with the plane of the sky are unknown. [The blue stream: "The angle made by the stream with the line of sight is unknown."]
- not stated (card): Exact versions of CASA and GILDAS used for calibration, imaging, and simulation; Specific cleaning thresholds, loop gain, number of iterations, and masking strategy in CASA and GILDAS; Image sizes (number of pixels) and pixel sizes for the 28SiO, CO, HCN, and SO cubes; Whether primary beam correction was applied to the cubes; Optimization algorithm / fitting routine used for the custom morpho-kin…

### 2024ApJ...966L..13D: Detection of Rydberg Lines from the Atmosphere of Betelgeuse (2024; evolved_stars, stellar_physics; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Are the emission lines near 232 GHz toward Betelgeuse Rydberg transition lines (H30alpha and a combined X30alpha of heavier elements), and what do they reveal about the extended atmosphere?
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: Spectral cubes made before and after continuum subtraction [Observations: "we made spectral image cubes before and after continuum subtraction"]
- processing.imaging.line_free_channels: Line-free channels used for the continuum image (channel ranges not given) [Observations: "the line-free channels used to made a continuum image"]
- step 1: Calibrate and self-calibrate, make continuum image
  - method: Concatenate pipeline-calibrated measurement sets, image line-free channels as continuum, use it as the model for phase self-calibration, then solve for the stellar spectral index in the final continuum image [Observations: "were concatenated, and the line-free channels used to made a continuum image to provide a model for phase self-calibration in"] [Observations: "The final continuum image was made solving for the stellar spectral index"]
  - software: CASA
  - parameter: Phase calibrator J0552+0313, cycled every 80-90 seconds; bandpass calibrator J0510+1800 [Observations: "cycling to the phase calibrator J0552+0313"] [Observations: "The bandpass was calibrated using J0510+1800."]
- step 2: Make spectral cubes
  - method: Apply the self-calibration to all spectral channels and image cubes before and after continuum subtraction [Observations: "The calibration was applied to all spectral channels and we made spectral image cubes before and after continuum subtraction."]
  - software: CASA
  - parameter: Cube noise 0.9 mJy/beam per channel; beam (28.8x20.6) mas at PA 50 deg [Observations: "the noise level per spectral channel is $\sigma_{\mathrm {rms}} =0.9$ mJy beam$^{-1}$"]
- step 4: Extract and fit line spectra
  - method: Extract continuum-subtracted integrated spectra in R65 (and central R11) in the stellar rest frame and fit Gaussian and Voigt profiles [Results: "shows the continuum-subtracted integrated spectrum taken within"] [Results: "were better fitted by Voigt rather than Gaussian profiles."]
  - parameter: Stellar rest frame with V* = V_helio = 20.9 km/s (V_lsr 4.9 km/s) [Table 1: "Fitted centre frequency in target frame adjusted to"]
  - assumption: Line identification by Rydberg formula rest frequencies; TiO2 excluded using relative CDMS/JPL intensities [Results: "we conclude that there is no evidence of significant contribution from this species"]
  - fitting: Voigt profiles (Gaussian + Lorentzian components) for H30alpha and X30alpha; sampler, priors and likelihood not stated [Table 1: "Combined FWHM, fitted using a Voigt profile"]
  - validation: Voigt fit compared against Gaussian fit in the integrated spectra [Results: "were better fitted by Voigt rather than Gaussian profiles."]
- result: H30alpha measured frequency = 231.9055 ± 0.0004 (formal fit error) GHz [Table 1: "231.9055"]
- result: X30alpha measured frequency = 232.0255 ± 0.0002 (formal fit error) GHz [Table 1: "232.0255"]
- result: H30alpha Voigt FWHM (Gaussian, Lorentzian components in brackets) = 42.6 (31.0, 19.4) ± not stated km/s [Table 1: "42.6 (31.0,19.4)"]
- result: X30alpha Voigt FWHM (Gaussian, Lorentzian components in brackets) = 21.5 (15.3, 10.2) ± not stated km/s [Table 1: "21.5 (15.3,10.2)"]
- result: H30alpha peak line flux (130 mas diameter aperture) = 50.6 ± not stated mJy [Table 1: "50.6"]
- result: X30alpha peak line flux (130 mas diameter aperture) = 80.8 ± not stated mJy [Table 1: "80.8"]
- result: H30alpha total intensity = 4.5e-19 ± not stated W m^-2 [Table 1: "$4.5\times10^{-19}$"]
- result: X30alpha total intensity = 5.7e-19 ± not stated W m^-2 [Table 1: "5.7$\times10^{-19}$"]
- result: He30alpha peak line flux upper limit = <5 ± not stated mJy [Table 1: "$<$5"]
- result: Observed blueshift of H30alpha and X30alpha peaks relative to V* = -5.9 (H30alpha), -3.0 (X30alpha) ± not stated km/s [Results: "appear to be blue-shifted relative to $V_{\star}$, by $-5.9$ and $-3.0$"]
- limitation (authors): Model ignores non-LTE level populations, so computed line fluxes are not over-interpreted [Models and Discussion: "We choose not to over-interpret the computed line fluxes because we have ignored non-LTE level population effects that are likely to change opacities and source functions and alter the distribution of the emission."]
- limitation (authors): Model is a mean 1D lukewarm chromosphere, without hot small-filling-factor plasma or cold molecular pockets [Models and Discussion: "This model represents the mean 1D dominant lukewarm chromosphere and not the small filling factor of hot chromospheric plasma that gives rise to the rich UV spectrum."]
- limitation (authors): H30alpha emission in the model requires an arbitrarily added hydrogen ionization fraction [Models and Discussion: "In the simulation we arbitrarily added a small H ionization fraction in the model to create the emission shown in the blue spectrum."]
- not stated (card): CASA version used for calibration, self-calibration and imaging; Pipeline version and whether the archive pipeline products were used unchanged; tclean parameters: weighting and robust value, deconvolver, cell and image size, masks, number of iterations, threshold; Line-free channel ranges used for the continuum and for continuum subtraction (and the method of subtraction); Self-calibration solut…

### 2019MNRAS.485..229R: Deep and narrow CO absorption revealing molecular clouds in the Hydra-A brightest cluster galaxy (2019; galaxy_clusters_and_sz, agn_and_quasars, nearby_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Are there cold molecular gas clouds along the line of sight to the Hydra-A mm-continuum core, and what are their kinematics, temperature and column densities (as a test of chaotic cold accretion)?
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: Performed after delivery; details not given [3 Observations: "we performed the necessary continuum subtractions and made channel maps at velocity resolutions to suit our needs."]
- step 2: Molecular gas mass of disc
  - method: Bolatto2013 relation; Monte Carlo of 100000 noise-perturbed spectra, double-Gaussian chi2 fit to get SCOdv [5 Molecular Gas Mass of the Disc: "100 000 simulated spectra are created based upon the observed spectrum."]
  - parameter: F21 = 3.2, F10 = 1; rms from line-free range outside -600 to +600 km/s [5 Molecular Gas Mass of the Disc: "For CO(1-0), $F_{10}=1$ and for CO(2-1) we use a value of $F_{21}=3.2$."]
  - assumption: X_CO = 2e20 cm-2 (K km/s)-1; D_L = 242.4 Mpc [5 Molecular Gas Mass of the Disc: "We use a standard value of $X_{\text{CO}} = 2 \times 10^{20}$ cm$^{-2}$ (K km s$^{-1}$)$^{-1}$"]
  - fitting: chi2 minimisation of double Gaussian; 1sigma from 15.865 and 84.135 percentiles [5 Molecular Gas Mass of the Disc: "for each of the 100 000 spectra produced such a fit is found using $\chi^{2}$ minimisation."]
  - validation: Compared with IRAM 30m mass of Hamer2013 [5 Molecular Gas Mass of the Disc: "the masses we find are consistent with the value of $M_{\text{mol}}=2.26 \pm 0.29\times 10^{9}$"]
- result: Molecular mass from CO(1-0) = 1.06e9 ± +0.02/-0.02 (e9) Msun [5 Molecular Gas Mass of the Disc: "we find an estimated mass of $M_{\text{mol}}=1.06^{+0.02}_{-0.02}\times 10^{9}$ $\text{M}_{\odot}$"]
- result: Molecular mass from CO(2-1) = 5.6e9 ± +0.1/-0.1 (e9) Msun [5 Molecular Gas Mass of the Disc: "we estimate a mass of $M_{\text{mol}}=5.6^{+0.1}_{-0.1}\times 10^{9}$ $M_{\odot}$"]
- limitation (authors): Mass may differ by up to an order of magnitude due to X-factor and F_ul uncertainty [5 Molecular Gas Mass of the Disc: "the molecular mass may differ from the estimated values by as much as an order of magnitude"]
- limitation (authors): Filling factor of 1 gives lower limit on tau; velocities are only apparent line-of-sight motions [6 Cold, Clumpy Gas Clouds: "these are simply apparent motions along the line-of-sight"]
- not stated (card): tclean parameters (weighting, robust, cell, image size, masking); line-free channels for continuum subtraction; flux calibrator and flux scale uncertainty; whether pipeline or manual calibration; exact extraction regions (circle radii/positions) for spectra; continuum flux measurement method; partition function and Aul values used; pixel/beam used for moment maps and clipping

### 2021MNRAS.506.1978L: High-resolution ALMA and HST images of q<SUP>1</SUP> Eri: an asymmetric debris disc with an eccentric Jupiter (2021; debris_disks, exoplanets; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Characterise the asymmetric outer debris belt of q1 Eri at sub-arcsecond resolution using ALMA and HST observations, and consider its relationship to the planetary system architecture, including the known exo-Jupiter q1 Eri b.
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: CASA uvcontsub (fit order = 1) for CO J=3-2 spectral line analysis [CO J=3-2 Spectral Line Analysis: "To remove continuum emission from any $\rm{CO}$ signal, we used the CASA tool $\rm{uvcontsub}$ with a fit order of 1 external to the region defined by $f_{\rm{CO}} {\pm} \Delta f$, where $\Delta f$ was set to a velocity width ${\pm}35\,\rm{kms}^{-1}$, avoiding fitting to channels where $\rm{CO}$ could be present."]
- processing.imaging.line_free_channels: Channels external to f_CO +/- 35 km s^-1 [CO J=3-2 Spectral Line Analysis: "To remove continuum emission from any $\rm{CO}$ signal, we used the CASA tool $\rm{uvcontsub}$ with a fit order of 1 external to the region defined by $f_{\rm{CO}} {\pm} \Delta f$, where $\Delta f$ was set to a velocity width ${\pm}35\,\rm{kms}^{-1}$, avoiding fitting to channels where $\rm{CO}$ could be present."]
- step 5: Extract CO J=3-2 spectral line profile and derive upper limit on CO gas mass
  - method: Subtract continuum with uvcontsub, image barycentric cube within +/-200 km s^-1 with tclean (0 iterations), apply spectro-spatial Keplerian mask in clockwise and anti-clockwise rotation directions between 38-125 au, compute 3 sigma integra… [CO J=3-2 Spectral Line Analysis: "We produced a data cube in the barycentric reference frame excluding all channels further than $200\,\rm{km\,s^{-1}}$ away from the J=3-2 line in velocity space using the CASA tclean algorithm (with zero clean iterations, since there was no significant emission per beam present in any channel)."] [CO J=3-2 Spectral Line Analysis: "We integrated all emission in rotation directions between projected radii of ${\sim}$38-125 au from the star (i.e., within the broad region defined by the full-width half-maxima of the sub-mm emission), which are both shown as a function of radial velocity in Fig. 10."]
  - software: CASA, uvcontsub, tclean
  - parameter: uvcontsub fit order = 1, exclusion window = +/- 35 km s^-1; tclean iterations = 0; radial integration = 38-125 au; channel spacing Delta v = 0.423 km s^-1; effective bandwidth factor = 2.667 [CO J=3-2 Spectral Line Analysis: "To remove continuum emission from any $\rm{CO}$ signal, we used the CASA tool $\rm{uvcontsub}$ with a fit order of 1 external to the region defined by $f_{\rm{CO}} {\pm} \Delta f$, where $\Delta f$ was set to a velocity width ${\pm}35\,\rm{kms}^{-1}$, avoiding fitting to channels where $\rm{CO}$ could be present."] [CO J=3-2 Spectral Line Analysis: "The velocity channel spacing of the data is $\Delta v {=} 0.423\,\rm{kms}^{-1}$, however the effective spectral bandwidth is ${\sim}2.667$ larger than this, since adjacent *ALMA* channels are not fully independent from each other"]
  - parameter: NLTE kinetic temperatures = 10 K, 50 K, 250 K [CO Gas Mass: "For three temperatures $10\,\rm{K}$, $50\,\rm{K}$ and $250\,\rm{K}$ (covering a range of values appropriate for debris discs), together with the stellar flux (see Fig. 3) and the peak emission radius of ${\sim}81.6$ au, Fig. 12 shows the estimated $\rm{CO}$ gas mass as a function of the electron density in the disc."]
  - assumption: Stellar mass = 1.1 M_sun, radial velocity v_rad = 27.82 +/- 0.15 km s^-1, CO photodissociation time = 120 years [CO J=3-2 Spectral Line Analysis: "To do so, we applied a Keplerian mask to the data cube to shift channels in both of the possible disc rotation directions (clockwise and anti-clockwise) based on a stellar mass of 1.1 $M_{\ensuremath{\odot}}$ (cite: Marmier13)."] [CO Gas Mass: "From equation 2 of (cite: Matra17) we can use this upper limit $\rm{CO}$ mass alongside the stellar mass, luminosity, disc fractional luminosity, disc peak emission radius and belt width, with a CO photo-dissociation time of 120 years (cite: Visser09) to estimate an upper bound on the fraction of planetesimals compose…"]
  - validation: Tested both clockwise and anti-clockwise rotation directions and took the larger rms value (7.1 mJy). [CO J=3-2 Spectral Line Analysis: "We found the rms of the two line profiles (clockwise and anti-clockwise rotations) across all velocities, and measured $\rm{rms{=}7.0\,\rm{mJy}}$ in the clockwise-rotated direction, and $\rm{rms{=}7.1\,\rm{mJy}}$ in the anti-clockwise rotated direction. We define our upper bound based on the larger of these two rms va…"]
- result: CO J=3-2 integrated line flux 3 sigma upper limit = 24.0 ± not stated mJy km s^-1 [CO J=3-2 Spectral Line Analysis: "The $3\,\sigma$ limit on this flux is then found as $\delta F_{\rm{CO},3\,\sigma} {=} 2.667 \times \Delta v \times \rm{rms} {=} 24.0\,\rm{mJy\,kms^{-1}}$."]
- result: CO gas mass 3 sigma upper bound (M_CO) = <4e-6 ± not stated M_earth [Abstract: "We find no evidence for CO gas, but set an upper bound on the CO gas mass of $4{\times}10^{-6}$ M$_{\oplus}$ ($3\,\sigma$), consistent with cometary abundances in the Solar System."]
- result: Planetesimal CO+CO2 mass fraction upper bound = <95 ± not stated % [CO Gas Mass: "to estimate an upper bound on the fraction of planetesimals composed of $\rm{CO}+\rm{CO_{2}}$, which is $f_{\rm{CO+CO_{2}}}<95\%$."]
- limitation (authors): The modified blackbody parameters (lambda_0 and beta_0) for the inner warm component are poorly constrained because the sub-mm slope relies only on low-S/N Band 6 and Band 7 inner excesses. [Flux Density Distribution: "However, the parameters defining the blackbody modification are not well constrained (i.e., due to uncertainty on the stellar photosphere and given that the two Band 6 and Band 7 data points these model have only low signal-to-noise excesses) and thus there are a broad range of values for which $\lambda_0$ and $\beta_…"]
- limitation (authors): Lower sensitivity in the Band 6 image prevents an independent detection of the ~20% SW/NE flux asymmetry seen in Band 7. [Flux Profile Analysis: "Despite these Band 6 flux ratios and flux difference not showing evidence for an asymmetry, the lower S/N of this image means that an asymmetry could still be present at broadly the same ${\sim}20$ percent level seen in Band 7. Deeper observations of q$^1$ Eri in Band 6 would be required to determine this."]
- limitation (authors): The HST coronagraph occults emission within ~2.25'', limiting the ability to quantify the significance of the minor-axis scattered light asymmetry. [Scattered Light Analysis: "However, since emission within ${\sim}2.25\ensuremath{''}$ cannot be probed due to coronagraph obscuration, this limits our ability to estimate the significance of this minor axis asymmetry."]
- not stated (card): Exact antenna and baseline flags applied manually to the Band 6 dataset; Image pixel (cell) size, image dimensions, clean threshold, and masking strategy used in CASA tclean for Band 6 and Band 7 continuum and CO line cubes; Whether primary beam correction was applied to the continuum images and CO data cube prior to measuring fluxes and radial profiles; Exact prior distributions and bounds used …

### 2022MNRAS.514.2994G: Low levels of sulphur dioxide contamination of Venusian phosphine spectra (2022; solar_system, astrochemistry; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Could the 1.1 mm absorption features in Venus' atmosphere attributed to phosphine (PH3) be due to sulphur dioxide (SO2) absorption instead, and what is the level of SO2 contamination?
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: Image-plane polynomial fits subtracted (6th-order for narrowband PH3 1-0 data, 3rd-order for wideband SO2 data) [2.1 ALMA: "The second additional procedure involved subtracting image-plane fits, which were of 6 th -order for the narrowband data including PH 3 1-0, and of 3 rd -order for the simultaneous wideband data covering a strong SO 2 transition."] [Figure 1 caption: "From left to right: Venus continuum-subtracted cube using all antenna baselines (15-314m) and subtracting only a 1st-order image-plane fit; using only baselines of 33-314m and with a 1st-order image-plane fit; using only baselines of 33-314m and with a 6th-order image-plane fit."]
- step 1: Re-process ALMA datacubes and extract PH3 and SO2 spectra
  - method: Exclude baselines <33m, subtract image-plane polynomial fits (6th-order for narrowband PH3, 3rd-order for wideband SO2), extract spectra from a 97x37 pixel box (49% of disc), divide by similarly-processed continuum signal from the same are… [2.1 ALMA: "The second additional procedure involved subtracting image-plane fits, which were of 6 th -order for the narrowband data including PH 3 1-0, and of 3 rd -order for the simultaneous wideband data covering a strong SO 2 transition."] [2.1 ALMA: "Line:continuum ratios are finally obtained via a similarly-processed continuum signal extracted from the same area."]
  - parameter: baselines 33-314m; box size 97x37 pixels (0.16 arcsec/pixel, covering 49% of disc, latitudes <= 40 deg); 6th-order fit (narrowband) and 3rd-order fit (wideband); template shift +145 km/s [Figure 1 caption: "using only baselines of 33-314m and with a 6th-order image-plane fit."] [4.1 ALMA 2019 data: "The new extracted spectrum is from a planet-spanning RA,Dec “box”, of 97x37 pixels where 1 pixel = 0.16 arcseconds."]
  - assumption: Line and continuum sources are similarly spatially distributed; 22% of continuum survives spatial filtering [2.1 ALMA: "This overcomes the difficulties in appropriately calibrating models of Venus, but involves the assumption that line and continuum sources are similarly spatially distributed, as discussed below."] [5.2 Abundances: "This effect was modelled by Akins et al. (2021), while Greaves et al. (2021a) calculated from the data that 22% of the continuum survives the spatial filtering."]
  - fitting: Image-plane polynomial fits (6th-order and 3rd-order); line integration over +/-6 km/s for PH3 detection confidence [4.1 ALMA 2019 data: "This spectrum has 7.7 sigma confidence when line-integrated over ±6 km/s."]
  - validation: Tested extractions for PH3 in latitude bands extending to 20, 30, 40, or 50 degrees from the equator, finding fitted PH3 line-depths varied by only ~+/-10% (at the noise level). [4.1 ALMA 2019 data: "Test-extractions were made for PH 3 for bands of latitude (instead of Declination) that extended to 20, 30, 40 or 50 degrees from the equator. The fitted PH 3 line-depths varied by only ∼±10%, at the level of noise."]
- result: ALMA 2019 PH3 1-0 detection significance (integrated over +/-6 km/s) = 7.7 ± not stated sigma [4.1 ALMA 2019 data: "This spectrum has 7.7 sigma confidence when line-integrated over ±6 km/s."]
- result: ALMA 2019 PH3 1-0 line centroid velocity = -0.2 ± 0.5 km/s [4.1 ALMA 2019 data: "The line-centroid is at -0.2 ± 0.5 km/s in the Venus-centric PH 3 1-0 reference-frame, fully in agreement with zero as expected if the absorption is dominated by phosphine."]
- result: Contamination-subtracted PH3 1-0 line-to-continuum minimum (ALMA 2019) = -1.7e-4 ± not stated dimensionless [5.2 Abundances: "After subtracting contamination and adopting uniform ≈3.5 km/s spectral binning, the line-minimum for JCMT is -2.1e-4 and for ALMA it is -1.7e-4, i.e. a variation of ∼10% around the mean."]
- limitation (authors): Excluding short ALMA baselines (<33m) filters out spatial scales larger than 4.3-7.1 arcsec, heavily downweighting the centre of Venus (15.2 arcsec diameter) so that only ~22% of continuum survives and true ALMA line-depths and abundances … [2.1 ALMA: "Hence, as Venus then subtended 15.2 arcsec, most of the centre of the planet is heavily downweighted, with most of the line signal coming from an annulus inwards of the limb."]
- limitation (authors): Cloud-level PH3 abundance and altitude profiles cannot be quantified because broad pressure-broadened line wings are heavily suppressed by the polynomial baseline subtraction and lack laboratory PH3-CO2 broadening coefficients. [1 INTRODUCTION: "Addressing (3) will require new observations and better laboratory data, as inversion of line-shapes to altitude-profiles of abundance needs lower-noise spectra and a PH 3 -CO 2 broadening-coefficient that improves on current theoretical estimates."]
- limitation (authors): The 1D radiative transfer model neglects scattering, beam convolution, and limb darkening, generating SO2 lines 15-25% less deep than detailed 3D/scattering radiative transfer codes. [3 MODELLING: "Verification tests (against Lincowski et al. 2021, Figure 2 and Greaves et al. 2020, Figure 4) found that our simple code generates SO 2 lines that are 15-25% less deep than from more detailed models (see discussion in Appendix A)."]
- not stated (card): CASA version and specific imaging parameters (e.g., tclean weighting, robust parameter, masking, deconvolver) used in the Greaves et al. (2021a) scripts; Exact synthesized beam dimensions for the ALMA cubes; Exact height increment (Delta z) used in the 1D radiative transfer integration

### 2025PSJ.....6..286M: Zonal Winds in Titan's Middle Atmosphere from a Stellar Occultation Observed with Keck Adaptive Optics (2025; solar_system; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Which ALMA-derived or GCM zonal wind profiles reproduce the resolved occultation light distributions around Titan?
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: Visibility-domain 2nd order polynomial fit to line-free (pseudo-)continuum regions in each spectral window [ALMA observations and data reduction: "Prior to imaging, the Titan continuum was subtracted from the interferometric visibility data using a 2nd order polynomial fit to the line-free (pseudo-)continuum regions in each spectral window."]
- step 5: Determine ALMA altitude sensitivity
  - method: NEMESIS radiative transfer and CH3CN abundance retrieval; derive wind contribution from altitude functional derivatives weighted by the spectral-line gradient [ALMA wind retrieval: "we used the Non-linear optimal Estimator for MultivariatE spectral analySIS (NEMESIS) to generate a detailed radiative transfer model for the observed CH$_3$CN emission"] [ALMA wind retrieval: "This abundance profile formed the basis of our “wind contribution function” calculation, which was obtained from the corresponding NEMESIS functional derivatives ($dQ/dz$) of flux with respect to altitude, as a function of frequency."]
  - software: CASA, tclean, NEMESIS
  - parameter: Re-image calibrated cubes before continuum subtraction; extract disk-averaged spectra with 1.5'' diameter aperture [ALMA wind retrieval: "The calibrated ALMA data cubes were re-imaged prior to continuum subtraction"] [ALMA wind retrieval: "Disk-averaged spectra were then extracted using a circular aperture with a diameter of 1.5$''$"]
  - parameter: 35 annuli; emission angles 0–75 degrees; tangential altitude up to 1050 km [ALMA wind retrieval: "Titan’s disk (plus atmosphere) was divided into 35 annuli corresponding to emission angles between 0-75 degrees, covering the center of Titan up to a tangential altitude of 1050 km"]
  - parameter: Model vertical extent 1200 km; negligible emission above 1000 km [ALMA wind retrieval: "the vertical extent of our model was 1200 km, but emission is negligible above 1000 km"]
  - parameter: Scale observed data, including continuum, by 0.982 [ALMA wind retrieval: "The observed ALMA data (including the continuum) were scaled by a constant factor of 0.982 to produce a match between the modeled and observed continuum levels."]
  - parameter: Average and peak-normalize the weighted contribution functions [ALMA wind retrieval: "which were then averaged and peak-normalized."]
  - assumption: Atmospheric temperature profile from (cite: 2020ApJ...903L..22T) [ALMA wind retrieval: "The atmospheric temperature profile was obtained from (cite: 2020ApJ...903L..22T)."]
  - assumption: A priori CH3CN abundance profile from (cite: nosowitz_improved_2025) [ALMA wind retrieval: "updated to incorporate the more recent CH$_3$CN abundance profile from (cite: nosowitz_improved_2025) for the *a priori* atmospheric state."]
  - assumption: J=36-35 has the same altitude sensitivity range because line opacities and energy levels are similar [ALMA wind retrieval: "Due to the similarity of the line opacities and energy levels, this range is also applicable to the $J=36-35$ band."]
  - fitting: Continuously retrieve CH3CN abundance at each altitude against J=35-34 line profiles [ALMA wind retrieval: "The CH$_3$CN abundance profile was continuously retrieved at each altitude to obtain a fit to the observed $J=35-34$ spectral line profiles."]
  - validation: Continuum matching after the 0.982 scaling [ALMA wind retrieval: "The observed ALMA data (including the continuum) were scaled by a constant factor of 0.982 to produce a match between the modeled and observed continuum levels."]
- result: ALMA continuum scaling factor = 0.982 ± not stated dimensionless [ALMA wind retrieval: "The observed ALMA data (including the continuum) were scaled by a constant factor of 0.982 to produce a match between the modeled and observed continuum levels."]
- result: Average CH3CN emission altitude = 303 ± not stated km [ALMA wind retrieval: "The corresponding average emission altitude is 303 km, with sensitivity to winds primarily in the range $\sim150$-450 km."]
- result: Primary ALMA wind sensitivity altitude range = ~150-450 ± not stated km [ALMA wind retrieval: "The corresponding average emission altitude is 303 km, with sensitivity to winds primarily in the range $\sim150$-450 km."]
- limitation (authors): ALMA rubidium-maser cable-routing error likely introduced an unknown frequency shift and slight spectral broadening [ALMA wind retrieval: "an error in the cable routing of the ALMA rubidium maser at the time of our observations, which is likely to have introduced an erroneous and unknown frequency shift in our observations"]
- limitation (authors): ALMA winds are not vertically resolved; equatorial-jet contributions cannot be isolated [Comparison between GCM, occultation, an…: "As the ALMA wind profile is not vertically resolved, isolating the contribution of the equatorial jet from the contributions of winds at lower altitudes that contribute more heavily to the CH$_3$CN profile is not possible."]
- limitation (authors): GCM and ALMA forward-model inputs differ in vertical treatment [Comparison between GCM, occultation, an…: "the implementations of ALMA and GCM profiles into our forward model varied slightly due to GCM simulations being altitudinally resolved while ALMA retrieved wind speeds are averaged over an altitude contribution function"]

### 2017AJ....154..206L: Mapping Vinyl Cyanide and Other Nitriles in Titan's Atmosphere Using ALMA (2017; solar_system, astrochemistry; archival_reuse)
- question: Can C2H3CN be detected at submillimeter frequencies in Titan's atmosphere and spatially mapped, along with other nitriles, to constrain abundances and photochemistry/circulation?
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: uvcontsub [Observations: "the continuum was subtracted from the visibility amplitudes using the CASA `uvcontsub` task."]
- step 1: calibrate and image
  - method: JAO scripts, flux scaling to Butler-JPL-Horizons, uvcontsub, clean (Hogbom, natural) [Observations: "Imaging and deconvolution were performed using the `clean` task; the point-spread function was deconvolved using the Hogbom algorithm, with natural visibility weighting"]
  - software: CASA
  - parameter: threshold 18 mJy; pixel 0.1'' [Observations: "The image pixel size was set to $0.1''\times0.1''$"]
- step 3: retrieve vertical abundance profiles
  - method: NEMESIS line-by-line radiative transfer with step (300 km) models for C2H3CN/C2H5CN and fractional scale-height gradient models for HC3N/CH3CN; spectra scaled to model in line-free regions [Results: "The abundances and fractional scale-height parameters $f_H$ were optimized by minimizing the sum of squares of the spectroscopic residuals"]
  - software: NEMESIS
  - parameter: 35 impact parameters up to 1000 km; Lorentz width 0.075 cm-1/bar, alpha 0.5 (HC3N 0.1, 0.75) [Results: "across 35 impact parameters between the center of Titan’s disk and the top of the model atmosphere at 1000 km"]
  - assumption: temperature profile from HASI+CIRS feathered 60-100 km; CO abundance 5.06e-5 [Results: "by feathering the profiles together in the range 60-100 km using a linearly graded (weighted) average."]
  - fitting: least squares minimization of residuals [Results: "optimized by minimizing the sum of squares of the spectroscopic residuals"]
  - validation: reduced chi-squared; step heights >200 km acceptable [Results: "we find that models with a step height of $>$200 km provide a reasonable fit to our C$_2$H$_3$CN and C$_2$H$_5$CN observations"]
- result: C2H3CN abundance (300 km step) = 3.03 ± 0.29 ppb [Table 1: "| C$_2$H$_3$CN | 3.03 (0.29) |"]
- result: C2H5CN abundance (300 km step) = 7.37 ± 0.32 ppb [Table 1: "| C$_2$H$_5$CN | 7.37 (0.32) |"]
- result: C2H5CN/C2H3CN ratio = 2.43 ± 0.26 dimensionless [Discussion: "The C$_2$H$_5$CN/C$_2$H$_3$CN abundance ratio of $2.43\pm0.26$"]
- result: HC3N fractional scale-height = 2.05 ± 0.16 dimensionless [Table 1: "$2.05\pm0.16$ and $1.63\pm0.02$, respectively."]
- result: CH3CN fractional scale-height = 1.63 ± 0.02 dimensionless [Table 1: "$2.05\pm0.16$ and $1.63\pm0.02$, respectively."]
- result: C3H8 mixing ratio = 8.5e-7 ± 0.6e-7 mixing ratio [Results: "best-fitting C$_3$H$_8$ mixing ratio of $(8.5\pm0.6)\times10^{-7}$"]
- limitation (authors): C3H8 lines blended so abundance uncertain; south pole partly hidden [Results: "our fitted value should be treated with extreme caution due to the fact that the two propane lines are both blended"]
- not stated (card): Line-free channels used for continuum subtraction; Velocity integration ranges for moment maps; Contour factor n and per-map RMS values; Exact CIRS temperature profile data; Initial values and optimizer settings for NEMESIS fits; Number of iterations/clean mask; Spectral windows and calibrators other than flux scale

- question: What is the structure of the ultra-cold Boomerang Nebula's molecular outflow, how does it relate to the visible-light nebula, and what do the mm continuum data say about the dust in the waist?

- limitation (authors): 1.3 mm data S/N modest with sparse uv coverage; large fraction of CO(1-0) absorption resolved out [Thermal Dust Emission from the Waist: "although S/N is modest and 1.3 mm UV-coverage is still sparse"]

### 2025A&A...703A..15M: Discovery of carbon monoxide emission from five debris disks around young A-type stars (2025; debris_disks, protoplanetary_disks; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Expand the sample of known CO-bearing debris disks around young intermediate-mass stars and explore the environmental conditions and origin (primordial vs. secondary) of their gas.
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: UVCONTSUB with first-order polynomial fit to line-free regions in uv space [Observations and data reduction: "For the analysis of line emission in the FDM spectral windows, we used the UVCONTSUB routine to fit a first-order polynomial to the line-free regions (Sect. [5](#sec:co)) in the $uv$ space and subtracted the continuum from the obtained data."]
- processing.imaging.line_free_channels: All channels except those with detected line emission within +-30 km/s of stellar radial velocity [Imaging: "In this process, we used data from all of the spectral windows except for the channels where line emission was detected (Sect. [5](#sec:co))."] [Imaging and CO line fluxes: "To search for $^{12}$CO (2–1) emission, we examined channels that are within $\pm$30 km s$^{-1}$ of the radial velocity of the target star in the LSR frame."]
- step 1: Calibrate, concatenate, and continuum-subtract ALMA visibility data
  - method: Calibrate with standard ALMA pipeline, concatenate multi-epoch MSs with CONCAT, recalculate weights with STATWT, and subtract continuum in uv-space with UVCONTSUB using a 1st-order polynomial [Observations and data reduction: "For objects that were observed at more than one epoch (Table [1](#tab:obslog)), the resulting measurement sets were concatenated using the CONCAT task. To ensure the relative weights between the different data sets are correct we recalculated the data weights with the `CASA` STATWT task."] [Observations and data reduction: "For the analysis of line emission in the FDM spectral windows, we used the UVCONTSUB routine to fit a first-order polynomial to the line-free regions (Sect. [5](#sec:co)) in the $uv$ space and subtracted the continuum from the obtained data."]
  - software: CASA, CONCAT, STATWT, UVCONTSUB
  - parameter: 1st-order polynomial in UVCONTSUB [Observations and data reduction: "we used the UVCONTSUB routine to fit a first-order polynomial to the line-free regions (Sect. [5](#sec:co)) in the $uv$ space"]
  - fitting: First-order polynomial fit to line-free channels in uv space [Observations and data reduction: "we used the UVCONTSUB routine to fit a first-order polynomial to the line-free regions (Sect. [5](#sec:co)) in the $uv$ space"]
- step 2: Image 1.33 mm dust continuum and fit visibility models to derive disk geometry and flux
  - method: Image line-free channels with TCLEAN (multiscale) and fit visibilities using UVMULTIFIT with point source, elliptical Gaussian, single Gaussian ring, or double narrow ring models [Imaging: "To construct continuum images, we used the TCLEAN task with the *multiscale* deconvolution algorithm, setting a list of scale sizes of 0 (point source), 5, 15, and 25 pixels (corresponding roughly to 1, 3, and 5 times the beam size)."] [Modeling of the continuum emission: "To model the spatial structure of the dust emission and to estimate the flux density of the disks at 1.33 mm, we used the UVMULTIFIT tool developed by (cite: martividal2014) to fit the observed visibilities."]
  - software: CASA, TCLEAN, UVMULTIFIT
  - parameter: multiscale scales = [0, 5, 15, 25] pixels; Briggs robust = 0.5 (natural for HD 144277, HD 176497; 1 arcsec taper for non-detections) [Imaging: "setting a list of scale sizes of 0 (point source), 5, 15, and 25 pixels (corresponding roughly to 1, 3, and 5 times the beam size)."] [Imaging: "With the exception of HD 144277 and HD 176497, where natural weighting was used, all other images were compiled with Briggs weighting where the robust parameter was set to 0.5."]
  - parameter: W_disk = 0.7 R_disk fixed for HD 176497, HD 112532, HD 141960, HD 145101 [Modeling of the continuum emission: "In the modeling of the faint disk around HD 176497, this width parameter (the FWHM of the Gaussian) was fixed to be 0.7 of the ring radius (cite: matra2025)."] [Modeling of the continuum emission: "For the other three disks, we added a point source model to the profile types to be tested, and in the case of ring models the width parameter was always tied to the radius as $W_\mathrm{disk} = 0.7 R_\mathrm{disk}$."]
  - assumption: 10% absolute flux calibration uncertainty added in quadrature [Modeling of the continuum emission: "The quoted uncertainties of the flux densities are quadratic sums of the measurement errors (listed in brackets) and the absolute calibration error (which were conservatively assumed to be 10%)."]
  - fitting: Visibility fitting with UVMULTIFIT; model selection via Bayesian Information Criterion (BIC) [Modeling of the continuum emission: "To evaluate which of the best-fitting models obtained for the two different profile types has better quality, we used the Bayesian Information Criterion (BIC), which favored the Gaussian ring approach in all cases."]
  - validation: Comparison of models using Bayesian Information Criterion (BIC) [Modeling of the continuum emission: "To evaluate which of the best-fitting models obtained for the two different profile types has better quality, we used the Bayesian Information Criterion (BIC), which favored the Gaussian ring approach in all cases."]
- result: HD 9985 1.33 mm continuum flux density = 1.26 ± +-0.14 (0.06) mJy [Modeling of the continuum emission: "| HD 9985 | 12.6 | Gaussian ring | 1.26$\pm$0.14 (0.06) | 84$\pm$9 | 57$\pm$25 | 48$\pm$3 | 104$\pm$11 | 2.6$\pm$0.3e-01 | 1 |"]

### 2021ApJ...910...53V: A Massive, Clumpy Molecular Gas Distribution and Displaced AGN in Zw 3146 (2021; galaxy_clusters_and_sz, agn_and_quasars; observational_alma_primary)
- question: How is the molecular gas in the Zw 3146 BCG distributed relative to the X-ray cavities, and why are the molecular gas and the mm continuum (AGN) source offset?
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: uvcontsub (visibility-plane) [Observations and Data Reduction: "Continuum-subtracted data cubes were created using uvcontsub and clean."]
- step 1: Calibrate and image the CO(1-0) cube and continuum
  - method: CASA pipeline calibration, uvcontsub continuum subtraction, clean imaging with Briggs weighting [Observations and Data Reduction: "The observations were calibrated in casa version 5.4.0 (cite: casa) using the pipeline reduction scripts."] [Observations and Data Reduction: "Continuum-subtracted data cubes were created using uvcontsub and clean."]
  - software: CASA 5.4.0, uvcontsub, clean
  - parameter: robust = 0.5 [Observations and Data Reduction: "Images of the line emission were reconstructed using Briggs weighting with a robust parameter of 0.5."]
  - parameter: velocity binning to 20 km/s [Observations and Data Reduction: "and was binned to a velocity resolution of $20\hbox{$\rm\; km\rm\; s^{-1}\,$}$"]
  - validation: Phase self-calibration was attempted but did not improve S/N [Observations and Data Reduction: "Additional phase self-calibration was attempted, but the continuum source was too faint to provide an improvement in signal-to-noise."]
- step 6: Measure the 3 mm continuum and fit its SED
  - method: Image line-free channels at 96.193 GHz in a 40'' square, fit a 2D Gaussian, then fit a power law to the SED with lower-frequency literature fluxes [Millimetre Continuum: "An image of the continuum at $96.193\rm\; GHz$ ($3.1166\rm\; mm$) was created by imaging the line-free channels in a square region measuring $40''$ on each side."] [Millimetre Continuum: "A two-dimensional gaussian fit to the continuum flux yielded an accuracy for the centroid of $0.006$ arcseconds."]
  - parameter: Contours start at 3 sigma with sigma = 7.45 uJy/beam, steps of 4 sigma [Millimetre Continuum: "These contours begin at $3\sigma$, where $\sigma=7.45\hbox{$\rm\; \mu Jy\rm\; beam^{-1}\,$}$, and increase in $4\sigma$ steps."]
  - assumption: Power law S_nu proportional to nu^-alpha [Footnote 1: "Following the convention $S_{\nu} \propto \nu^{-\alpha}$"]
  - fitting: Power-law fit to the SED giving alpha = 0.685 +- 0.063 [Millimetre Continuum: "The spectral energy distribution (SED) is best fit by a power law with spectral index[^1] $\alpha = 0.685\pm0.063$, which is a typical value for an AGN (cite: Hogan15a)."]
- result: 3 mm continuum integrated flux density at 96.193 GHz = 133 ± 13 uJy [Millimetre Continuum: "A single, unresolved source was identified (R.A.: 10:23:39.5948, decl.: +4:11:11.032), which had a total integrated flux density of $133\pm13 \rm\; \mu Jy$."]
- result: Continuum centroid accuracy = 0.006 ± not stated arcsec [Millimetre Continuum: "A two-dimensional gaussian fit to the continuum flux yielded an accuracy for the centroid of $0.006$ arcseconds."]
- result: Continuum SED spectral index alpha = 0.685 ± 0.063 dimensionless [Millimetre Continuum: "The spectral energy distribution (SED) is best fit by a power law with spectral index[^1] $\alpha = 0.685\pm0.063$, which is a typical value for an AGN (cite: Hogan15a)."]
- limitation (authors): ALMA misses large-scale flux (about half of earlier CO flux, scales around 5 arcsec) [Molecular Gas Mass: "This suggests that our ALMA observations are missing large-scale flux, predominantly on scales of $\sim5''$ ($22\rm\; kpc$)."]
- limitation (authors): True gas morphology cannot be determined without higher resolution data [Comparison to other BCGs: "Without additional higher resolution observations of Zw 3146 we cannot conclusively determine its true molecular gas morphology."]
- limitation (authors): A molecular disk cannot be ruled out [Comparison to other BCGs: "Nevertheless, we are unable to rule out the presence of a molecular disk."]
- not stated (card): Flux, bandpass and phase calibrator names and the per-execution-block configurations (not given); Pipeline version (only CASA 5.4.0 is stated); Number of spectral windows and exact rest-frame frequency or velocity axis used for CO(1-0); uvcontsub line-free channel ranges and fit order; clean cell size, image size, niter, threshold, masking and deconvolver for the line cube; Whether the cube was p…

### 2015A&A...576A.129B: Antifreeze in the hot core of Orion. First detection of ethylene glycol in <ASTROBJ>Orion-KL</ASTROBJ> (2015; astrochemistry, star_formation; archival_reuse)
- question: Is ethylene glycol (aGg' and gGg' conformers) detectable in Orion-KL hot core, and how do its abundance relative to methanol, ethanol and glycolaldehyde compare with Sgr B2 and comets?

  - method: Continuum image from continuum bands plus line-free channels of the main SPW; line cube from the line SPW after continuum subtraction; Hogbom deconvolution with Briggs weighting [2 Observations: "The continuum image was obtained using the continuum bands plus the channels of the main spectral window where the line is not present."] [2 Observations: "The convolution method used the Hogbom algorithm with Briggs weighting."]
  - software: CASA 5.4.0
  - parameter: Cell size 0.1 mas, image 2048 x 2048 pixels [2 Observations: "the images have a cell size of 0.1 mas and a total size of"]
  - parameter: Cube velocity range -1187 to 1235 km/s, 1.262 km/s resolution [2 Observations: "In velocity, the cube range goes from $-1187$ to 1235 km s$^{-1}$"]
- limitation (authors): Lower flux than in 2012 may be due to emission outside the maximum recoverable scale [3.1 Continuum emission: "the lower flux density obtained in the present observations can be due to the existence of emission outside the maximum recovery scale of the ALMA configuration."]
- limitation (authors): Unclear whether the narrow line in the compact-source spectrum belongs to the compact source [3.2 The H30alpha spectrum: "It is not clear if the narrow line belongs to the compact source or if it only represents the contribution of the superimposed extended NW region."]
- limitation (authors): Blob proper motions depend on identification of Weigelt blobs with ALMA sources [4.5 The Weigelt blobs: "Of course, this result depends on the accuracy of the identification of the Weigelt blobs with the compact sources detected by ALMA"]
- not stated (card): Briggs robust value and CASA task/parameters (niter, threshold, masks, gridder, weighting scheme details) used for tclean-like imaging; Which channels were treated as line-free for the continuum image and the order of the continuum subtraction fit; Whether the archive pipeline products or a manual calibration script were used, and flux-scale uncertainty; Self-calibration, if any, and its solution…

### 2018MNRAS.481.4931E: ALMA reveals the aftermath of a white dwarf-brown dwarf merger in CK Vulpeculae (2018; stellar_physics, evolved_stars, astrochemistry; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What are the dust and molecular gas structures in the inner region of CK Vul, and what do they imply about the origin of the 1670 event?
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: uvcontsub [Observations: "These channel ranges were then used to remove the continuum data in the $uv$ plane, using uvcontsub."]
- processing.imaging.line_free_channels: SPW0 25-118; SPW1 50-75, 90-118; SPW2 all; SPW3 9-35, 95-119 (continuum channels) [Observations: "SPW0 25 to 118; SPW1 50 to 75 and 90 to 118; SPW2 all; and SPW3 9 to 35 and 95 to 119."]
- step 2: Image continuum and cubes
  - method: tclean on continuum channels; uvcontsub then spectral cubes [Observations: "The continuum–subtracted data in each spectral window were then imaged in spectral cube mode to allow examination of the emission."]
  - software: CASA
  - parameter: robust=2 [Observations: "Briggs weighting was applied with robust=2."]
- limitation (authors): Velocity resolution is modest and dynamical modelling is deferred. [Observations: "We defer modelling of the dynamics of these emission lines to a future, more detailed, paper"]
- not stated (card): Image size, cell size, masking, deconvolver, cleaning threshold; Spectral cube channel width and rest-frame/velocity settings; Whether tclean used the combined data and any primary beam correction; CASA and pipeline versions; Flux scale uncertainty; Integration ranges and moment/PV extraction details; CO column density value used for H2 mass

### 2014ApJ...790..160S: ALMA Observations of a Candidate Molecular Outflow in an Obscured Quasar (2014; agn_and_quasars, nearby_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What are the morphology, kinematics and mass of molecular gas in the obscured quasar SDSS J1356+1026, and is there a molecular outflow related to the known ionized outflow?
- processing.imaging.continuum_subtraction: CO(1-0): uv-plane subtraction; none for CO(3-2) [2.1 ALMA CO (1-0) and CO (3-2) Observat…: "Continuum subtraction was not applied to the CO (3-2) data cube as there are not enough line-free channels to determine the continuum level."]
- processing.imaging.line_free_channels: 36/43 channels on blue/red side (CO 1-0) [2.1 ALMA CO (1-0) and CO (3-2) Observat…: "line free channels (36/43 channels on the blue/red side)"]
- step 1: Measure 100 GHz continuum
  - method: Moment-0 of line-free channels in 93/103 GHz spectral windows of CO(1-0) data [3.1 100 GHz Continuum: "The radio 100 GHz continuum is measured from the line-free channels in two spectral windows at 93/103 GHz"]
  - software: CASA
- result: 100 GHz continuum flux density = 1.51 ± ±0.07 (rms) ±0.07 (calibration) mJy [3.1 100 GHz Continuum: "a flux density of $1.51 \pm 0.07 \pm 0.07$ mJy"]
- limitation (authors): Outflow quantities order-of-magnitude; XCO and line ratio uncertain [5.2.1 Properties of the Molecular Outfl…: "First, the $X_{\rm{CO}}$ factor in the context of an outflow is uncertain."]
- not stated (card): Exact clean parameters (cell size, image size, niter, masks, spectral-line channel ranges for CO 3-2); Line-free channel ranges for CO(3-2) 300 GHz continuum check; Aperture sizes/centres for flux extraction; Kinematic fitting details of 2D Gaussian per channel; Herschel photometry values (Petric et al. in prep.); Exact formulae for outflow energetics and Kennicutt SFR conversion; HST astrometric…
